> **STATUS: v2 DRAFT (2026-10-14), NOT binding.** Protocol v1 (V66, with Addenda G1 and Z1) stays binding until the author answers the new [AUTHOR] items A36–A68 and approves v2. v2 also loosens seven v1 bounds (Appendix D), which needs an explicit exception to V66. Made by a 20-agent workflow: 4 evidence agents, 2 drafts, 2 judges, synthesis, 5-lens review (46 findings, verified), revision. No project seed was used; the toy runs used non-project seeds only.

> **STATUS: v2 DRAFT (2026-10-13), revised 2026-10-14 after the v2 design review (Appendix E): pending author answers to the open [AUTHOR] items A36–A68 and approval; supersedes v1 before any project seed.** v1 (approved 2026-10-13, V66, with Addenda G1 and Z1) stays binding until the author approves v2 in a commit. **No project seed (7,400,000–7,419,999) has been used.** Every change from v1 is made before data and is listed in Appendix D.

# Protocol P0–P2 (v5.B.1): plant realism, metric informativeness, tool checks T0–T3, scenario band and early KILL

*Revision v2 DRAFT, 2026-10-13. It starts from the full text of v1 and folds Addenda G1 (V68 geometry) and Z1 (V71 zones, V72 CAV speed) into the body. It then implements the author decisions V70 (capacity-drop candidate), V74 (smoothness and safety as the claim), V75 (incident regime) and V76 (lane commands). It is the synthesis of two amendment drafts: "rigor", the base, which both judges named the winner, and "lean", whose best ideas are grafted in. The defects the judges found in both drafts are fixed (Appendix D lists every change, the decision behind it and the draft it came from). **Status: the new items A36–A54 (§11.2) and A55–A68 (§11.4) await the author's answers; the answers to A1–A35 (V58–V65) stand unless an item from A36 on re-opens them.** CLAUDE.md phase table: Extra High effort, ultracode on for the design review of v2. **Revision 2026-10-14:** the v2 design review (five lenses: metrics, statistics, SUMO mechanics, honesty, bidirectional) is applied in place. Its 46 findings, their verified severities and the change made for each are in Appendix E; the findings that need the author became A55–A68 or new options in A38, A41 and A44.*

*Provenance. No project seed was used to write it. The simulations behind v2 are toy runs on non-project seeds 1–7 on toy networks in the session scratchpad (`scratchpad/protocol_v2/`: `toy/`, `metrics_ev/`, `inc_toy/`, `rigor_toy/`; desk arithmetic in `synth/desk_synth.py`), the v1 toy runs (`scratchpad/protocol/toy/`, `scratchpad/protocol/review_phys/`), and the pre-P0 pilot (V69, V73: non-project seeds 1–5, **not data**). None of them ran on F3Z with a project seed. The `v5b1` worktree and the archive checkout were read, never modified. Decisions are cited as V# (`docs/decisions.md` on `v5b1`, V1–V76, the authority). Archive files are on branch `claude/v5b-core`, written ARCH/… (checkout `/srv/shared/Workspace/phd/phd_speed_harmo_v5`, read-only). The four evidence packages of the v2 workflow (metrics, SUMO mechanics, incident, lane rules) are cited as "evidence: metrics / mechanics / incident / lanes"; their read statuses are carried over as they stated them (Appendix A).*

**Labels used throughout**
- **[AUTHOR Ax]**: an open decision, listed in §11 with options, costs, a recommendation and a deadline. The primary design below uses the recommendation; nothing is decided silently.
- **[VERIFY]**: a source not read in full (abstract, secondary source or summarising fetch only), or a SUMO behaviour not yet confirmed by a local test.
- **ASSUMPTION**: a threshold with no published source; its rationale is given next to it, and it is listed in [AUTHOR A24].
- **[ADDENDUM X]**: a value fixed in a dated addendum *before* the data it governs (§9.4). An addendum can never relax a bound.
- **"my arithmetic"**: a number computed for this protocol, not taken from a source (v2: `scratchpad/protocol_v2/synth/desk_synth.py`).
- **FLAG**: recorded, shown in the report and used only by a named rule; never a gate unless a rule says so.
- **toy**: a local SUMO 1.27.1 run on a toy network and a non-project seed. Toy numbers inform method choices and the design of checks; they never set a PASS bound.
- **[DEV]**, **[PART]**, **[DOC]**: read statuses carried over from `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` (developer advice; a source read in part; documentation).

**Conventions.** SI units and euro (V20): m, s, km/h, veh/h/lane, m/s², kWh, €. "Inflow" means vehicles arriving at the network boundary (V9). **λ** is the inflow level: it scales all three streams together, and λ = 1.0 is the nominal peak of B13 (mainline 4,000, on-ramp 1 1,200, on-ramp 2 1,000 veh/h). **B0** = the all-HDV fleet with no commands (the D5.8 no-control baseline, V44). **B1** = the same vehicles with the CAV-flagged cars on plain ACC, no commands (V44). **R1–R4, O** are arms (`system_context.md` §4); **F** is the best fixed tuned rule (§6.6). The on-ramps are called **OR1, OR2** here, so that they cannot be confused with the arms R1–R4 [AUTHOR A13]. **ℓ₁, ℓ₂, ℓ₃** are the three P1 levels (§5.0). A level is **admissible** if it is not plant-invalid under the single admissibility rule of §8.8. New in v2:
- **S** = the co-primary smoothness measure; **T** = the co-primary safety measure; **h_all** = the all-vehicle hard-braking rate at speed (§3.1). **C**, the generalised cost, is a reported secondary result (V74).
- **INC** = the incident regime (§2.4b); **I1, I2, I3** = its three inflow levels; **x_I** = 13,000 m, the incident position; **T_on** = 3,000 s, its onset.
- **Scenario point s** = a no-incident level ℓ or an INC point; **S_tuned** = the tuned scenario points (§6.2). **K** = the scored vehicle set: the cohort at no-incident points, **cohort_I** at INC points (§2.4b), where the guards also run on the full cohort. **t_blk** = the obstacle's block start; **t_b** = a probe's sustained-onset minute (§3.2).
- **CD0–CD4** = the capacity-drop candidates of the C0′ ladder (§2.6): CD0 is the v1 plant, CD1–CD4 are the brief's C1–C4, renamed so that they cannot be confused with the calibration items C1–C11.
- **LM-B, LM-E** = the lane modules shared by the rule arms (balancing at the drop; evacuation at the incident; §6.1). **R-KEEP, R-LANE** = P1-only arms (§5.0).
- **Lane indices** are SUMO's (right-hand convention): lane 0 is the nearside lane next to the ramps, and "left" means index + 1, towards lane 2 (§1.1 item 32).

**Design rule.** Every phase is ordered so that the most likely failure is tested first, with the fewest runs and the least code written. v5 never obtained an artefact-free capacity drop in SUMO, a published benchmark found none at a SUMO merge (Boukhellouf, Buisson & Chiabaut 2023 [VERIFY]), and the pre-P0 pilot found none on F3Z up to λ = 1.5 (V73, not data). Under V74 the claim no longer rests on delay but on smoothness and safety, so the first gate (P0-G1) tests what the claim still needs from the bottleneck: a plausible, artefact-free queue discharge. The capacity drop is pursued by a pre-registered ladder of published or developer-advised settings (CD1 rests on developer advice [DEV]), screened on the calibration seeds (V70; that timing differs from V70's text, §1.1 item 39 and [AUTHOR A41] (vii)), and its ratio is gated or report-only per [AUTHOR A42]. **The claim metric itself changed after the pilot: §0.5 discloses why and when.** The cheapest likely stop is metric futility (F6) on the calibration seeds, before any check seed. Every KILL in this protocol is conservative: it can stop a claim, never create one.

---

## 0 Purpose and scope

### 0.1 What the three phases decide

| Phase | Question | Possible outcomes |
|---|---|---|
| **P0** plant realism and metric informativeness | Does F3Z with the V42 fleet discharge from its bottleneck like a real freeway, with waves travelling upstream at a plausible speed and congestion starting at the bottlenecks? Does it receive its inflow without insertion artefacts and run free of simulator artefacts? Are its energy levels plausible? Do uncommanded CAVs (B1) avoid model artefacts? Is the 0.2-s step converged enough? Is there a capacity drop (gated or report-only per [AUTHOR A42])? **New:** do S and T carry congestion-related information on this plant (P0-G6)? **New, regime-specific:** does the incident behave like a one-of-three-lanes blockage, delivered identically to every arm and without artefacts (P0-GI)? | PASS → P1. A realism FAIL → STOP; pre-registered ladders only: at most two plant-model variants and at most six setting changes after the original plant (k ≤ 7 counting the original, V59), each on fresh seeds (§9.2), then **P0 KILL**. **G6 FAIL → STOP** (no V74 claim is measurable on this plant; [AUTHOR A53]). **GI FAIL → that INC point (or the regime) is dropped and disclosed**; this is not a P0 FAIL |
| **P1** tool checks | T0: speed and lane commands are executed as V26/V36/V76 specify, and they bind on traffic. T0g: the incident is delivered identically to every arm. T1: the bottleneck discharges in the experiment scenario on fresh seeds (the drop per [AUTHOR A42]), and control matters on S or T. T2 (report-only): best parameters vary with the scenario. T3: determinism and common random numbers. | PASS → P2. Code FAIL → fix, no seed spent. Speed-actuator FAIL → [AUTHOR A34], decided now. Lane-actuator FAIL → [AUTHOR A46]: lane commands removed from every arm. T1a FAIL → P0 FAIL path. T1b FAIL → [AUTHOR A17], decided now. |
| **P2** scenario band | At which scenario points does the informed oracle O(s) or the best fixed tuned rule F cut **both S and T by ≥ 10 % vs B0** and by ≥ 5 % vs B1, with CIs, within the guards (delay, hard braking, censoring, origin queue, validity) ([AUTHOR A40])? | **GO** iff at least one **no-incident** level is in the band (band, map and designated level ℓ* frozen; the incident regime enters the D5.8 regime set, V75). No no-incident level in the band → **KILL-3**, whatever the INC point shows (principle 15). Two futility KILLs come earlier (§6.5, §6.6). |

**No learning arm (L1, L2) and no VSL reference arm (I1, I2) runs before P2 ends.**

### 0.2 Decision flow

```
 S0 author answers A36–A68 (§11.2, §11.4) ──► Addendum B (VoT, prices, constants; C is still reported) committed
   │
 P0-B code acceptance: plant, inflow, health, metrics (S, T, h_all), stats, seed guard, ops,
      own TTC vs hand values and vs SSM (B14), S on SUMO (B15), incident injector (B16),
      ACC approach pre-screen, energy tests (non-project seeds only)
   │
 P0-S smoke 7,400,000–001 (+ one B0 INC run per seed) ──► Addendum A (estimator and implementation fixes only)
   │
 P0-C calibration 002–009 (throw-away, disclosed):
      C0′ capacity-drop ladder CD0 → CD1 → CD3 → CD4 → CD2, sequential, first screen PASS adopted [A41]
      C1–C8 on the adopted plant (λ_c, λ_sub, BN*, λ_a, insertion, ACC ladder, r, T3-early, CPU caps)
      C9  floors of S and T; informativeness pre-check; TTC* fork 1.5 s → 3.0 s if needed [A37, A53]
      C10 incident: F_inc, binding, teleports, block start; I1 rule; INC points screened [A43]
      C11 SSM cross-check (report)
      ──► futility stops F1–F6 ──► P0 FAIL path (§9.2), STOP, or INC dropped; no check seed spent
   ▼  calibration report (design quantities and futility outcomes only, §4.3) ──► Addendum D (CPU plan)
 P0-G1  discharge + artefacts at BN* (gated); drop ratio per A42 (010–039)   ◄── checked first, alone
   ▼ PASS
 P0-G2  onset location, waves (gated), artefacts, insertion, desired speeds, B0 triangle (040–059)
 P0-G3  uncommanded CAV artefacts, B1 vs the same vehicles in B0 (040–059)
 P0-G4  energy (deterministic)      P0-G5  step 0.1 s vs 0.2 s, paired by seed, J1–J3 gated (040–059)
 P0-G6  informativeness of S and T in B0 (040–059, no extra runs)
 P0-GI  incident plant check at I1–I3, B0 and B1 (040–059)
   │    realism FAIL ──► ladders, k ≤ 7 ──► P0 KILL ;  G6 FAIL ──► STOP ;  GI FAIL ──► INC point dropped
   ▼ PASS     (exec with lane calls, v2x with lane fields and the incident message, R4, R-KEEP, R-LANE
   │           are built only now; Addendum C committed before seed 060)
 P1 (060–089): T0a exec · T0b authority · T0c bottleneck authority (Dl, throughput, S, T) · T0d sensing
               T0e lane execution · T0f lane authority (λ_sub and ℓ₂) · T0g incident integrity
               T1a discharge on fresh seeds · T1b control matters on S, T (incl. I2) · T3 determinism + CRN
   │    T0b/T0c FAIL ──► [A34] ; T0f FAIL ──► [A46] lane commands removed ; T1a FAIL ──► P0 FAIL path ;
   │    T1b FAIL ──► [A17] proceed
   │    P1 report: σ_S, σ_T, corr(r_S, r_T), predicted P(enter) ──► resolution-futility STOP [A68]
   ▼ PASS     R1–R3 + lane modules LM-B, LM-E built ──► P2-B ──► checkpoint "plant and all opponents done"
 P2 stage 1 (100–107): B0, B1, every candidate at every tuned point (8 levels + INC point I2)
   │    edge-level and parameter-adequacy checks ──► triggered additions on 100–107 (§6.10)
   │    KILL-1: at every no-incident tuned level, no candidate reaches g_S ≥ t_S and g_T ≥ t_T (§6.5)
 P2 stage 2 (108–137): finalists ──► O(s), F (F chosen on no-incident levels only)
   │    KILL-2: at every no-incident tuned level, no finalist reaches 10 % on both S and T in sample
   ▼    n_eval ∈ {40, 60} fixed (σ rule + predicted pass rate of the whole band rule, §6.7)
 P2 held-out (200–239 or 200–259): B0, B1, O(s), F at every tuned point; keep-only twins;
        INC map rows at I1, I3 ──► band rule on S and T (both, same arm; per-point informativeness [A59];
        INC guards on cohort_I and on the full cohort [A55])
        no no-incident level in the band ──► KILL-3 ; otherwise GO: band, map, ℓ* (no-incident), regime set frozen.
        Only now may a learning arm run.
```

### 0.3 Principles that bind inside this protocol
1. **Before data.** Criteria, seeds, sample sizes and statistics are fixed here. Every [AUTHOR] item is answered before seed 7,400,000 (A9 and A30, and the content that A47 and A48 decide, before seed 7,400,060), before any output it governs exists. Values marked [ADDENDUM] are fixed in a dated addendum before the data they govern. P0 and P1 results may feed only a dated addendum that **tightens** a bound; they never open a decision that this protocol has closed.
2. **KILL first.** The most likely failure is tested first, with the fewest runs and the least code. A KILL rule may only stop a claim.
3. **Intersection-union.** All gated criteria of one check must pass. For an "all must pass" rule no multiplicity correction is needed (an intersection-union test keeps level α without one); where a decision applies one anyway (V74, [AUTHOR A39]), this is stated as conservative.
4. **Union claims are corrected.** "At least one level", "at least one rule" or "at least one arm" claims use Bonferroni (α/m), with m stated per family (§8.3).
5. **Nothing is dropped.** Every run goes into the ledger. FAIL, invalid and collapsed runs count against the arm that produced them (V6; ARCH docs/lab/t1_bn4_plant_checks_protocol.md:64). An operations failure (a hung or killed worker, §2.7) is re-run once on the same seed and logged; it is never scored as the arm's failure and never silently dropped.
6. **Bounds are never relaxed after data** (ARCH docs/lab/t2_zipmerge_protocol.md:38; ARCH docs/lab/gate_ctx_r6/s1_protocol.md:164-166).
7. **Not computable = FAIL** for every gated statistic (ARCH docs/lab/t2_realism_protocol.md, Addendum A).
8. **Fresh seeds for every new round** (CLAUDE.md). A seed guard in code refuses any seed outside its role (§7).
9. **Never B0 alone.** From P1 on, every table shows B1 and the tuned rules next to B0 (CLAUDE.md, Metrics).
10. **No continuous model parameter is fitted to pass a realism check.** Calibration fixes design quantities (levels, cells, references) by rules written now. Choosing among **pre-registered discrete candidates** (the capacity-drop candidates CD0–CD4, the insertion configurations, the ACC candidates A0–A3, the step, the TTC threshold fork) on the throw-away calibration seeds, and confirming the choice on fresh check seeds, is allowed and disclosed; every look is counted (k, k_cal; §9.2) and reported. V36's "calibrated ACC base model" is read as this selection [AUTHOR A11]. Driver, truck and energy model parameters are never tuned towards a realism band (that would make the check circular); in v2 this rules out any rubbernecking or driver-parameter step chosen to bring the incident's remaining capacity into its band ([AUTHOR A43] (viii)).
11. **Counted, not hidden.** Every plant setting tried on check seeds, every capacity-drop candidate and ACC candidate screened, the TTC threshold in force, and every addendum is listed in the phase report with its seeds and outcome.
12. **Co-primaries are an intersection.** A claim at a (scenario point, arm) pair needs **both** S and T to meet their effect criteria ([AUTHOR A40]). No claim, in P2 or in the learning phases, rests on one co-primary alone ([AUTHOR A54] (a)). The level of each component is fixed by [AUTHOR A39].
13. **Windows and cohorts are arm-independent.** Every spatial window, temporal window and cohort is defined from the network, the manifest and the reference times (C5, C6), never from an arm's outcome; incident windows are written relative to T_on. No window is chosen after data. **Harm outside a scored cohort is guarded:** at INC points the guards also run on the full cohort ([AUTHOR A55]).
14. **A metric is never made informative by changing the plant.** If S or T is uninformative on the plant (C9, P0-G6), no plant variant may be used to repair it. Only the pre-registered threshold fork of [AUTHOR A37] (a) is allowed.
15. **A regime cannot rescue a KILL.** The incident regime is tuned and reported in P2, but KILL-1, KILL-2 and KILL-3 are decided on no-incident levels only, and ℓ* is a no-incident level. An INC band point without a no-incident band level is reported and never yields a GO. This principle was written by the drafters, not chosen by the author; [AUTHOR A44] (d) offers the alternative (an incident-only claim) with its cost.

### 0.4 Expected outcome, written before data (my judgement, not a measurement)
- **A capacity drop in the field band is unlikely.** The pilot found none up to λ = 1.5: the drop discharged about 1,920 veh/h/lane at every inflow (V73, not data). No published SUMO lane-drop demonstration was found (`capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` §4a). CD1 is the only candidate with a stated mechanism at a zipper, and it rests on developer advice [DEV] with no published numbers. In the mechanics toy, CD1 lowered zipper discharge by about 6 % against the default zipper; that is not a drop ratio (evidence: mechanics). Under [AUTHOR A42] (b) the gated part of P0-G1 (discharge 1,600–2,300 veh/h/lane, no artefacts) is expected to PASS; under (a), G1 is expected to FAIL unless a CD candidate qualifies.
- **P0-G3 with the SUMO ACC defaults (A0) is expected to FAIL**, and A0 is also expected to fail the ACC approach pre-screen (§2.6 C4). In the pilot, B1 had 4–5 collisions per 5 runs at λ ≥ 0.85 and 3–4 times B0's emergency-braking warnings (V69, not data). In the v1 toy (not F3Z), a lone ACC CAV at 27.78 m/s approaching a slower leader first braked at a 98.5 m gap, at −6.06, −5.12 and −4.33 m/s², beyond its own 4.5 m/s² decel, with no cut-in; the EIDM HDV's minimum in the same scenario was −1.71 m/s² (toy a_L0_free_slower_leader_cav.csv; whether this is an ACC mode switch near 100 m is [VERIFY]). In mixed toy traffic (3 → 2 lanes, 30 % ACC, 0.2 s) the CAV/HDV ratio of per-step severe deceleration depends on how the 4.5 m/s² decel cap is counted: 3.1–3.7 counting steps at or beyond the cap, 9.1–11.9 counting "a ≤ −4.5 m/s²" (floating-point rounding then decides whether 9–26 % of the at-cap steps count), 11.5–16.6 counting steps strictly beyond the cap (scratchpad review_phys/knife.py, seeds 1–2; my arithmetic). About 67 % of toy events came within 1 s of a leader change (toy b_diag.py), so about a third were not cut-ins. **This is the most likely P0 FAIL** under A42 (b).
- **T at TTC\* = 1.5 s is expected to be uninformative, so the C9 fork to 3.0 s is expected to fire.** In the rigor toy (EIDM, a 3 → 2 zipper, seeds 1–3; not F3Z), TET below 1.5 s at speed was 0.003 s per 1,000 veh-km in a heavy queue, lower than in free flow (0.041), with 0–1 conflict pairs per run; TET below 3.0 s at speed rose 23-fold from free flow to the heavy queue. The mechanics toy (30 % ACC) and the metrics toy (Krauss + ACC) also found TET at 1.5 s near zero (evidence: mechanics, metrics).
- **S is expected to be informative from about λ_c on.** The rigor toy's heavy-queue S is about 5 times its free-flow value (5.3 × with the 300-s boxcar, 5.2 × with the Gaussian trend of [AUTHOR A58]; review toy), and the pilot saw 8–11 stop-and-go oscillations per run from λ ≈ 0.85 (pilot README; not data).
- **The incident may be inadmissible at its highest level.** In the incident toy, a stopped obstacle in an outer lane gave teleports in 2 of 24 runs (Clopper–Pearson 95 % CI 0.01–0.27, evidence: incident). With a true rate of 8 %, a level passes the ≤ 5 % admissibility rule with probability 0.52 at n = 20 and 0.37 at n = 40 (binomial, my arithmetic). The block start can depend on the arm: in the incident toy (lane 0, insertion 600 m upstream 40 s before onset, no rubbernecking), it lay 10.4 s before to 2.6 s after the intended onset over 18 runs at 3,000–5,100 veh/h, and on lane 2 at 5,100 veh/h up to 34.4 s after it (this synthesis's read of `inc_toy/runs/*/result.json`).
- **Lane commands under the gap-respecting mode 1878 will rarely execute in dense traffic.** In the mechanics toy, 4–8 % of random requests executed within 2 s at 6,000 veh/h on three lanes, and 33–43 % at 3,000 veh/h (evidence: mechanics; those rows ran mode 1877, and 1878 with an explicit hold; 1878 with keep = no request was not stress-tested). Whether lane commands bind inside the queue at ℓ₂ (T0f(b)) is uncertain.
- **λ = 1.0 is probably over capacity and may be drain-capped** on F3Z (§2.1 desk estimate; the pilot's B1 left 485 vehicles unfinished at the cap at λ = 1.0, not data). It may be plant-invalid or fail the band guards by construction [AUTHOR A6].
- **The joint bar is harder than v1's single bar.** Under the normal planning approximation at n = 40, σ = 0.085 and m = 18 (§6.8), a pair with a true 12 % gain on both co-primaries enters with probability ≈ 0.78 if the two estimates are independent, and at most 0.88 (one co-primary's value) if they are perfectly correlated (my arithmetic). Under negative dependence it is below 0.78: in the rigor toy (3 seeds, not F3Z) the harm70 stand-in ranked its S and T changes in opposite order across seeds (S −8.2 / −11.1 / −11.0 %, T −0.6 / +4.0 / +14.5 %), so the independent value is not a lower bound (§6.8). **If P0 passes, a P2 KILL is still more likely than a GO.** No source read in the evidence pass reports an effect of CAV control on TET or on within-vehicle speed SD on an open three-lane freeway with about 26 % CAVs (evidence: lanes, metrics). The ring-road magnitudes (Stern et al. 2018, full text: speed SD −80.8 % in experiment A, −49.5 % in B, −54.7 % in C) come from one AV among about 21 vehicles at about 7 m/s and do not transfer. Published hybrid gains over fixed-parameter controllers are about 1.5–6 % (CLAUDE.md prior), on cost-type metrics.

### 0.5 Why the headline changed, and when (disclosure)
- **Date and order.** The claim metric moved from the generalised cost C and delay (v1: V29, V45) to the co-primaries S and T on 2026-10-13 (V74). That was **after** the pre-P0 pilot (V69, V73: non-project seeds 1–5, B0 and B1 only, no controller, **not data**) and **before** any project seed.
- **What the pilot had shown** (`docs/research/pilot/README.md` §2, findings 2–4; V74's own reason in `decisions.md`):
  - about 85–95 % of the delay is unavoidable queueing at the drop;
  - no capacity drop at any λ ≤ 1.5;
  - therefore "delay gains from harmonisation are capped. The ≥ 10 % bar then becomes unlikely (the early-KILL path)" (README §2, finding 2): **the pilot predicted that the approved v1 headline would end in a KILL**;
  - stop-and-go waves from λ ≈ 0.85 (8–11 oscillations per run), which S measures;
  - B1 (default ACC) with 3–4 × B0's emergency-braking warnings and 4–5 collisions per 5 runs at λ ≥ 0.85, which T and h_all measure.
- **So the switch is outcome-informed.** The new headline measures what the pilot saw, after the pilot showed that the old one would probably fail. What keeps it honest: no project data was seen, and every definition, threshold, window, guard and test of S and T is fixed in this protocol before seed 7,400,000 (principle 1, §9.4).
- **Consequence for claims.** Every thesis sentence that states an S or T result carries a short form of this disclosure (§6.8 wording rule, §12.2). C, delay, served throughput and TTT stay in every table, and delay is still a gate (V37).

## 1 Decisions implemented

Every V# of `docs/decisions.md` has a row, including the superseded and pending ones.

| V# | What it fixes in this protocol | Where |
|---|---|---|
| V1, V7, V17, V51 | Branches, the trainer and planned profiles: outside P0–P2 | — |
| V2, V56, V68, V71 | Network F3Z, evolved from CORR2X v3, with the V68 positions (merge 1 at 3,000–3,250 m, merge 2 at 9,000–9,250 m, lane drop at 15,000 m, road end 18,250 m). Zones per V71: **Z1 8,000–9,000, Z2 11,000–12,000, Z3 14,000–15,000 m**; Z3+ 15,000–16,000 m is an observation-only V2X unit (A30).
