### 1.1 Conflicts and gaps in the binding inputs (each recorded as a decision before the data it governs)
*Items 1–20 are v1's (answered 2026-10-13, V58–V65; §11, Decision column). Items 21–38 are new in v2. Items 39–43 come from the v2 design review (Appendix E).*
1. **Hold mechanism.** V36 (decided) says "`slowDown` over 2 s, then `setSpeed` to hold". `interfaces.md` §3 still said "re-issuing `slowDown(target, 2.0)` holds it", and its Entry row did not say how the rounded target is reached. Both holds are exact in the toy (max deviation 0.000000 km/h; toy summ_a.py A2, A3). This protocol implements and tests V36, with the entry target reached by a `slowDown` ramp (§2.5). [AUTHOR A9]: correct `interfaces.md` §3 (done in v1, V65).
2. **Reference-time class.** `interfaces.md` §5.2 defined r(route, class) but not "class". If it meant the driver class, the same vehicle would have different references in B0 (HDV) and B1 (CAV), which biases C across arms. [AUTHOR A8]: (a), `interfaces.md` §5.2 amended in v1 (V65).
3. **V42 arithmetic.** The five sales shares sum to 98.39 %. Renormalised, HDV cars are 60.0 % ICE (my arithmetic); V42 says "about 59 %", which matches keeping the 1.61 % residual inside the HDV cars (58.7 %). [AUTHOR A13]: renormalise (V65); V42's wording in `decisions.md` is not amended by this protocol.
4. **Desired speed of CAVs in B1** was not specified anywhere, and it changes B1 − B0 and every commanding arm's delay vs B0 (§2.3). [AUTHOR A13] (iii); confirmed by V72 (b).
5. **"The realistic level"** (D5.8; CLAUDE.md early KILL) is undefined under the V31 sweep, and on paper λ = 1.0 exceeds F3Z's capacity (§2.1). [AUTHOR A6]: the designated level ℓ* (V65); in v2 ℓ* is a no-incident level (principle 15).
6. **VoT in 2026 €** is still [TBD] (`interfaces.md` §5.4). The PAG category is already fixed there (cars: commuting €8.90 per person-hour × 1.23 occupancy; trucks: work VTT × 1.03, V54); only the uplift to 2026 € is open. It needs no data, so it is frozen in Addendum B **before seed 7,400,000** (C stays a reported secondary). [AUTHOR A18].
7. **Archive runner defaults** that would silently change results: step 0.5 s default, `collision.check-junctions false`, collisions counted per vehicle-step and FAIL on any collision (against V6's > 2), a determinism hash over aggregates only (port_map_v0.md runner traps 5, 7; corr_run traps (a)–(c)). §2.2 and §2.7 fix each.
8. **Truck fuel price.** V21 prices are retail incl. taxes; TAF §8.11.3 treats work fuel net of VAT (irish_parameters_v0.md §2). [AUTHOR A18] (report-only sensitivity row).
9. **Invalid runs beyond V6.** V6 lists collisions and teleports only. This protocol also counts the other §2.7 health FAILs (in v2 including incident non-delivery) as invalid runs of their arm, and separates operations failures (re-run once) from plant collapse (arm FAIL). [AUTHOR A19].
10. **Per-episode inflow noise.** `interfaces.md` §4 [PROPOSAL] includes it; this protocol switches it off in P0–P2. [AUTHOR A12].
11. **B12/B13/B14** are draft inputs, not V-decisions (decisions.md header). [AUTHOR A12, A28].
12. **ACC base model.** `system_context.md` §3 says "SUMO ACC, calibrated"; ladder candidate A3 (§2.6) departs from it, and "calibrated" names no target. [AUTHOR A11].
13. **Truck length.** The archive `_IDM_TRK` uses 12 m; V40 describes a 40 t articulated vehicle. [AUTHOR A13]: 16.5 m [VERIFY].
14. ARCH design_v0.md §3 lists "trucks 10 %"; V40 (7.5 %) supersedes it and is used here.
15. **Headline vs co-headline (v1).** v5b1 CLAUDE.md (Metrics) makes door-to-door delay including origin queues the primary metric, co-headlined with served throughput; V29 made the generalised cost C the headline. Superseded in v2 by item 25.
16. **Hard-brake speed condition.** V36 fixes 3 m/s² with no speed condition; draft input B14 says safety is counted at speed (zipper-queue creeping is not a safety event). [AUTHOR A28] for h and the G3 events; [AUTHOR A38] for h_all; [AUTHOR A37] for T.
17. **V18 (pending) and V22 (open item).** [AUTHOR A35] (closed V18; V22 a disclosed mismatch).
18. **Release on zone exit.** V36 and `interfaces.md` §1/§3/§9 release CAVs with `setSpeed(−1)` at zone exit. [AUTHOR A10]: (a). v2 adds the lane-request cancel and mode restore before it (§2.5).
19. **Non-learning adaptive scheduler.** CLAUDE.md (Method, headroom) includes one in "best non-learning". [AUTHOR A31]: dropped from P2, disclosed (V63).
20. **Geometry vs nominal inflow.** V58/V64 moved the 3 → 2 lane drop to the downstream end of Z3 (15,000 m under V68), so the first jam forms inside the zones. The B13 nominal inflow still exceeds the lane-count capacity of the two lanes after the drop by about 32–65 % (§2.1). [AUTHOR A26] is superseded by V58/V64.
21. **V74 "safety = TET plus hard braking" vs the brief's "the hard-braking constraint stays".** The reading is not settled here: it is [AUTHOR A38]. Its recommended option (a) reads hard braking as a constraint (not worse by more than y), not as a part of the ≥ 10 % safety improvement; that **narrows V74's text**, and A38 labels the V74-literal readings (c) and (d).
22. **V74 "each needing ≥ 10 %" plus "a multiplicity correction for testing two".** When both must pass, an intersection-union test keeps the level without a correction (Berger 1982 [VERIFY]; principle 3). [AUTHOR A39] decides whether the decided correction is applied anyway (recommended: yes, as V74 states).
23. **V74's TTC threshold of 1.5 s vs the measured toy base rate** (§0.4). [AUTHOR A37]: keep 1.5 s with a pre-registered fork to 3.0 s.
24. **V45 defines the band on C; V74 demotes C.** [AUTHOR A40] re-reads V45 on the co-primaries.
25. **CLAUDE.md (Metrics) makes door-to-door delay with served throughput the primary; V74 makes S and T the claim.** V74 is newer and more specific and is followed. Delay (with origin queues), served throughput and TTT stay in every table, and delay is a gate (V37). The binding repo text would still contradict v2 after approval, so the CLAUDE.md Metrics bullet is added to the companion edits (Amendment log), and the claim wording is [AUTHOR A56] (item 40).
26. **CLAUDE.md (Method) lists "T1: a capacity drop exists" as a tool check.** [AUTHOR A42] (b) makes the drop ratio report-only. That would be a disclosed deviation from CLAUDE.md, decided by the author before data.
27. **V36 and `interfaces.md` §3 say "exactly one `exec` call per CAV per boundary".** V76 adds a lane call. v2 reads it as one speed call plus at most one lane call (§2.5); `interfaces.md` §3 needs a companion edit.
28. **`system_context.md` §2 puts incidents out of scope and names per-CAV speed commands as the only actuator.** V75 and V76 change both (companion edit). VSL and meters stay out (V15).
29. **V27/V36 allow only own speed and the leader radar onboard.** Lane decisions need side and rear sensing. [AUTHOR A47].
30. **V11 says HDV hard braking is a placeholder; A29 made it report-only.** [AUTHOR A38] (a) gates all-vehicle at-speed hard braking. If chosen, A29 is superseded for at-speed events.
31. **`interfaces.md` §8 has `Discrete(3)`.** Under V76 the learning-phase action space is `Discrete(9)` with an action mask (companion edit; learning phase, not P0–P2).
32. **Right-hand lane indexing.** SUMO's default right-hand convention is kept: lane 0 is the nearside lane next to the ramps; "left" means index + 1, towards lane 2 (the median side). The M50 drives on the left; the model is its mirror image. LC2013 keepRight becomes "keep to the nearside". Disclosed; no decision needed.
33. **The archive incident method failed.** The archive converted a stream vehicle into a 360-s blockage; teleports occurred in 209 of 220 runs (ARCH STATUS #18, via evidence: incident). A 900-s blockage exceeds the 300-s teleport time even more, which is why V6 strictness and admissibility are pre-registered (§2.4b). v2 uses a dedicated, non-cohort obstacle with a scheduled stop (§2.4b).
34. **V70 vs principle 10 and §9.2 (new in the synthesis).** CD3 sets the EIDM driving error `sigmaerror` to 0.1 (SUMO source default 0.04: 2.5 times the default; `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` §2c, §4b), and CD4 sets Krauss `sigma` to 0.8 (default 0.5). Both are driver-error terms above the SUMO defaults, which v1 §9.2 lists as "never used as a fix" (the archive H4 collapse). V70 (decided) authorises adopting a published setting before P0. [AUTHOR A41] (v): allow CD3 and CD4 only as pre-registered candidates adopted through the C0′ calibration screen, whose artefact conditions and futility stop F3 are the H4 guard, and keep the §9.2 ban for every other use (recommended); or drop CD3 and CD4 from the ladder.
35. **V74 "speed fluctuation in the jam area" vs the S window (new).** S is measured over W1, the whole route upstream of 15,500 m, which is wider than the jam area, because a jam-area window can be escaped by relocating the jam (§3.4). The jam-area rows (W2 = [8,000, 15,500) m and the strips) are reported. [AUTHOR A36].
36. **V75 "starting during the rising peak" has no exact time** (new). v2 fixes T_on = 3,000 s, 35 min into the scored window and 36 min before the inflow peak ([AUTHOR A43] (iii)).
37. **V75 "a pre-registered regime" vs the band (new).** The incident regime enters the D5.8 regime set at GO if its points passed P0-GI, whether or not an INC point is in the band; it can never produce the GO itself (principle 15; [AUTHOR A44]).
38. **Addendum G1 omission (new).** v1 §3.2 had probes "at x = 250·i m (i = 1…36)", which covers only the V64 length. Under V68 the range is i = 1…72 (§3.2).
39. **V70's procedure vs the C0′ screen (review).** V70 asks for a setting adopted "before P0", "never tuned on our data", "recorded as a dated protocol addendum before seed 7,400,000". C0′ adopts it inside P0-C on calibration seeds 002–009 by a screen on our data, from pre-registered discrete candidates; one candidate (CD1) is developer advice [DEV], not a published study. v1's principle 10 already allowed such a discrete calibration-seed choice, and `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` §4b proposed the screen for the author to decide. CD2 is a layout change (V69 requires a decision and a dated addendum before project seeds; approving v2 is that decision). [AUTHOR A41] (vii) offers the V70-literal procedure.
40. **V74 vs CLAUDE.md "no proxy headline metrics" and the CLAUDE.md primary metric (review).** T is a surrogate safety measure from microsimulation (TTC exposure at the TTC\* in force, possibly 3.0 s after C9), not a crash rate; the word "safer" built on it is a proxy headline unless the claim names the measure. [AUTHOR A56] fixes the claim wording; the CLAUDE.md Metrics bullet gets a companion edit.
41. **V69 vs the pilot-informed changes (review).** V69: "No PASS rule or bound may change because of it". [AUTHOR A42] (b) changes PASS rules partly on pilot evidence; V-1's removal and the CD order use pilot facts as design reasons. Listed in the §1 V67/V69/V73 row; [AUTHOR A66].
42. **D5.8 "not significantly worse by > 5 % in any pre-registered regime" has no fixed statistic (review).** CLAUDE.md gives no direction, level or reference; the readings differ in what the claim means. [AUTHOR A57] fixes the statistic, the reference and the regime granularity before P2 results.
43. **V74 "speed fluctuation" vs a filter whose response depends on the oscillation period (review).** The centred 300-s boxcar trend amplifies periods of about 150–300 s (variance gain up to 1.48) and attenuates longer ones, so S can change with the period at equal amplitude. [AUTHOR A58].

---

## 2 Plant and fleet as built

*Nothing is built yet on `v5b1`. This section is the specification the port must meet. P0-B checks the built artefacts against it, and the P0 report replaces "as specified" with the built values and hashes. If a value changes after a check has run, that check is re-run on fresh seeds.*

### 2.1 Network F3Z (V2, V30, V58/V64, V68, V71)
Ported from ARCH vsl_lab/netgen/corr2.py `SPEC_AUX` (CORR2X v3) and trimmed per `port_map_v0.md`: v_main changed from 33.33 to **27.78 m/s** (V30); TLS meter nodes, the all-green program, ALINEA loops and the O1 branch removed (V15); the spec passed explicitly; a mandatory spec hash (port_map corr2 traps 1–4); every edge given an explicit nominal length (ARCH notes_networks.md L1: MRG3 edges drifted to 465.6 m instead of 500 m without explicit lengths). **Layout:** the order and lane counts of V58/V64 (the 3 → 2 lane drop at the downstream end of Z3), with the V68 positions (5 km of plain motorway before each control zone's trouble spot) and the V71 zones on the drop's approach (Addenda G1 and Z1 of v1, folded in here). Edge ids and split points inside a section of constant lane count are provisional and fixed at the port; the x ranges, lane counts and zones are those of V68 and V71.

| Edge group | x from–to (m) | Lanes | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| entry | 0–3,000 | 3 | no zone (V71) |
| merge1 | 3,000–3,250 | 4 (aux lane 0 + 3) | **BN-M1**: OR1 joins; aux lane 0 continues as off-ramp X1 |
| plain 1 | 3,250–9,000 | 3 | **Z1 = 8,000–9,000** (ends where merge 2 starts) |
| merge2 | 9,000–9,250 | 4 (aux lane 0 + 3) | **BN-M2**: OR2 joins; aux lane 0 continues as off-ramp X2 |
| plain 2 | 9,250–15,000 | 3 | **Z2 = 11,000–12,000**; **incident point x_I = 13,000** (INC only, §2.4b); **Z3 = 14,000–15,000** |
| zipper node | 15,000 | 3 → 2 | **BN-LD**: lanes 0, 1 → 0; lane 2 → 1 |
| exit | 15,000–18,250 | 2 | **Z3+** = 15,000–16,000: V2X summary unit, observation only, no commands (A30) |
| OR1o → OR1, OR2o → OR2 | 200 m + 400 m | 1 | on-ramps, 25 m/s (archive spec; re-confirm at the port); no traffic light (V15) |

- **Routes** (ARCH vsl_lab/plants/corr2.py:49-53): 10 % of mainline-origin vehicles leave at X1, 10 % at X2; 10 % of OR1 vehicles leave at X2; OR2 vehicles all continue [AUTHOR A13].
- **Zones** are defined by the mainline position x of the vehicle front, not by edge id (zones split edges; review_v0 EX-C6). Zone gaps: Z1 → Z2 over 9,000–11,000 m (contains merge 2); Z2 → Z3 over 12,000–14,000 m (contains x_I). **V2X "next downstream zone"** (`interfaces.md` §6/§7): Z1 → Z2, Z2 → Z3, Z3 → Z3+. No zone lies upstream of merge 1: merge 1 stays a road feature with its detectors and health checks (§2.7, §3.2), but no CAV is commanded there.
- **Bottleneck reference points** x_k, taken from the built net and recorded with the network hash before calibration: BN-M1 = end of the merge1 auxiliary section (nominal 3,250 m); BN-M2 = end of the merge2 auxiliary section (9,250 m); BN-LD = zipper node (15,000 m). **Bottleneck groups** for onset location: [x_k − 750, x_k + 250] m, i.e. [2,500, 3,500], [8,500, 9,500] and [14,250, 15,250] m (non-overlapping). **Entry group**: [0, 1,000] m. **Incident group** (INC runs only, for onset attribution): [12,250, 13,250] m; in no-incident runs an onset there counts as "open road".
- **Measurement sections**: 3,400 / 9,400 / 15,100 m (§3.2). **Incident sections** (INC only): flow section 13,100 m (all three lanes, 100 m past the obstacle), head probe 12,750 m, downstream probe 13,250 m (both on the 250-m probe grid). **Lane-use sections**: 8,990, 11,990 and 14,790 m (the downstream ends of Z1, Z2 and of Z3's commandable part), 12,750 m and 15,100 m.
- **Lane-command ban segment** [14,800, 15,000) m: the last 200 m before BN-LD, enforced by the `exec` mask on the CAV's **predicted** position over the request's 2-s life (§2.5; [AUTHOR A45]); no network change. **Zipper zone** for the T rows: [14,940, 15,060) m.
- **Desk estimate per section, not measured** (my arithmetic from the route shares above; lane-count capacity only, with c = 1,600–2,000 veh/h/lane; weaving and uneven lane use lower these values; ARCH design_v0.md §3 gives 3,800–4,000 veh/h for two lanes, i.e. c = 1,900–2,000). Lane counts, routes and flows are unchanged by V68, so this table and the critical levels are those of v1 at the new positions:

  | Section | x (m) | Lanes | Flow at λ (veh/h) | Per lane | λ at which flow reaches lane-count capacity |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | entry | 0–3,000 | 3 | 4,000λ | 1,333λ | 1.20–1.50 |
  | merge1 weave (BN-M1) | 3,000–3,250 | 4 | 5,200λ in; 1,200λ move left (OR1), 400λ move right (to X1) | 1,300λ | 1.23–1.54 before the weave penalty |
  | plain 1 (Z1) | 3,250–9,000 | 3 | 4,800λ | 1,600λ | 1.00–1.25 |
  | merge2 weave (BN-M2) | 9,000–9,250 | 4 | 5,800λ in; 1,000λ move left (OR2), 520λ move right (to X2) | 1,450λ | 1.10–1.38 before the weave penalty |
  | plain 2 (Z2, x_I, Z3) | 9,250–15,000 | 3 | 5,280λ | 1,760λ | 0.91–1.14 |
  | **exit, after the lane drop (BN-LD)** | 15,000–18,250 | 2 | **5,280λ** | **2,640λ** | **0.61–0.76** (0.72–0.76 at design_v0's c) |

  Consequences, all labelled as desk expectations:
  - **BN-LD binds first on paper**, at the downstream end of Z3, as V58 intended. BN* (chosen from data in C0′/C2) is therefore expected to be BN-LD. The pilot agrees (not data): the queue started at the drop between λ ≈ 0.75 and 0.80, and merges 1 and 2 never became active bottlenecks below λ = 1.0 (V69, V73).
  - The next tightest section, plain 2 (Z2, x_I and Z3 lie on it), reaches its lane-count capacity only near λ ≈ 0.91–1.14; plain 1 and the two weaves come later still. Below λ ≈ 0.91 the bottlenecks therefore do not interact much: on paper BN-M1 and BN-M2 cannot starve BN-LD. Once BN-LD is active, its queue grows upstream through Z3, past x_I and Z2, towards merge 2, which ends 5,750 m upstream of the drop; a queue spilled back into merge 2 is not an active onset at BN-M2 (§3.2, downstream-probe test). The drop estimators still exclude upstream starvation (§3.2), which matters near and above λ ≈ 0.91.
  - λ = 1.0 puts 1.32–1.65 times BN-LD's lane-count capacity on the exit, while plain 2 carries 0.88–1.10 times its own. A point-queue model of the drop on the §2.4 triangle (desk estimate: one queue, no spatial storage, travel times ignored; scratchpad protocol_v1/desk_v64.py; it depends on flows, not lengths, so V68 leaves it unchanged) leaves about 1,400 / 2,010 / 2,670 vehicles queued when inflow stops at 9,900 s, at a discharge of 1,900 / 1,750 / 1,600 veh/h/lane; they need about 22 / 34 / 50 min to drain, against the 30-min cap (V49). The three lanes from the end of merge 2 to the drop hold about 2,300 vehicles at a jam spacing of 7.5 m (5,750 m × 3 lanes ÷ 7.5 m; SUMO's default car length 5 m plus minGap 2.5 m; ASSUMPTION, trucks ignored; my arithmetic). The pilot (not data) saw the queue reach Z2 and merge 2 only at λ ≥ 0.95, fill the road to the entry above λ ≈ 1.05, and leave 485 vehicles unfinished at the cap in B1 at λ = 1.0. **λ = 1.0 is therefore likely drain-capped** [AUTHOR A6; A26 superseded by V58/V64].
  - λ_c may lie near 0.6 on the desk estimate (the pilot suggests 0.75–0.80, not data), so C1 keeps its downward extension and its rule for an undefined λ_c (§2.6).
  - This is why the P2 grid is anchored on the measured critical level, with λ = 1.0 always kept as a reported row (§6.2).
- **The lane drop is a SUMO zipper node** (ARCH vsl_lab/netgen/corr2.py:63, :123), kept at its position (a drop without the zipper is pre-registered only as the last capacity-drop candidate CD2, §2.6). Archive zipper bottlenecks discharged from stopped queues at near-fixed headways below the floor (BN4 "a metronome… releasing vehicles one at a time from a stopped zipper queue", ARCH docs/lab/gate_ctx_r6/report_S1_20261004-1508.md:57-59; ZM3 1,528 veh/h/lane, ARCH STATUS.md:57), and most raw SUMO emergency-braking warnings in the CORR2X replay were zipper-queue creeping (B14, ARCH design_v0.md:43). Hence the per-lane discharge FLAG at BN-LD, the expected primary bottleneck (§3.2), and the location and speed attribution of braking events (§2.7, [AUTHOR A28]). The zipper connection attribute `visibility` (default 100 m for zipper links in the 1.27.1 loader; evidence: mechanics, `NLHandler.cpp`) is the knob of candidate CD1 (§2.6).
- **Stranding.** Lane 0 of merge1 continues to X1, so for OR1 vehicles bound for plain 1 the auxiliary lane still ends on their route: stranding at the end of the merge area stays possible, and its detector stays (§2.7). Dead-end acceleration lanes are never reintroduced (port_map trap 12; ARCH STATUS #15).

### 2.2 SUMO configuration (every option explicit; never changed to pass a check, except through the pre-registered discrete ladders of §2.6 and §9.2, each change counted in k or k_cal)

| Option | Value | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| version | SUMO / libsumo 1.27.1, `toolchain/requirements_lock.txt` (V14) | determinism scope (T3) |
| `--step-length` | **0.2 s** | ACC/CACC emergency-braking artefacts at 0.5 s (ARCH STATUS #14); EIDM docs give 0.05–0.5 s [VERIFY]. A realism verdict is step-specific: a step change re-runs P0 on fresh seeds (ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md:175-199) |
| `--time-to-teleport` | 300 s (default, set explicitly); never raised | raising it only hides artefacts (ARCH round2_protocol.md:129). Vehicles at a stop are exempt from the waiting-time teleport, vehicles queued behind the incident obstacle are not (evidence: mechanics) |
| `--collision.action` | `warn` | the default `teleport` turns every collision into a teleport (toy c_collide.py), which would merge V6's two counts; `none` does not count it |
| `--collision.check-junctions` | `true` | F3Z has merges and a zipper; with `false` a merge collision is detected only later, downstream (toy c_junction.py) |
| `--collision.mingap-factor` | `0` | a "collision" means physical overlap whatever the car-following model (toy c_mingap2.py: the default registers Krauss at a 2.4 m gap) |
| `--xml-validation`, `--xml-validation.routes` | `always` | a misspelt vType attribute is otherwise silently ignored (toy b_mixed.py: same hash as the defaults) |
| `--seed` | the run seed, from the seed guard | the SUMO default (23423) is never used |
| `--max-depart-delay` | not set | no vehicle is ever discarded; insertion queues count in door-to-door time |
| outputs | statistic-output, tripinfo-output (departDelay), log with errors captured; per-vehicle per-edge traversal times; power traces (V10) only for the pre-declared subset of §10.4, written from our own per-step read | no fcd or emission files in production runs (toy e_cost.py: 6.3× the step cost, 652 MB per 30 min) |
| HDV car model and BN-LD zipper | **CD0**: EIDM H5 with the default zipper (§2.3), or the candidate adopted by the C0′ ladder ([AUTHOR A41]): **CD1** `visibility="20"` on the BN-LD zipper connections (netconvert keeps the attribute; evidence: mechanics); **CD3** the Salles et al. 2020 EIDM sets (four sets in equal shares, values as listed in `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` §4b; F3Z speed factors, lengths and masses kept, disclosed); **CD4** Krauss `accel=1.5 decel=4.5 tau=1.0 sigma=0.8 sigmaStep=1` for HDV cars; **CD2** lane 0 of plain 2 dead-ends at 15,000 m (no zipper; adopting CD2 puts the lane-0 incident on the ending lane and re-opens the incident lane choice, [AUTHOR A43] (ii)). Trucks keep the EIDM truck type under every candidate (ASSUMPTION; the sources concern cars). **CD3 and CD4 also change G3's yardstick** (the same vehicles' severe events in B0), screened per [AUTHOR A65] | V70; principle 10: discrete, pre-registered, never tuned; the CD3/CD4 error terms per [AUTHOR A41] (v) and §1.1 item 34 |
| lane-change model | LC2013 defaults, written explicitly, enabled for every vehicle (CAVs under command included); **no `lcAssertive`**. Lane-change mode 1621 (default) for every vehicle, except a CAV inside a zone from its first commanded lane request until its zone exit: **M_cmd** ([AUTHOR A45]; recommended **1878**) | lcAssertive moved onsets to the network entry (ARCH STATUS.md:74); V76; §2.5 |
| `--lanechange.duration` | **0** (instantaneous, the default, set explicitly) | [AUTHOR A51]; a non-zero value is a plant-wide change that also affects HDVs (toy: one lane per 0.2-s step) |
| insertion | chosen in calibration (C3) from this fixed order: (`departLane="random"`, `departSpeed="avg"`), (random, desired), (free, avg), (free, desired) on the mainline; ramps lane 0, `"avg"` | `"best"` moved the bottleneck to the insertion point (toy b_mixed.py: departDelay 201 s vs 0.03 s); `"max"` caused insertion failures (ARCH STATUS #9). Checked by P0-G2(d) |
| emergency-braking warning threshold | default | diagnostic only; braking is measured from trace accelerations (toy: 101 warnings vs 216 episodes of a < −4.5 m/s²; warnings fire only at the full emergencyDecel, evidence: mechanics) |
| collision count | statistic-output `safety/collisions` (pairs), cross-checked against distinct (collider, victim) pairs from `getCollisions()` | never the per-step sum of `getCollisions()` (toy: 2,137 entries for 2 collisions) |
| ground-truth read | one plain-getter read per vehicle and step (position, lane, speed, acceleration, fuel, electricity), **plus one `getLeader(id, 150 m)` and the leader's speed** (own TTC, §3.1), shared by every consumer | no subscriptions (toy e_cost.py: slower overall); the 150-m range per [AUTHOR A52] |
| SSM device | **off in every production run.** On only in P0-B14, the C11 cross-check and the T0f SSM rows (§5), with `--device.ssm.probability 1`, one `--device.ssm.file`, measures TTC only, trajectories on, threshold 3.0 s, range 150 m, and **no SSM-side conflict-type exclusion** (the default). The encounter types are filtered offline, in the on-the-fly aggregation, by their numeric codes (SUMO 1.27.1 `MSDevice_SSM.h`, read in this revision): **ego as follower = {2 FOLLOWING_FOLLOWER, 7 MERGING_FOLLOWER, 11 CROSSING_FOLLOWER}**; adjacent-lane types {4 ON_ADJACENT_LANES, 8 MERGING_ADJACENT} reported separately. `--device.ssm.exclude-conflict-types foe` is **not** used: the "foe" set in `MSDevice_SSM.cpp` (tag v1_27_1) is {3, 7, 11, 13, 15}, so it removes MERGING_FOLLOWER (7), the merges where the ego follows, and `flushConflicts` drops a whole record if any type in its type span is excluded, so a zipper merge that turns into car-following ({7, 2}) would vanish (v2 review SF6; the enum, the two sets and `flushConflicts` were read in this revision) [VERIFY the option spellings at the port] | cost (§10.1; evidence: mechanics, metrics: +3.3 to +4.4 µs per vehicle-step, about 6–12 × the plain loop in the metrics toy); without one `--device.ssm.file` each vehicle writes its own file (docs, not tested) |
| process | `unset SUMO_HOME; OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`; one simulation per process; file names include every varying parameter and the PID | CLAUDE.md toolchain |

### 2.3 Fleet (V40–V43, V35, V38, V46, V72)

| Group | Share | Model |
|---|---|---|
| Heavy vehicles | 7.5 % of all vehicles (V40) | 40 t articulated; EIDM truck parameters (archive `_IDM_TRK` with the H5 EIDM layer); vType maxSpeed 25 m/s (90 km/h); length 16.5 m [VERIFY] ([AUTHOR A13], V65); energy by road-load + Willans (V34); never CAVs; unchanged under every CD candidate |
| Cars | 92.5 % of all vehicles | sales mix renormalised over the five V42 classes (my arithmetic, [AUTHOR A13]): petrol 25.52 %, diesel 17.37 %, HEV 22.85 %, PHEV 15.06 %, BEV 19.20 % |
| CAVs | 50 % of electrified + hybrid cars = **28.55 % of cars, 26.41 % of all vehicles** | SUMO ACC, candidate chosen in calibration (C4, [AUTHOR A11]); `speedDev=0`; desired speed 100 km/h, speed factor 1.0 ([AUTHOR A13 (iii)], confirmed by V72 reading (b)): the 44.7 % of CAVs whose B0 speed factor exceeds 1.0 therefore drive slower in B1 and in every commanding arm than in B0, a built-in delay increase and energy saving vs B0 that P0-R2 measures and every band table shows (B1 − B0); powertrains BEV 33.6 %, PHEV pair 66.4 % |
| HDV cars (B1 and commanding arms) | 71.45 % of cars | EIDM, archive driver H5 (every EIDM-specific parameter, error terms included, at the SUMO default; ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md H4 collapse) under CD0 and CD1; the Salles et al. 2020 sets under CD3; Krauss under CD4 ([AUTHOR A41]); petrol 35.7 %, diesel 24.3 %, PHEV pair 26.5 %, BEV 13.4 % |
| PHEV pair (HEV + PHEV) | — | `HBEFA4/PC_PHEV_petrol_Euro-6d_(P)` or `_(El)`, one mode per trip, 50 % electric (V46) |
| ICE and BEV classes | — | `HBEFA4/PC_petrol_Euro-6d`, `HBEFA4/PC_diesel_Euro-6d`, `HBEFA4/PC_BEV` (all load in 1.27.1; toy f_emission.py) |
| **Incident obstacle** (INC runs only) | 1 vehicle per INC run | vType `obstacle`: passenger class, length 5.0 m, minGap 2.5 m, the HDV-car car-following parameters, speed factor 1.0. **Not a cohort vehicle:** not in D, D_K or r, never an ego in any metric, but a **leader** in T. Lane-change mode 0 from insertion to the block end, **restored to 1621 at T_on + 900 s**; **removed** (`vehicle.remove`) at the first step its front is ≥ x_I + 200 m, so it can never strand at a lane end (v2 review SF9); its removal counts as its arrival in the conservation identities; no energy read (§2.4b) |

- **HDV car desired speeds** (V43, labelled assumptions): speed factor 1.0 (55 %), uniform in (1.0, 1.2] (44.7 %), uniform in (1.2, 1.5] (0.3 %) on the 100 km/h network. "Uniform within each range" is an ASSUMPTION (V43 gives only the ranges). `speedDev=0` in the HDV vTypes, because the factor is drawn per vehicle. Note: in the toy, EIDM HDVs with sigma = 0 still exceeded the lane maximum by up to 2.1 km/h, probably through EIDM's own error processes [VERIFY]; P0-G2(e) measures compliance.
- **B0** drives every car, CAV-flagged or not, as an HDV car (the adopted CD model) with its manifest speed factor. **B1** and every commanding arm drive the CAV-flagged cars with the chosen ACC.
- **Attribute manifest (common random numbers).** Route, desired departure, category, powertrain, emission class, PHEV mode, speed factor and CAV flag are drawn once per seed, before any arm is known, from NumPy generators keyed by (seed, stream): one stream per route for arrivals and one per attribute, so that changing one route's rate no longer shifts every later route's arrivals (port_map plant trap 3). They are written per vehicle in the route file. vTypes are assigned per vehicle by id; `vTypeDistribution` is not used, because SUMO would draw types from its own RNG (port_map mrg3 trap (b)); under CD3 the four Salles sets are assigned per vehicle from a manifest stream as well. CAVs are tracked by id, never by type id (ARCH STATUS #5, `av@<id>` rename). The per-vehicle speed factor read back from SUMO must equal the manifest (P0-B7) [VERIFY the per-vehicle attribute in 1.27.1; fallback: generated vTypes, because SUMO 1.27 rejects distribution strings for accel, ARCH STATUS.md:89]. The incident schedule (T_on, lane, x_I, Δ_ins) and the obstacle are deterministic manifest entries that draw no random number (P0-B4, B7).
- **Disclosed limit of common random numbers.** Inflow and attributes are identical across arms (T3b). SUMO's own random draws (EIDM driver noise, lane-change randomness, and the lane picked at insertion under `departLane="random"`) are not in the manifest; they diverge as soon as arms behave differently. This is unavoidable here and is why sample sizes rest on paired variances (§8.7).
- **Every vType attribute is set explicitly** (toy b_params.py: the ACC defaults read back include `speedDev` 0.1 and `HBEFA4/PC_petrol_Euro-4`; libsumo cannot read model gains back), including mass (the HBEFA4 fuel cut-off depends on it, literature_v0.md, and the P0-G4 acceleration floor uses it). The full vType XML and its hash go into each run record.

### 2.4 Inflow family and episode ([AUTHOR A12]; proposal with labelled assumptions)
- **Episode (V49).** Warm-up [0, 900) s at the base level q₀; scored window [900, 9,900) s; **inflow stops at 9,900 s** (the reading of V49 and `interfaces.md` §4/§5.1: the drain adds no new arrivals); drain until every cohort vehicle has arrived or t = 11,700 s (58,500 steps of 0.2 s). Cohort = vehicles whose desired departure lies in [900, 9,900) s. Control stays active in warm-up, window and drain (V8; `interfaces.md` §5.1). Edge effect, identical across arms: the last cohort vehicles drive on an emptying road.
- **Shape, per stream** (a triangle approximation of the fitted US-169 SB S460 morning peak, B12): q₀ = 0.5·q̂ during warm-up and at window start; linear rise to q̂ at **t_pk = 5,160 s** from episode start (71 min into the window); linear fall to **q₁ = 0.7·q̂** at 9,900 s. Anchors (port_map m_day verifier, upheld): within 06:00–10:00 the fitted curve starts at 0.47 of its AM peak, peaks at 07:54, and is still at 0.70 at 10:00. Mapping 06:00–10:00 linearly onto the 2.5-h window puts the peak 114/240 × 9,000 s ≈ 4,275 s into the window, rounded to 4,260 s (my arithmetic). Rise 71 min, fall 79 min ("rise faster than fall", `interfaces.md` §4). The fitted curve is not a triangle (its plateau term holds about 0.65 all day): the triangle is a labelled simplification. Mapping the 4-h morning onto the 2.5-h window makes the rise and the fall 1.6 times faster than on the real road (240 / 150 min; my arithmetic), which changes queue sizes at a given λ; disclosed in [AUTHOR A12].
- **Level.** q̂ = λ × (4,000; 1,200; 1,000) veh/h for (mainline; OR1; OR2) (B13 draft input).
- **Per-episode noise: off in P0–P2** (Poisson arrivals only). The archive magnitudes are invented (port_map trap 9; m_day trap 1), and noise on the peak level would blur what "level λ" means for the band and inflate the paired variance. Noise enters later as a pre-registered robustness row (§12).
- **Arrivals.** Non-homogeneous Poisson per route; explicit vehicles snapped to the 0.2-s grid; exact due(t) counting with depart < t; no per-second or per-route cap. v5 capped every route at 3,600 veh/h and B0 never broke down (ARCH notes_networks.md V1); a SUMO flow probability is capped at one vehicle per second per flow (toy b_mixed.py).
- **Expected size at λ = 1.0** (my arithmetic): about 13,200 vehicles per episode, 12,440 of them in the cohort.
- **Plateau probe (P0-G1 and calibration only).** Warm-up 900 s at 0.5·q̂_a; rise over 900 s to q̂_a; plateau [1,800, 5,400) s; fall over 900 s to 0.5·q̂_a; inflow stops at 6,300 s; drain ≤ 1,800 s (end ≤ 8,100 s). Same fleet and ramp ratios (OR1 = 0.30, OR2 = 0.25 of the mainline). It exists because a triangle confounds any within-run pre/post drop estimate by construction (ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md Addendum A; STATUS #13: every smoke breakdown began when the peak arrived, and the ratios came out at 1.2–1.5).

### 2.4b Incident scenario INC (V75; [AUTHOR A43, A44], with the recommendations as the primary design)
The literature anchors below were read by the incident evidence package as stated (Appendix A); Knoop, Hoogendoorn & Adams 2009 was spot-checked by the rigor drafter (local text `inc_src/knoop2009/paper.txt`, line 582: "Mean 0.72 0.36 0.18 0.69"). An INC run is a no-incident run of the same seed, level and arm plus the obstacle below; everything else is unchanged.

| Item | Specification | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Model | A dedicated obstacle vehicle (§2.3) is inserted by TraCI (`vehicle.add`) on **lane 0** at x = 12,400 m, with `departSpeed="avg"`, at t = T_on − Δ_ins. At insertion it gets `setLaneChangeMode(obstacle, 0)` and `setStop(edge, position of x_I = 13,000 m, laneIndex = 0, duration = 0.0, until = T_on + 900 s)`. At T_on + 900 s its mode is restored to 1621 and it drives off; it is removed (`vehicle.remove`) at the first step its front is ≥ 13,200 m, so it never reaches the lane drop (under CD2 it would otherwise strand at the dead end with mode 0 and teleport). Δ_ins is fixed in P0-B16 on a free road (non-project seed) so that the free-road block start is T_on ± 1 s; desk value ≈ 25 s (18.5 s cruise + 6.2 s braking at 4.5 m/s², my arithmetic). It is written into the inflow manifest as a deterministic special vehicle (no random draw), so the conservation checks (P0-B4), RNG isolation (B7) and T3b include it | The mechanics and incident toys found the alternatives worse (evidence: mechanics, incident):<br>• a stop-based obstacle gave late merging (median 44–210 m before the obstacle; no field comparison was read, so whether this is realistic is [VERIFY]), no collisions and an exact end time;<br>• permission closures (`setDisallowed`, `closingLaneReroute`) made drivers leave far upstream (median 435–823 m), gave F = 0.62–0.81 (above the literature band) and caused a collision;<br>• a `setSpeed(0)` breakdown was teleported after 300 s in 9 of 9 runs;<br>• a converted stream vehicle has an arm-dependent identity, and the archive's 360-s stream-vehicle blockage on BN4 had teleports in 209 of 220 runs (§1.1 item 33).<br>`duration = 0` with `until` is required: a stop given only `until` never ended in 1.27.1 (its duration defaulted to a sentinel of about 9.2e15) |
| Block start and integrity | **t_blk** = the first step at which the obstacle's speed is < 0.1 m/s at x_I (the block start; written t_blk throughout so that it cannot be confused with t_b, a probe's sustained-onset minute, §3.2). **Integrity:** t_blk ∈ [T_on − 15, T_on + 15] s, lane 0, front at 13,000 ± 1 m, moving again at T_on + 900 ± 0.2 s, removed at ≥ 13,200 m. A run outside this is **invalid for its arm** (§2.7). The block end is fixed, so the block length varies by at most 30 s of 900 (3.3 %, my arithmetic). Arm dependence of t_blk is measured and gated (P0-GI(d), T0g) | The obstacle's 600-m approach lies downstream of Z2 and is never commanded, but surrounding traffic can delay its insertion or its approach. In the incident toy (lane 0, inserted 600 m upstream 40 s before onset, no rubbernecking, 18 runs at 3,000–5,100 veh/h), the block start lay 10.4 s before to 2.6 s after the onset; on lane 2 at 5,100 veh/h it lay up to 34.4 s after (this synthesis's read of `inc_toy/runs/*/result.json`). An inserted obstacle at departSpeed 0 waited 0.4–20.6 s for an insertion gap (evidence: mechanics). ±15 s is an ASSUMPTION covering the lane-0 toy range with a margin |
| Fallback insertion (pre-registered; used only after a T0g FAIL) | The obstacle is inserted already stopped at x_I on lane 0 at exactly T_on, with SUMO's insertion checks relaxed (the vehicle attribute `insertionChecks` [VERIFY its name, values and behaviour in 1.27.1 in a B16 case before any use]). The first 60 s after T_on are reported as an "incident onset" row (hard braking, TTC and emergency-braking warnings near x_I). If the fallback fails B16 or T0g, the INC regime is dropped (disclosed) | It makes the start arm-independent at the price of a disclosed instant appearance; it gives T0g a defined route instead of a dead end |
| Lane | **Lane 0** (the nearside lane, next to the ramps; on the mirrored M50, the lane next to the hard shoulder), the same on every seed and in every arm ([AUTHOR A43] (ii)). The middle lane is excluded and the exclusion disclosed; lane 2 is not a claim scenario | In the incident toy a stopped vehicle in the middle lane made the flow past it collapse (median 270 veh/h) with teleports in 12 of 12 runs, a SUMO/LC2013 artefact. One fixed lane gives one regime with one paired-difference distribution; balancing lanes 0 and 2 by seed parity would make every paired difference a 50/50 mixture of two sub-regimes with an unstable median (judge finding, Appendix D). Lane 0 feeds the zipper's combining lanes (0, 1 → 0), so the incident queue and the recurrent jam share lanes. If CD2 (lane 0 of plain 2 dead-ends at 15,000 m) is adopted, the blocked lane is the ending lane, and the lane choice is re-opened before seed 010 by a dated addendum (A43 (ii)) |
| Onset, duration | **T_on = 3,000 s**, which is 35 min into the scored window and 36 min before the inflow peak at 5,160 s; **900 s** (V75), so the blockage clears at 3,900 s | Desk (evidence: incident, `inc_desk/desk.py`; checked): the inflow at 13 km at T_on is 3,700λ veh/h (2,590 / 2,775 / 2,960 / 3,330 at λ = 0.70 / 0.75 / 0.80 / 0.90), above the toy incident capacity of 2,100–2,760 veh/h from λ ≈ 0.75 up, so a queue forms at once at λ_c. Clearance at 3,900 s precedes the desk onset of the recurrent drop jam at λ ≤ 0.9 (3,985 s at λ = 0.9; 4,846 s at 0.8). **Disclosed:** 15 min is short. The HCM 7th default for one closed lane has a mean of 34.6 min and a minimum of 16 min ([VERIFY], via the TxDOT manual); the Dutch median over all incidents is 36.8 min (Knoop 2009 thesis, §6.7.2, read by the incident package). 15 min matches the M50 incident-response arrival target (TII page, read by the incident package), which is not a clearance time |
| Levels | **I1 = λ_c − 0.10, I2 = λ_c, I3 = λ_c + 0.10**, all points of the §6.2 grid, so every INC run pairs with a no-incident run on the same seed and level. **I1 rule (C10):** if I1 fails the calibration screen only on binding, I1 := λ_c − 0.05 and it is re-screened once; otherwise it is dropped (disclosed) | Grid points give incident − no-incident contrasts by common random numbers at no design cost. I1 ≈ "incident-only congestion"; I2 ≈ "the incident triggers the recurrent jam"; I3 ≈ "the incident overlaps the recurrent jam". V75 asks for 2–3 levels |
| Role in P2 | per [AUTHOR A44] (b): **I2 is a tuned scenario point** (stage 1, stage 2, held-out; in the band family); I1 and I3 are held-out map rows with O(I2) and F. **A regime cannot rescue a KILL** (principle 15). The INC points that passed P0-GI enter the D5.8 regime set at GO (V75) | CPU (§10.2); the incident is tested with tuned rules at the claim level of the regime |
| Cohort and window | **cohort_I** = cohort vehicles whose route passes x_I and whose desired departure + r_13(route) lies in **[T_on, T_on + 3,600) s** (= [3,000, 6,600) s at T_on = 3,000 s), where r_13 is the reference time from the route origin to 13,000 m (C6). **S and T** at INC points use cohort_I with the §3.1 definitions. **The guards (e), (f), (f′), (f2) and (g) are evaluated twice, on cohort_I and on the full cohort, and both must pass**, and a **full-cohort non-inferiority row** applies: full-cohort S and T not worse than the reference by more than 5 % (point form, as A27) ([AUTHOR A55]; v2 review MV-1, BDP2-5). Reported: the full cohort's S and T; the blockage [T_on, T_on + 900) and recovery [T_on + 900, T_on + 3,600) sub-windows; the strips [10,000, 13,000) and [13,000, 15,500) m; the subgroups "X1- or X2-bound" (never reach x_I) and "route through x_I, reference arrival at 13 km ≥ T_on + 3,600 s". The incident − no-incident contrast uses cohort_I **in both runs** (it is defined from the manifest, so it exists in the no-incident twin) | Arm-independent (manifest + reference times; principle 13). The window covers the blockage, the release of the stored vehicles and the triggered drop jam (45 min after clearance). Route-aware, unlike a desired-departure window, which would include X1/X2-bound vehicles and ignore the OR2 origin. **Why the full-cohort guards:** vehicles that never pass x_I (X1/X2-bound, e.g. slowed in Z1 by a policy that meters flow into the blockage) and vehicles arriving after T_on + 3,600 s (inheriting a stored queue) are outside cohort_I, so without them a policy could buy cohort_I gains by moving harm onto them in the regime that may carry a D5.8 conditional advantage (the review's failure scenario is a construction, not a run). Re-scoring only, no new runs |
| Teleports | **V6 strict:** any teleport makes the run invalid for its arm. Level admissibility ≤ 5 % (§8.8). **Teleport margin ([AUTHOR A64]):** every INC run records the maximum waiting time of the vehicles queued behind the obstacle and the count above 200 s; C10 drops a point with more than 1 of 8 runs whose maximum wait exceeds 240 s (ASSUMPTION); per arm, the share of INC runs invalidated by teleports and its paired difference vs B0 are reported | An exemption for vehicles trapped behind the obstacle would shorten their trips and so create a gaming route; the incident toy had teleports in 2 of 24 outer-lane runs (evidence: incident). In the lane-0 stop runs of that toy (no rubbernecking) the maximum wait on the blocked lane was 248, 291 and 299 s in 3 of 12 runs at 4,200–5,100 veh/h, against the 300-s timer, and 83–156 s at 3,000–3,600 veh/h (v2 review SF5; re-read in this revision from `inc_toy/summary.json`); I3's flow lies near that range, I2's below it. So a level can be lost to a SUMO timer, and an arm's effect on gaps in lane 1 can turn the timer into that arm's failure |
| Information | A V2X hazard message (blocked lane index, x_I, age) is available from **t_blk + 60 s** to every arm whose declared observation spec includes the `lane` spec (§6.3; every rule arm, and later every learning arm that issues lane commands) | 60 s is an ASSUMPTION: no source on detection delay was read; under V27 no loop detector may serve as the detection model. Without a message no arm can sense the blockage before its queue is within V2V range (300 m, V50) of a Z2 CAV, because Z2 ends 1 km upstream of x_I |
| Rubbernecking | **None, and no contingency** ([AUTHOR A43] (viii)). CAVs are assumed not to rubberneck (ASSUMPTION; no source on automated-vehicle behaviour at incidents). Rubbernecking on an opposite carriageway is not modelled (one direction) | The toy obstacle gave F = 0.37–0.48 without any rubbernecking layer, inside the literature band; HDV tau 2.0 s near the obstacle overshot to F = 0.26–0.32 (evidence: incident). A tau step chosen to bring F into its band would fit a driver parameter to a realism band, which principle 10 forbids |
| Training (later) | DRL training seeds (7,410,000+) randomise incident on/off, lane ∈ {0, 2}, T_on ∈ [2,400, 4,200] s and x_I ∈ [12,500, 13,500] m. This is a training distribution, never a claim | evidence: incident |

### 2.5 Execution layer as tested (V26, V36, V47, V76; `interfaces.md` §3 except the hold, the entry ramp and the lane part, §1.1 items 1 and 27)

**Speed part** (unchanged from v1 except the call count):
- **Zone entry:** at the first 2-s boundary after a CAV's front enters a zone, target = its current speed rounded to the nearest 5 km/h, clipped to [50, 100] km/h. The target is always reached with `slowDown(id, target, 2.0)` from the current speed, never with a `setSpeed` step (a `setSpeed` from 97.4 to 95 km/h brakes at −3.333 m/s² for one 0.2-s step, a V36 hard brake manufactured by `exec`; scratchpad review_phys/hold_recover.py). If the arm also commands u = ±1 at that boundary, the same single call ramps from the current speed to the rounded target ± 5 km/h: at most 7.5 km/h over 2 s, i.e. ≤ **1.04 m/s²** (my arithmetic; the only ramp allowed to exceed 0.694 m/s², T0a).
- **At each later 2-s boundary**, u ∈ {−1, 0, +1}: target ← clip(target + 5u, 50, 100). If the target changed: `slowDown(id, target, 2.0)`. If it is unchanged and the previous ramp has ended: `setSpeed(id, target)` (hold, V36). **Exactly one speed call per CAV per boundary** (and at most one lane call, below), only from `exec` (toy A2b: re-issuing `slowDown` every step never reaches the target).
- **Zone exit:** release per [AUTHOR A10], decided (a): `setSpeed(id, −1)` at the first boundary after exit (V36, `interfaces.md` §3, §9), after the lane-request cancel and mode restore below; the ACC then accelerates at its own 2.6 m/s² towards its desired speed (toy A3, A6), which is the ACC's own behaviour, not a command. Z1 ends where merge 2 starts, so CAVs leaving Z1 are released at the first boundary after 9,000 m, at the start of the merge-2 weave.
- **Lane changes** stay enabled under every command (LC2013, §2.2); a CAV changing lanes during a ramp or a hold keeps its target (T0a tests it).
- **Speed mode 31** (default): car-following safety overrides every target (toy L1/L2: a CAV commanded to 90 km/h follows a 60 km/h leader with a 21.5 m minimum gap, no collision).
- **Commanded stops are impossible** by construction (v_min = 50 km/h), so a hold cannot manufacture a standstill longer than the 300-s teleport time (ARCH STATUS #18: 360-s blockages teleported in 209 of 220 runs).
- **No large target jumps** come from `exec`; T0a gates on 0 exec-induced steps below −3 m/s².

**Lane part (V76; [AUTHOR A45])** (toy facts from evidence: mechanics, SUMO 1.27.1, non-project seeds):
- **Action** per CAV per 2-s boundary: (u, ℓ) with u ∈ {−1, 0, +1} and ℓ ∈ {keep, left, right}: 9 combinations. "left" = lane index + 1, "right" = index − 1 (§1.1 item 32).
- **keep = no lane call.** LC2013 stays free to change lanes on its own under the CAV's current mode, as in B1. An explicit hold (`changeLane(id, current, 2.0)` at every boundary) is not used: in the toy it suppressed a speed-gain change behind a slow vehicle (15.1 vs 27.8 m/s), and under strategic bits 1 it stranded a CAV at a lane end for 300 s until it was teleported.
- **left / right**, if not masked: at the CAV's **first lane request in the current zone**, `exec` first issues `setLaneChangeMode(id, M_cmd)`; then `changeLaneRelative(id, ±1, 2.0)`. A request lasts exactly one decision interval, so requests never overlap; it is retried by SUMO each step while active and expires unexecuted if no safe gap opens in time (toy: a 10-s request executed at +2.8 s; a gap that opened after a 2-s window was not used). A request moves exactly one lane (toy).
- **Mask** (geometric only; identical code for every arm; in `exec`). A left/right action is replaced by keep, and logged, if: the target lane does not exist on the current edge (SUMO silently ignores invalid targets, e.g. right from lane 0 or an index beyond the edge, with no exception, so the mask is mandatory); the CAV is on an auxiliary lane; or its **predicted front position** x̂ = x + 2.0 s · max(v, v_tgt) (v = current speed, v_tgt = the speed target after this boundary's speed command) reaches the **ban segment [14,800, 15,000) m** or the zone's downstream end (9,000 m for Z1, 12,000 m for Z2, 14,800 m for Z3), i.e. x̂ ≥ that position. A request lives for 2.0 s and SUMO may execute it at any step in that time, at the vehicle's position then (SUMO 1.27.1: `Vehicle.cpp` sets a [now, now + duration] lane timeline that `MSVehicle::adaptLaneTimeLine` carries across edges, and lane changes run after the movement step; read by the review's SUMO lens, SF1), so a mask on the issue position alone would let a request issued 1–56 m before the boundary execute inside the ban or after the zone. The 200 m are an ASSUMPTION: the default zipper visibility of 100 m plus a margin; in the toy, commanded changes 13–23 m before the zipper node made the zipper partner brake at −3.2 and −4.5 m/s² under mode 1621. A rule's own conditions (e.g. LM-B's 60-s no-repeat) live in the arm, never in `exec`.
- **M_cmd = 1878** (recommended) = strategic 2 + cooperative 1 + speed gain 1 + keep right 1 + TraCI priority 3 + sublane 1 (bit layout from the TraCI docs and the `getLaneChangeMode` source, evidence: mechanics). A requested change executes only into a gap that is already safe, without asking neighbours to adapt their speed (priority 3); strategic changes override requests (strategic 2), so a commanded CAV bound for X2 or a lane end cannot strand (toy: under strategic bits 2 the CAV changed at the same time as with no command). Setting the mode only at a first lane request keeps a zone with speed-only commands bit-identical to the v1 speed-only `exec` (T0e(k)), so keep-only twins equal the speed-only arms. **TraCI priorities 0 and 1 are forbidden** (toy mode 1365: 93–94 % execution, 4–6 collisions per run).
- **Zone exit**, at the first boundary after the front leaves the zone, in this order: (1) `changeLane(id, current index, 0.0)` cancels a pending request (toy: with the cancel no change followed; without it a change came at +2.8 s); (2) `setLaneChangeMode(id, 1621)` if the mode was changed; (3) the speed release `setSpeed(id, −1)`. **No lane request outlasts the zone:** the predicted-position mask stops any request that could still be live at the zone end, and the cancel is a backstop. Whether CAVs evacuated in Z2 re-enter the empty blocked lane after 12,000 m is measured, not prevented (T0e(i), §6.12).
- **Calls per boundary:** at most **one speed call and at most one lane call** (plus the mode call at a first request) per CAV per boundary, all from `exec` only. This amends T0a(5) and `interfaces.md` §3 (§1.1 item 27).
- **Lane changes are instantaneous** (`--lanechange.duration 0`, [AUTHOR A51]); a changed lane counts as a new leader for T from the next step (§3.1).
- **Logging:** every lane call, mask and mode change goes into the command log, which enters the trace hash (T3).
- **Evidence for the mode choice** (mechanics stress test: random lane commands every 2 s for 30 % CAVs on a 3-km toy approach to a 3 → 2 zipper, 6,000 veh/h, seeds 1–3; this synthesis re-read `toy/out/t2_stress.jsonl` to label the rows):

  | Mode as run | keep | Executed within 2 s | Hard braking at speed (vehicle-steps; no commands 88–216) | Collisions (vehicles) | Own TET at 1.5 s (s per run) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1621 (strategic 1, TraCI priority 2, urgent) | no request | 70–72 % | 262–529 | 0–2 (one junction collision on the zipper internal lane in 3 runs) | 1.0–2.8 |
  | 1621 | explicit hold | 70–71 % | 258–366 | 0 | 1.0–3.8 |
  | 1622 (strategic 2, priority 2) | explicit hold | identical to the 1621-hold rows on all three seeds | | | |
  | 1877 (strategic 1, priority 3) | no request | 4–8 % | 67–135 | 0 | 0–1.0 |
  | 1878 (strategic 2, priority 3) | explicit hold | 4–8 % | 75–92 | 0 | 1.2–2.6 |
  | 1365 (priority 1) | no request | 93–94 % | 1,769–2,252 | 4–6 | 27–41 |

  At 3,000 veh/h, execution was 77–83 % (1621) against 33–43 % (1877); hard braking 103–269 (1621) and 93–109 (1877) against a no-command baseline of 93–151. **The recommended combination, 1878 with keep = no request, was not itself stress-tested** (it differs from the 1877 rows only in the strategic bits, and from the 1878 rows only in keep); T0e and T0f test it on F3Z. Random commands are a stress test, not a policy.

### 2.6 Calibration on the throw-away seeds 7,400,000–009 (disclosed)
Calibration fixes only the design quantities below, by rules written now (principle 10). **Nothing is calibrated on 7,400,010 or later.** A calibration report (§4.3) is committed before seed 7,400,010 is used. **C0′ runs first**, because the plant it adopts determines every other design quantity; C1–C8 then run once on the adopted plant (re-using C0′'s plateau runs as C2), followed by C9, C10 and C11.

| Id | Seeds | What is set | Rule (fixed now) |
|---|---|---|---|
| C0 smoke (P0-S) | 000–001 | nothing; golden B0 trace hash (seed 000, triangle λ = 1.0); CPU time | §4.2 |
| **C0′ capacity-drop ladder** (V70; [AUTHOR A41]) | 002–009 | the adopted HDV car model / zipper setting | **Order: CD0 (the v1 plant) → CD1 → CD3 → CD4 → CD2, sequential** (§2.2). For each setting: the B0 plateau ladder of C2 below (λ ∈ {0.60, 0.65, …, 0.95} × 8 seeds, with its extensions); BN* and λ_a by the C2 rule; then **Q_dis,cal** = the §3.2 Q_dis at BN* on the **pooled** plateau runs at λ_a − 0.05, λ_a and λ_a + 0.05 (24 runs; a screen-only pooling, because 8 runs at one level cannot hold ≥ 5 runs in each group), and **Q_ff,cal** = the §3.2 Q_ff on the no-onset runs of **the highest of these three levels that has ≥ 3 runs without an active onset** at BN* (none → not computable → not qualifying) ([AUTHOR A63]; v2 review SF4). ρ_cal = Q_dis,cal / Q_ff,cal. Reason: no-onset runs carry their level's inflow (2,640λ veh/h/lane at BN-LD; adjacent levels differ by 132 veh/h/lane, 6.7 % at λ_a = 0.75, my arithmetic), and with λ_a near an onset share of 0.5 they come mostly from the lowest pooled level, so a pooled Q_ff would pull ρ_cal towards 1 and could reject a setting with a real drop of a few percent. Reported next to it: the fully pooled ρ (the earlier draft's estimator) and the within-run plateau ratio (pre-onset plateau flow vs Q_dis in the same run). **Screen PASS (all):** BN* = BN-LD; Q_ff,cal computable as above, and ≥ 5 pooled runs valid for Q_dis (else not computable → not qualifying); ρ_cal ∈ [0.82, 0.97]; Q_dis,cal ∈ [1,600, 2,300] veh/h/lane; 0 collisions, 0 teleports and 0 health FAIL over that setting's ladder runs at λ ≤ λ_a + 0.1; **G3 computability ([AUTHOR A65]):** ≥ 20 at-speed severe events (A28 definition) of the CAV-flagged vehicles, driven as HDVs, pooled over that setting's plateau runs at λ_a and λ_a + 0.05 (16 runs; ASSUMPTION), so that G3's yardstick (the same vehicles in B0) is not degenerate; the B0 severe-event rate per 1,000 km is reported for every screened setting. **The first setting that passes is adopted, and the ladder stops.** No sweep and no tuning inside a setting. **If none passes:** CD0 is adopted; under [AUTHOR A42] (b) G1(b) is report-only; under (a), V-2 (§9.2) is screened next in the same way and adopted if it passes; if it also fails, futility stop **F7**. Every screened setting is listed with every value in the calibration report and counted in k_cal (§9.2) |
| C1 triangle ladder | 002–009 | λ_c, λ_sub | B0 triangle at λ ∈ {0.6, 0.7, …, 1.2} × 8 seeds. **λ_c** = the level at which the share of runs with an active sustained onset at any bottleneck (§3.2) reaches 0.5, linearly interpolated between ladder points, rounded to 0.01. **Downward extension, once:** if the share at 0.6 is already ≥ 0.5, B0 is also run at {0.4, 0.5} on the same seeds. If the share is still ≥ 0.5 at 0.4, λ_c is undefined: futility stop **F5**. **λ_sub** = the highest ladder level with no sustained onset in any of its 8 runs; if none qualifies, λ_c − 0.2 |
| C2 plateau ladder | 002–009 (from C0′) | BN*, λ_a | B0 plateau probe at λ ∈ {0.60, 0.65, …, 0.95} × 8 seeds. **BN*** = the bottleneck with the most active sustained onsets pooled over the ladder. **λ_a** = among levels with 0 teleports and 0 health FAIL, the level whose BN* onset share is closest to 0.5; ties go to the lower level (archive cell rule, ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md:42-48). Extensions, at most one: if the maximum share is < 0.2, extend to {1.00, 1.10, 1.20}; if the share at 0.60 is > 0.8, extend to {0.50, 0.55}. The adopted setting's C0′ runs are its C2 runs |
| C3 insertion | C1/C2 runs | `departLane` / `departSpeed` | the first configuration of the §2.2 order that meets P0-G2(d) on the calibration runs |
| C4 ACC ladder | non-project seed 1, then 002–009 | ACC candidate ([AUTHOR A11]) | **Pre-screen (deterministic, non-project seed 1, before any calibration seed is spent on the ladder):** (i) the platoon test (one lane, 10 followers, leader 100 → 60 km/h at −3 m/s²: peak deceleration of follower 10 ≤ the leader's; toy b_string.py: the defaults attenuate, 2.90 → 1.19 m/s²); (ii) the **approach test**: a lone CAV at 100 km/h approaching, from 300 m, a leader at 60 km/h and a stopped leader: peak deceleration ≤ 4.5 m/s² and 0 steps beyond the vType decel + 0.01 m/s² (ASSUMPTION; the HDV car's peak in the same scenario is reported). A candidate failing the pre-screen is dropped (logged). **Calibration:** B1 for each surviving candidate in order, paired with B0, at **λ_c and λ_c + 0.1** (the G3 levels, §4.6; both below the plant-invalid line by construction); B0 at these two levels is run once on 002–009 (16 runs). R is the G3(b) statistic (pooled rate ratio of severe events, definition [AUTHOR A28]), computed only over seeds where B0 is valid with 0 health FAIL; the pooled B0 event count per level is reported with it (if CD3 or CD4 was adopted, the G3 reference has changed with the HDV model, [AUTHOR A41], A65). **Selection:** the first candidate with G3(a) met and R ≤ **0.85** at both levels (a margin, ASSUMPTION, so that the fresh-seed check is not a coin flip); if none reaches 0.85, the candidate with the lowest max-over-levels R among those with R ≤ 1.25 at both levels goes to G3, flagged. Candidates in order: **A0** SUMO ACC defaults (explicit); **A1** A0 with tau 1.2 s; **A2** A0 with decel 3.5 m/s²; **A3** an IDM-based ACC without driver noise (Kesting et al. 2008 [VERIFY]; departs from "SUMO ACC" in `system_context.md` §3; allowed by V60). Excluded a priori: SUMO CACC (uncommanded CACC at 25 % made delay +46 to +93 % worse, ARCH round4_th.md); the ACC "original-model" gain set (toy: 4,552 collision log lines at 100 % ACC); tau 1.5 s (toy: 6.03 vs about 1.5 hard brakes per 1,000 vehicle-steps at 100 % ACC) |
| C5 reference times | 002–009 | r(route, cat) | B0 triangle at λ = 0.05 on all 8 calibration seeds; median trip time (insertion to arrival) per route and cat ∈ {car, truck} [AUTHOR A8], over all 8 runs pooled. **Minimum count:** ≥ 20 vehicles per (route, cat) cell (ASSUMPTION). **Fallback** for a sparse cell: the category's median free-flow pace (s/km, pooled over all routes of that category) × the route length; the cells that use it are recorded. **Sanity row:** r vs route length ÷ the category's desired speed (100 or 90 km/h), FLAG outside ±10 % (ASSUMPTION). Stored with the network hash and its own hash (T3c); **identical for every arm**. (Expected sparse cell, my arithmetic: OR1 → X2 trucks ≈ 0.96 per seed at λ = 0.05) |
| C6 travel-time offsets | from C5 | tt_k, r_13 | reference travel time from the mainline origin to each measurement section (§3.2); **r_13(route, cat)** = the reference time from the route origin to x = 13,000 m (for cohort_I, §2.4b), from the same runs and rules |
| C7 T3-early | 3 runs of C1 | — | each re-run in a fresh process; bitwise-identical per-step trace hash, else STOP (protects pairing before any check seed is spent) |
| C8 CPU-time caps | all | per-run CPU-time cap per arm family | median **process CPU time** per episode (SIGSTOP intervals excluded by construction), measured at production concurrency in the C1/C2 batches; cap = 4 × that median at the nearest level, applied from seed 010 on. During P0-S and P0-C a provisional cap of 8 × the P0-S median CPU time applies (ASSUMPTION: generous, because the smoke runs at low concurrency and low levels); a calibration run over it is a collapse for F3. Commanding arms: B1's cap × their CPU overhead ratio over B1, measured in the P1-B (R4, R-LANE) and P2-B (R1–R3 with lane modules) timing tests (§5.0, §6.3). A run over its cap is a **collapse**: killed and counted as a FAIL of its arm on that seed, never excluded (§2.7) |
| **C9 co-primary floors and informativeness** ([AUTHOR A37, A53]) | C1 runs (no new runs) | S_floor, T_floor; the TTC threshold TTC\* in force | **S_floor** = median S of B0 at λ_sub; **T_floor** = median T of B0 at λ_sub (8 runs each). **Pre-check** on the C1 B0 runs at the ladder level nearest λ_c + 0.1: (i) median S ≥ **1.5 × S_floor**; (ii) T > 0 in ≥ **7 of 8** runs, **and** median T ≥ **3 × T_floor**, **and** a median of ≥ **30 conflict episodes** per run (§3.1). Under [AUTHOR A37] (a): if (ii) fails at TTC\* = 1.5 s **at that level or at the highest C1 ladder level ≤ 1.0 with no invalid B0 run** (the levels where ℓ* is likely; [AUTHOR A59] (b)), **TTC\* := 3.0 s** for T everywhere (one-way; recorded in the calibration report before seed 010; the 1.5-s values are reported as "severe", with the FLAG of §3.1), and (ii) is re-checked at 3.0 s at the level nearest λ_c + 0.1. Any remaining failure of (i) or (ii) is futility stop **F6**. A metric definition, not a plant setting: not counted in k. Reported: the episode-duration CV c_d at each checked level, so that T's compound noise (CV ≈ √((1 + c_d²)/N) per run) can be stated (§4.11) |
| **C10 incident** ([AUTHOR A43]) | 002–009 | which INC points go to check seeds; the I1 move | B0 with INC at I1, I2 and I3 (24 runs; + 8 if I1 moves). Per point: F_inc,cal (§3.2, with q_lane = Q_dis,cal(BN-LD) per lane of the adopted plant from C0′), binding share, B0 teleport runs, block-start offsets t_blk − T_on, and the maximum waiting time behind the obstacle per run. **Screen PASS per point:** median F_inc,cal ∈ **[0.30, 0.55]**; the incident binds in ≥ **7 of 8** runs; ≤ **1 of 8** runs with a teleport; t_blk within ±15 s in ≥ **7 of 8** runs; **≤ 1 of 8 runs with a maximum wait behind the obstacle > 240 s** ([AUTHOR A64]; ASSUMPTION: a 60-s margin to the 300-s timer, so that knife-edge points are dropped before check seeds). **I1 rule** as in §2.4b. A point that fails is not run on check seeds (disclosed). If I2 fails, the tuned INC point of §6.2 is the passing point nearest λ_c (ties to the higher). **If every point fails, the INC regime is dropped before any check seed** (disclosed; V75 not achievable on this plant). No rubbernecking or driver-parameter step is ever used to pass this screen (principle 10) |
| **C11 SSM cross-check** (report-only) | 002–009 | none (a measurement check) | B0 and B1 at the C1 ladder level nearest λ_c + 0.1 with the SSM device (§2.2) (16 runs), plus B0 and B1 with INC at I2 on seed 002 (2 runs): own TET at 3.0 s vs SSM TET (ego-as-follower records, i.e. types {2, 7, 11} filtered offline, §2.2; SSM times shifted by +1 step, the mechanics finding). Reported: the relative difference inside and outside the zipper zone [14,940, 15,060) m; the share of SSM conflicts below 3.0 s of types 7 (merging, ego follows) or {4, 8} (adjacent lanes), the conflicts that own same-lane TTC cannot see before a lane change. C11 sees only B0 and B1, which issue no lane commands; the commanding-arm counterpart is the T0f SSM rows (§5). **FLAG** if the difference exceeds ±10 % outside the zipper zone (ASSUMPTION; the toy agreed within about 4 %). A FLAG triggers a code review before seed 010, never a metric change |

**Any later change of insertion configuration, step or HDV model re-runs C1–C11 except C0′ on 002–009 before its fresh-seed re-check** ([AUTHOR A16d]); a new ACC candidate re-runs only C4. C0′ is never re-run from the start: after a check-seed FAIL, the ladder continues from the adopted setting's successor (§9.2).

**Calibration futility stops ([AUTHOR A4]; STOP direction only, disclosed).** If any holds, no check seed is spent; F1–F3 lead to the plant FAIL path (§9.2):
- **F1** no sustained onset at any λ ≤ 1.2 in either ladder (no breakdown to measure);
- **F2** in every calibration run with an active onset at BN*, the per-run discharge at BN*'s section (§3.2) is < 90 % of the discharge floor ([AUTHOR A25]; 1,440 veh/h/lane under the floor of 1,600; ASSUMPTION: a margin wide enough that the check seeds could not plausibly reach the floor);
- **F3** a ladder level ≤ λ_c + 0.1 COLLAPSED: in at least half its runs, a run exceeds the CPU-time cap, or B0 is not drained at the cap (archive H4 precedent: discharge 185 veh/h/lane, runs about 12 times longer; ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md:127-135). Non-drain at higher levels is over-saturation, not collapse: it is reported (P0-R6) and never a stop. F3 is also the H4 guard for the error terms of CD3 and CD4 (§1.1 item 34);
- **F4** every ACC candidate failed the pre-screen, or every surviving candidate has calibration R > 1.25 at some G3 level or fails G3(a): P0-G3 is not run on check seeds, and [AUTHOR A11] applies: P0 KILL, or a new CAV base model under a new protocol. The plant-variant ladder (§9.2) does not apply to F4, because it changes the HDV plant, not the CAV model;
- **F5** λ_c undefined (onset share ≥ 0.5 already at λ = 0.4): the plant breaks down far below the nominal inflow; STOP (a re-designed plant or inflow needs a new protocol; [AUTHOR A26] is superseded by V58/V64). Not a realism FAIL and not a variant;
- **F6** (new) S or T fails the C9 pre-check after the [AUTHOR A37] fork: **STOP** before any check seed. The co-primary cannot carry a claim on this plant; principle 14 forbids repairing it with a plant change; a new metric needs a new protocol ([AUTHOR A53]);
- **F7** (new; only under [AUTHOR A42] (a)) no setting of the C0′ ladder and not V-2 passes the C0′ screen: **P0 KILL** without spending a check seed, because G1(b) could not pass ("no SUMO setting tried yields a capacity drop in the field band on F3Z", with k_cal stated).

**Incident screen outcome** (C10) is not a futility stop of P0: it only removes INC points or the regime.

### 2.7 Health layer: every known artefact has a detector
Ported from ARCH vsl_lab/sim/runner.py:7-17,179-263, with the gaps between the runner and the roadmap closed (runner H-R2 and H-R5 only WARNed; H-R8 existed only for BN4; no golden hash existed). "FAIL" = the run is invalid for its arm ([AUTHOR A19]); "FLAG" = recorded and used by a named rule; "OPS" = an operations failure, re-run once on the same seed.

| Artefact (archive or toy evidence) | Detector | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Teleport (V6; H-R3) | SUMO teleport count | FAIL (in INC runs too: V6 strict, §2.4b) |
| Collision (V6; runner trap 5) | statistic-output pairs, cross-checked against distinct pairs; junctions checked; mingap-factor 0 | > 2 in a run: FAIL; every one counted (gates in §4) |
| SUMO error, assert, traceback (H-E4, H-R7; STATUS #11 quiet stderr) | log scan; non-zero exit | FAIL. A crash is re-run once on the same seed; a second crash stays FAIL (`interfaces.md` §9) |
| Loaded ≠ generated (H-S2) | counters vs manifest (the obstacle included as a manifest vehicle) | FAIL |
| Conservation at the end, per route (H-E2 adapted to censoring) | generated = arrived + running + pending at the end | FAIL |
| Conservation every step (H-R1; STATUS #3 off-by-one) | generated = inserted + pending; inserted = arrived + running (+ teleported) | FAIL; unit-tested in P0-B4 |
| Statistic-output mismatch (H-E3) | SUMO statistics vs own counters | FAIL |
| Insertion backlog on a free entry (H-R2, tightened from WARN) | departDelay of vehicles inserted while the first 500 m of their entry edge has a 1-min speed ≥ 70 km/h | any > 60 s: FAIL; median > 1 s: FLAG (P0-G2(d)) |
| Stranded vehicle at a lane end (H-R5 tightened; STATUS #15) | speed < 0.1 m/s within 50 m of the zipper or an auxiliary-lane end (CAVs under lane commands included) | > 120 s continuously: FAIL; > 60 s: FLAG |
| Severe, at-cap and extreme deceleration (STATUS #4, #14; toy b_mixed.py; review_phys/knife.py) | per-step acceleration from the trace; definitions by [AUTHOR A28] (decided: **severe** = contiguous stretch with a < −(4.5 + 0.01) m/s², i.e. strictly beyond 4.5 m/s², the decel of the HDV car and of ACC candidates A0 and A1 (A2 uses 3.5; A3's value is stated in its vType), with speed at onset ≥ 10 m/s; **at-cap** = \|a + decel_vType\| ≤ 0.01 m/s²; **extreme** = a ≤ −8.9 m/s² at v ≥ 10 m/s). Every event stores its onset speed and location; events within 150 m upstream of the zipper node or an auxiliary-lane end are attributed "lane end"; in INC runs, events within 300 m upstream of x_I during the blockage are attributed "incident" | counted per km of the relevant vehicles (P0-G3, P0-R7, §6.12). The threshold must differ by ≥ 0.01 m/s² from every decel in the vType manifest (P0-B3). SUMO emergency-braking warnings are a diagnostic only |
| Zipper metronome (ARCH report_S1:57-59) | per-lane 10-s counts at the BN-LD section (15,100 m) during active discharge minutes; CV of lane 0's counts | FLAG if CV < 0.2 (ASSUMPTION; a Poisson stream at 1,600 veh/h has CV ≈ 0.47 over 10 s, my arithmetic) or if lane 0's discharge is below the floor while the section mean is in band (P0-R9). When the second FLAG fires, the limitation sentence discloses it ([AUTHOR A62]) |
| NaN or ±∞ energy | metrics check | FAIL (`interfaces.md` §9) |
| Not drained at the cap (H-E1) | cohort vehicles not arrived at 11,700 s | FLAG; censored count per arm reported and guarded (§6.8(g)) |
| Queue reaching the network entry | 1-min speed < 60 km/h at the 250 m probe; origin-queue vehicle-hours | FLAG; reported and guarded (§6.8(g)) |
| Plant collapse (ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md:127-135) | process CPU time > the C8 cap of its arm family | killed; FAIL for that arm on that seed, never excluded |
| Worker hang (`interfaces.md` §9) | CPU time grows by < 1 s over 10 min of wall time while the guard has not paused the job (the guard logs every SIGSTOP and SIGCONT) | OPS: killed and re-run once on the same seed, logged; a second hang is a FAIL of that arm and reported. A kill that overlaps a guard pause is OPS, never FAIL |
| Unknown or inert vType attribute (STATUS #10) | XML validation `always` + R-0 hash test | build FAIL (P0-B3) |
| Silent code change in the plant | golden B0 trace hash (seed 7,400,000, λ = 1.0) | an unexplained hash change: STOP |
| **Incident not delivered** (INC) | t_blk outside [T_on − 15, T_on + 15] s, wrong lane or position, block end not at T_on + 900 ± 0.2 s, obstacle teleported or removed during its stop, mode not restored at the block end, or not removed by 13,200 m | FAIL (invalid for its arm); rate per arm reported and gated (P0-GI(d), T0g). If only the reference failed, §8.4 applies |
| Vehicles behind the obstacle teleported (INC) | SUMO teleport count | FAIL under V6 (strict, [AUTHOR A43] (vi)) |
| Waiting time behind the obstacle (INC; [AUTHOR A64]) | maximum waiting time of the vehicles queued behind the obstacle; count above 200 s | diagnostic, reported per run and arm; feeds the C10 screen |
| Lane request to a non-existent lane, from an auxiliary lane, outside a zone, or with a predicted position x̂ = x + 2.0 · max(v, v_tgt) at or beyond the ban start or the zone end, reaching SUMO | `exec` call log vs geometry and the mask, **checked at the issue position and speed** | code FAIL (the mask must catch it; P1-B, T0e) |
| Commanded lane change inside the ban segment or after the zone end | trace: a lane change while x ∈ [14,800, 15,000), or after the zone's downstream end, within 2 s of a request | code FAIL |
| TTC overlap record | own gap g ≤ 0 with a leader returned by `getLeader` (e.g. zipper-partner or long-vehicle geometry) | not counted in T; counted and reported; FLAG if > 1 % of T's vehicle-steps |
| Masked lane action | `exec` log | logged and reported per arm; never a FAIL |

---

## 3 Metrics used per check

### 3.1 Claim metrics, constraints, secondary and reported metrics (`interfaces.md` §5, to be amended)
All are computed in `metrics/` from the per-step ground-truth trace, the inflow manifest and the command log, by one implementation for every arm, **online**: a vehicle's series is reduced when it leaves the relevant window, and production runs write no fcd (§2.2). So a sensitivity row is a re-scoring without new simulation **only if its sufficient statistic is in the storage schema below**; every pre-registered row is. A row outside the schema needs new runs on fresh seeds (v2 review MV-6). **K** is the scored vehicle set: the cohort at no-incident points, cohort_I at INC points (§2.4b).

**Per-vehicle storage schema** (one record per vehicle of the full cohort, so that cohort_I and the full-cohort rows are both re-scorable; P0-B10 recomputes every row from a full 0.2-s trace on a non-project seed and requires equality with the online values to 10⁻⁹ relative):

| Family | Stored per vehicle (online) |
|---|---|
| S | for each trend form (Gaussian σ_g = 40, 100, 200 s; boxcar 300 s; none) × window (W1, W2, W3, each strip): n and Σ residual²; Σv and Σv² in W (Stern form, mean speed); the 1-s acceleration-noise sum of squares; stop-and-go cycle counts at 20/50 and 30/60 km/h; time and planned km in W; the sample count used by the imputation rule |
| T | TET and TIT for each threshold {1.5, 3.0 s} × speed condition {≥ 10 m/s, all} × location tag {zipper zone, outside} × leader type {obstacle, other} × lane-change attribution {within 5 s after a CAV lane change, other}; conflict-episode counts and durations (for c_d); DRAC > 3.4 m/s² exposure; overlap-record count; the short-headway exposure (below) with its lane-change split; served km and unserved planned km at the cap |
| Braking, delay, energy | the event list (onset time, speed, location, category, attribution); τ_v, d_v, censoring flag; energy by source; per-edge traversal times |

**Shared definitions** (v1, with K and the INC additions)

| Symbol | Definition |
|---|---|
| Cohort | vehicles whose desired departure lies in the scored window [900, 9,900) s; all categories; the incident obstacle is never in it |
| K, cohort_I | K = the cohort (no-incident points) or cohort_I (INC points: route passes x_I and desired departure + r_13(route) ∈ [T_on, T_on + 3,600) s = [3,000, 6,600) s; §2.4b). At INC points the guards are also evaluated on the full cohort ([AUTHOR A55]) |
| τ_v | door-to-door time = arrival − desired departure (insertion queue included, from the manifest); censored at 11,700 s and the run flagged |
| r(route, cat) | median trip time (insertion to arrival) in B0 at λ = 0.05 on seeds 7,400,002–009 (C5, with its minimum count and fallback), cat ∈ {car, truck}; stored with the network hash and its own hash; **identical for every arm** [AUTHOR A8]. r_13(route, cat): the same to x = 13,000 m (C6) |
| d_v, Dl | delay d_v = τ_v − r; network delay Dl = Σ_{v∈K} d_v (veh·h). R_tot = Σ_v r_v (veh·h), the total reference time of K, used by the delay-guard floor (§6.8(e)) |
| D, D_K | fixed denominators: Σ over the cohort (D) or over K (D_K) of the planned route length (km); identical across arms on a seed |
| Validity | V6 + §2.7 FAIL rows [AUTHOR A19], including incident non-delivery; OPS failures are re-run, not scored |
| TTT | ∫ over [900, 11,700) s of the number of vehicles that have reached their desired departure time and not yet arrived, in the network **or in the origin queue** (veh·h); unfinished vehicles counted to the cap (CLAUDE.md; the archive's "TTS gaming via insertion blocking" trap, ARCH docs/lessons_metric_gameability.md §5) |
| Served throughput | cohort vehicles arrived by the cap, and vehicles per hour at the exits over the window; reported with a CI wherever S and T are |

**Co-primary S: smoothness, in m/s** ([AUTHOR A36], recommended form (a))

| Item | Definition |
|---|---|
| Window W (W1) | every position on vehicle i's own route **upstream of x = 15,500 m** (the drop + 500 m): mainline lanes, merge areas, auxiliary lanes and on-ramp edges. The two-lane exit beyond 15,500 m and the off-ramp edges after the diverges are excluded |
| Samples | v_ij = speed at every 0.2-s step j while i's front is in W, standstill included (time samples, so full stops keep full weight) |
| Trend | m_i(t_j) = a **centred Gaussian-weighted mean** of i's own samples in W, standard deviation **σ_g = 100 s**, truncated at ±300 s and renormalised at i's first and last sample in W ([AUTHOR A58], recommended (b); v2 review BDP2-1). Its high-pass keeps the share (1 − exp(−2π²σ_g²/P²))² of a sinusoid's variance, which falls steadily with the period P and is ½ at P ≈ 401 s, the half-power period of the 300-s boxcar used in the earlier draft (≈ 397 s). The boxcar was replaced because its response is not monotone: it keeps 1.47 × the variance at P = 200 s, 1.00 at 300 s, 0.66 at 360 s and 0.13 at 600 s, so at equal amplitude a change of period alone moves S (300 → 360 s: S × 0.81; 150 → 200 s: S × 1.21), where the Gaussian gives × 0.88 and × 0.99 (my arithmetic, `scratchpad/protocol_v2/review_fix/desk_review.py`). The Gaussian still depends on the period, so an S claim also needs the co-confirmation (b_S′) of §6.8. The 300-s boxcar is a reported row |
| Per vehicle | σ̃_i² = (1/n_i) Σ_j (v_ij − m_i(t_j))² |
| Weights | L_i = the **planned** km of i's route inside W (manifest), identical in every arm |
| S | **S = √( Σ_{i∈K} L_i σ̃_i² / Σ_{i∈K} L_i )** |
| Missing or truncated | a vehicle of K with **fewer than 150 samples in W** by the cap (never inserted, or inserted in the last 30 s) enters with σ̃_i² = **c_s, one arm-independent constant per seed: the 95th percentile of σ̃² over the B0 vehicles of K with ≥ 150 samples in W on seed s** (ASSUMPTION), the same for every arm on that seed (B0 included), and is counted. A vehicle still in W at the cap with ≥ 150 samples uses its samples so far (ASSUMPTION: 30 s; every complete route spends far longer in W). The earlier draft's "largest σ̃² of K in that run" depended on the arm (a smoother arm paid a smaller penalty) and was an extreme value (v2 review ST7, MV-8).
