Reported: the imputed L-share per arm; S without imputed vehicles; S with the imputation max(B0's run maximum, the arm's run maximum). A point whose band membership changes between these is labelled "imputation-dependent"; a point where B0's imputed L-share exceeds 2 % (ASSUMPTION) is FLAGged |
| Effect | r_S = median_s(S_A − S_B0) / median_s(S_B0) (§8.1 convention) |
| Sources | speed SD as the smoothness measure: Stern et al. 2018, TR-C 89:205-221, Sec. 4.1 (full text, evidence: metrics); speed variability as the CIRCLES metric family: Jang et al. 2025 (full text, Sec. 4.3, evidence: metrics) and Ameli et al. 2024 (**introduction only**, evidence: metrics); speed variation as a crash precursor: the Lee, Hellinga & Saccomanno **2003** crash-potential model **as described in** Allaby, Hellinga & Bullock 2007 (Allaby full text, evidence: metrics; the Lee et al. original not read [VERIFY]); Rahman & Abdel-Aty 2018 (abstract only [VERIFY]). **The high-pass is this protocol's choice**: §3.2's 5-min high-pass filters Eulerian 10-s probe series for a lag estimate and does not by itself justify a per-vehicle filter. The per-vehicle, fixed-weight form and the kernel are inferences (evidence: metrics, variance argument; rigor toy; the review toy below) |
| Why per vehicle and high-passed | The Stern pooled form adds between-vehicle and peak-trend variance on an open 4-h peak (evidence: metrics; in the rigor toy the Stern SD was 10.6 m/s against a raw within-vehicle SD of 7.8 m/s in the heavy queue). Without the high-pass, about half of a congested vehicle's within-vehicle variance is the contrast between free flow and queue speed, which wave damping cannot remove: in the rigor toy's heavy queue the 300-s boxcar high-pass keeps 48 % of it and a 120-s boxcar 26 % (rigor toy, seeds 1–3, not data; re-checked in `synth/desk_synth.py`). **Gaussian vs boxcar on the same toy** (a review copy of the rigor toy with the simulation part unchanged, `scratchpad/protocol_v2/review_fix/s_kernel_toy.py`, EIDM, 3 → 2 zipper, non-project seeds 1–3, not F3Z, not data; this revision): heavy-queue share kept 0.43–0.45 (σ_g = 100 s) vs 0.48–0.49 (boxcar); S in the light and heavy queue 2.93 × and 5.22 × the free-flow value (boxcar 2.92 ×, 5.27 ×); the 70 km/h stand-in −7.8 / −10.3 / −10.6 % (boxcar −8.2 / −11.1 / −11.0 %; S_raw −5.6 / −6.6 / −7.5 %); the 50 km/h crawl −30.4 to −32.1 % at +58 to +69 % travel time (boxcar −31.0 to −32.7 %). So the kernel change leaves the toy evidence behind G6's factors and the gameability rows essentially unchanged. A per-vehicle dominant-period estimator tried in the same toy was dominated by the length of each vehicle's series (600–900 s in the toy window) and is **not** used. Fixed planned-km weights stop crawling from diluting the average with extra samples. Computation: each vehicle's series in W is held until it leaves W, then reduced (about 2,000–3,000 vehicles in W at once × up to about 3,000 samples, float32: tens of MB per process; my arithmetic) |

**Co-primary T: safety, in s per 1,000 planned vehicle-km** ([AUTHOR A37], recommended (a); [AUTHOR A52])

| Item | Definition |
|---|---|
| Leader and gap | each step, for every vehicle i of K from insertion to arrival or the cap: (L, d) = `getLeader(i, 150 m)`; **g = d + minGap_i** (the returned distance excludes the ego minGap: exactly 2.5 m in 2,000 of 2,000 checks; evidence: metrics, mechanics). The libsumo 1.27.1 "no leader" return value is handled explicitly [VERIFY in P0-B10] |
| TTC | if g > 0 and v_i > v_L: TTC = g / (v_i − v_L). If g ≤ 0: an overlap record (§2.7), not counted |
| Threshold TTC\* | **1.5 s** (V74), or **3.0 s** if the C9 fork fires (recorded before seed 010; one-way) |
| Exposure | TET_i = Σ_t 0.2 s · 1[TTC_i(t) < TTC\*] · 1[v_i(t) ≥ 10 m/s] (follower speed; B14, A28 (α)). A **conflict episode** is a maximal run of consecutive steps of one follower with that indicator = 1 (used by C9 and G6) |
| Unserved km (imputation) | a vehicle of K that is never inserted or is censored at the cap has planned km it never drove. It receives imputed exposure **e_s × its unserved planned km**, where e_s = Σ TET over B0's K / Σ served km of B0's K on seed s, one arm-independent rate per seed, used for every arm (B0 included) (v2 review MV-8). Without it, a never-inserted vehicle adds planned km to D_K and no exposure, so holding vehicles off the network would lower T. Reported: T without imputation and the imputed share per arm; a point whose band membership changes is labelled "imputation-dependent" |
| T | **T = 1,000 · (Σ_{i∈K} TET_i + imputed exposure) / D_K** |
| Leaders counted | any vehicle, in K or not, including the incident obstacle and the zipper partners that `getLeader` returns on internal lanes (merge conflicts count). **Obstacle attribution (v2 review MV-7):** T is also stored split by leader (the obstacle vs any other), and TET within 300 m upstream of x_I during the blockage is reported, mirroring h_all's "incident" attribution; an INC point whose band membership changes when the obstacle term is removed is labelled "obstacle-dependent" (its size depends on LC2013's late merging, whose realism is [VERIFY], §2.4b) |
| Followers counted | all categories (CAV, HDV car, truck), whole trip, all edges; never the obstacle |
| Effect | r_T = median_s(T_A − T_B0) / median_s(T_B0). **Not computable** (→ FAIL for that point, principle 7) if median_s(T_B0) = 0 |
| Sources | TET/TIT discrete form: Minderhoud & Bovy 2001, via the Abdel-Aty & Wang review (arXiv 2303.15231, full text, evidence: metrics); the original was not read [VERIFY]. 1.5 s: the SSAM default (Gettman, Pu, Sayed & Shelby 2008, FHWA-HRT-08-051, full text, evidence: metrics), citing Hayward 1972 and Sayed et al. 1994 [VERIFY]. 3.0 s: the SUMO SSM default (docs page) and the freeway value in the review's Table 3 |
| Cost | own TTC adds about 0.74–1.0 µs per vehicle-step, against 3.3–4.4 µs for the SSM device (evidence: mechanics, metrics; toys). Same-lane own TTC agreed with SSM within 0.005 s after a one-step label shift (evidence: mechanics) |

**Constraints (gated in P2)**

| Symbol | Definition | Trace |
|---|---|---|
| h | CAV hard-brake events (contiguous stretches with a < −3 m/s², V36), all speeds, per planned CAV-km of K (fixed); the split at onset speed ≥ 10 m/s reported | V25, V36, A28 (α′), unchanged |
| h_all | **events of all vehicles of K** (HDV cars, trucks, CAVs; contiguous a < −3 m/s² with onset speed ≥ 10 m/s), per 1,000 planned veh-km of K (D_K). Onset speed, location (lane-end and incident attribution, §2.7) and category stored per event | [AUTHOR A38] (a); B14 |
| Dl | network delay of K (above) | V37 |

**Secondary (reported, never a claim in P0–P2; V74):**

| Symbol | Definition |
|---|---|
| E_v | energy over the vehicle's whole trip: HBEFA4 fuel (mg/s) or electricity (Wh/s) × 0.2 s for cars; road-load + Willans for trucks; electricity net of regeneration, at the battery (V53). kWh via E10 41.5 MJ/kg, 748 kg/m³ and B7 42.7 MJ/kg, 836 kg/m³ (JEC WTW v5; the Irish blend is [VERIFY]). Vehicles in the origin queue use no energy in SUMO (an imputation row exists, §6.12) |
| € energy | petrol €1.98/L, diesel €2.15/L, electricity €0.4042/kWh (V21; price date in Addendum B) |
| **C** | **[Σ_v VoT_cat · d_v + Σ_v € energy_v] / D**, € per vehicle-km over the cohort; VoT_car and VoT_truck frozen in Addendum B before seed 7,400,000 (V29, V54). Reported with its CIs, its automation/control decomposition and the v1 band criteria evaluated on it as a labelled "C-band (secondary)" row (§6.8) |
| h_HDV | the h definition on HDV cars and trucks per HDV-km (all speeds), and the severe-event rate per HDV-km; report-only [AUTHOR A29] (superseded for at-speed events by h_all under A38 (a)) |

**Reported (pre-registered rows; never gates unless §6.8 says so):**
- **S family:** S_raw (no high-pass; also the co-confirmation of §6.8 (b_S′)); S_g40 and S_g200 (Gaussian σ_g = 40 s and 200 s, half-power periods ≈ 160 s and 802 s, the counterparts of v2-draft rows S_hp120 and S_hp600); S_box300 (the earlier draft's 300-s boxcar); the **Lagrangian wave-period row**: T_L = τ · |c| / (v̄_cong + |c|) from the Eulerian period τ, the wave speed c and the mean queue speed v̄_cong of §3.2, printed next to the filter's pass band (v2 review BDP2-1; my arithmetic for the formula; with τ = 600–1,200 s, |c| = 15 km/h and v̄ = 20–40 km/h it gives about 160–510 s, the steep part of every kernel's response); the Stern pooled SD over W (Stern et al. 2018 form: all samples about the space-time mean); **S/v̄_W** (v̄_W = Σ distance in W / Σ time in W over K: the speed-normalised form, FLAG when it moves against S); S per category (S_CAV, S_HDV, S_truck); S over the peak sub-cohort (desired departure in [3,360, 6,960) s = t_pk ± 30 min, ASSUMPTION); S over W2 = [8,000, 15,500) m (the "jam area" reading of V74) and W3 = the whole trip; S per strip [8,000, 9,000), [11,000, 12,000), [12,000, 14,000), [14,000, 15,500) m; acceleration noise from 1-s differenced speeds, L_i-weighted (Jones & Potts 1962 [VERIFY, abstract only]; raw 0.2-s accelerations are not used, because in the metrics toy their free-flow floor was about 87 % of the congested value for Krauss); stop-and-go cycles per vehicle-km on the mainline at 20/50 km/h (v1, the "stop-and-go index") and at 30/60 km/h (sensitivity, fixed now; the pilot's waves bottomed near 20 km/h); mean speed in W.
- **T family:** TET at 1.5 s at speed (reported as "severe" if the fork fired; pooled continuity-corrected ratio, §8.5), with the **severe FLAG**: if the fork fired and the exact conditional (binomial) 95 % CI of the pooled 1.5-s exposure ratio, arm vs B0 or vs B1, lies wholly above 1, the FLAG is printed in the claim sentence and in the band row (v2 review BDP2-4, MV-2; a gate is an option in [AUTHOR A56]); T at the other threshold; TIT_i = Σ_t 0.2 s · (TTC\* − TTC_i)⁺ at speed; all-speed TET, **also outside the zipper zone** [14,940, 15,060) m (FLAG when the at-speed T improves while the all-speed TET outside the zipper zone worsens by > 5 %; this keeps B14's exclusion of zipper creep; a gate is an option in [AUTHOR A56]); **short-headway exposure** Σ 0.2 s · 1[g_i/v_i < 1.0 s] · 1[v_i ≥ 10 m/s] per 1,000 planned veh-km, split into "within 5 s after a commanded change" and "other" (FLAG when a lane-commanding arm exceeds 1.1 × its keep-only twin; v2 review MV-3; ASSUMPTIONS: 1.0 s, 1.1); T split by leader type (obstacle, other); DRAC > 3.4 m/s² exposure, DRAC = (v_i − v_L)² / (2 g_i), from the same leader data (review value, evidence: metrics); T per follower category; T split into "within 5 s after a CAV lane change" (the CAV as the changer or as the new leader) and "other"; T without the zipper zone [14,940, 15,060) m; overlap records; collisions.
- **Braking:** h_all at −2.67 and −4.5 m/s² (sensitivity thresholds: an OEM hard-braking value and the decel cap; evidence: metrics); h; h_HDV (A29 rows); severe and extreme rates.
- **Lanes:** lane changes per vehicle-km (CAV commanded, CAV autonomous, HDV); per-lane flow shares and speeds at 12,750, 14,790 and 15,100 m; the lane-request execution rate within 2 s; the masked share; neighbour decelerations < −3 m/s² within 3 s of an executed commanded change; CAV time in mode M_cmd; at INC points, CAV re-entries into the blocked lane between 12,000 and 13,000 m.
- **v1 reported rows**, unchanged: per-zone travel time; CAV mean speed vs B1 (speed give-up); kWh per vehicle-km per category and powertrain; scenario totals in kWh and € (V24); share of CAV-boundaries with the target at 50 or 100 km/h (V47); origin-queue vehicle-hours per arm; censored count per arm; Dl, TTT and served throughput with CIs.

### 3.2 Plant diagnostics (P0, P1; never in any arm's observation, V27)
- **Speed probes.** Space-mean speed of the vehicles on the **through lanes** within ±50 m of x (vehicle-weighted: the mean over vehicles, not a mean of lane means), sampled every 10 s, at x = 250·i m (**i = 1…72**, to 18,000 m under V68) (the archive probes10 method). The auxiliary lanes of merge1 and merge2 are excluded; their speeds and waiting counts are reported separately. A 1-min speed is the mean of its six 10-s samples; empty samples are excluded, and a minute with no vehicle counts as uncongested.
- **Flow counters (virtual detectors).** Front crossings per lane at every probe and section, in 10-s and 60-s bins. P0-B6 shows they equal SUMO E1 counts (toy e_detect.py: 1,427 = 1,427 = 1,427; at most 1 vehicle apart per interval at bin edges). Timestamps are interval **ends** (ARCH report_S1 onset-label bug). Any E1 read uses `getLast*` (CLAUDE.md).
- **Congested probe-minute:** 1-min speed < **60 km/h**. **Sustained onset** at a probe: ≥ 5 consecutive congested minutes; t_b = its first minute. Source: the TII lane-capacity note (NRA/TII, "A Study of Lane Capacity in the Greater Dublin Area", Feb 2012, §5.4, read in full) finds a speed boundary of about 60 km/h between congested and uncongested conditions on the M50/M1/N11 sites and adopts Lorenz & Elefteriadou's criterion, speeds below 60 km/h for five minutes or more (original [VERIFY]). **Recovery** t_rec: the first of ≥ 5 consecutive minutes ≥ 70 km/h (ASSUMPTION: a 10-km/h hysteresis, the archive realism.py pair 60/70).
- **Measurement sections** (per lane, just downstream of each bottleneck, so that a lane-local drop is not diluted; ARCH notes_networks.md L9): BN-M1 at 3,400 m (3 lanes, plain 1); BN-M2 at 9,400 m (3 lanes, plain 2); BN-LD at 15,100 m (2 lanes, exit); positions taken from the built net. At BN-LD, lane 0 (the zipper output) is also reported on its own, with the metronome FLAG (§2.7).
- **Active bottleneck.** A discharge minute at section k counts only if the 1-min speed at the probe 200 m further downstream is ≥ 70 km/h, so that the bottleneck is discharging freely and not inside a queue spilled back from downstream (the usual meaning of an active bottleneck; the 200 m and 70 km/h implementation is an ASSUMPTION). An onset is **at bottleneck k** (an active onset) if it is a sustained onset at k's head probe (the probe nearest x_k − 250 m) while that downstream probe is ≥ 70 km/h at t_b. **Upstream bottlenecks** of k: BN-M1 and BN-M2 for BN-LD; BN-M1 for BN-M2; none for BN-M1 (an entry-group onset counts as upstream of every bottleneck). An upstream bottleneck j is **active** from its active onset t_b,j until its recovery t_rec,j. In INC runs the incident is an upstream bottleneck of BN-LD while it is active (below).
- **Between-run drop estimator** (ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md:139-152, Addendum A), on plateau runs at λ_a:
  - Q_ff(k) = median, over runs **without** an active onset at k **and without** any upstream bottleneck active during [2,400 + tt_k, 5,400 + tt_k) s, of the mean per-lane flow at section k over that interval, active minutes only;
  - Q_dis(k) = median, over runs **with** an active onset at k, of the per-run mean per-lane flow over [t_b + 300, min(t_b + 1,200, t_rec, 5,400 + tt_k + 600)) s, active minutes only, **excluding minutes in which an upstream bottleneck of k is active** (upstream starvation); a run needs ≥ 5 such minutes, else it is not valid for Q_dis;
  - ρ = Q_dis / Q_ff. The bottleneck carried Q_ff without breaking down, so Q_ff is a lower bound on capacity and ρ **understates** the drop (the archive's argument);
  - the runs and minutes excluded for upstream activity are counted and reported. The C0′ screen applies the same estimator to runs pooled over three plateau levels (§2.6).
- **Triangle discharge** (P0-G2, G5, T1a): per run with an active onset at k, the mean per-lane flow over [t_b + 300, min(t_b + 1,200, t_rec, 9,900 + tt_k)) s, active minutes only, excluding minutes with an upstream bottleneck active, ≥ 5 such minutes.
- **v_cong:** mean 1-min speed of the probes in [x_k − 800, x_k − 300] m over the Q_dis window (Yuan, Knoop & Hoogendoorn 2015 poster: queue discharge rises with the speed in the queue).
- **Origin of congestion:** per run, the probe with the earliest sustained onset (ties → the most downstream; archive realism.py), assigned to a bottleneck group (§2.1), the entry group, the incident group (INC runs only) or "open road".
- **Wave speed c** (archive estimator, ARCH vsl_lab/eval/realism.py `wave_speed`): 10-s probe speeds; high-pass = series minus its centred 5-min mean; on the longest jointly congested segment (centred 5-min means < 60 km/h) of ≥ 15 min; lag of the cross-correlation peak searched in 10-s steps from 0 to min(600 s, segment − 600 s); valid if the peak correlation is ≥ 0.3 (ASSUMPTION, archive value) and the lag is interior (not at either end); c = −Δx / lag. Here up to four probe pairs 500 m apart, going upstream from the active bottleneck's head probe; the run estimate is the median over valid pairs. Resolution: 10-s lags at 500 m resolve c to about 1.5 km/h near −15 km/h, whereas a 1-min series could give only −15 or −10 km/h at lags of 2 or 3 min (the pilot's 1-min estimate spread over −5 to −45 km/h, not data).
- **Wave period τ:** first autocorrelation peak of the high-passed 10-s series at the probe 1 km upstream of the active bottleneck, over the congested segment (Treiber & Kesting 2010 method, arXiv 1008.1639, sections read; venue [VERIFY]). Report-only.
- **Free-flow speed:** speed of vehicles on the entry section [0, 3,000) m with no leader within 150 m.
- **Pre-breakdown flow** (report-only): the highest 1-min per-lane flow at the section in the 5 min before t_b. Within-run and confounded by the inflow ramp; shown only next to the TII 2012 breakdown flows for context.
- **Incident diagnostics** (INC runs): **incident active minute** = 1-min speed < 60 km/h at the head probe 12,750 m and ≥ 70 km/h at the downstream probe 13,250 m (the v1 active-bottleneck conventions moved to the incident). **Binding** = a sustained onset at the 12,750-m probe whose first minute t_b lies in [T_on, T_on + 600) s while the 13,250-m probe is ≥ 70 km/h at that minute. **F_inc** (runs with a binding incident) = the median, over the incident-active minutes in **[T_on + 300, T_on + 900) s** (anchored to the onset, so that no post-clearance minute can enter; v2 review BDP2-6), of the 1-min all-lane flow at the 13,100-m section, divided by **3 × q_lane**, where q_lane = Q_dis(BN-LD) per lane from P0-G1 (calibration: Q_dis,cal from C0′); a run needs ≥ 5 such minutes, else it is not valid for F_inc. Knoop, Hoogendoorn & Adams 2009 define the ratio against the normal queue-discharge rate (full text, evidence: incident). Also reported: queue reach (the most upstream congested probe during the blockage; does it reach Z2 at 11–12 km?), teleport locations, the maximum waiting time behind the obstacle, t_blk (the obstacle's block start, §2.4b) and the probe onset t_b. **Notation:** t_b is always a probe's sustained-onset minute; the block start is t_blk.
- **Lane diagnostics:** per-lane 1-min flows and speeds at every section; the share of CAVs on each lane at each zone's downstream end (8,990, 11,990, 14,790 m).

### 3.3 Which metrics each check uses

| Check | Metrics |
|---|---|
| C0′, P0-G1 | Q_ff, Q_dis, ρ, v_cong, onset counts and valid Q_dis runs at BN*; upstream exclusions; per-lane discharge at BN-LD; health |
| C9, P0-G6 | S, T, conflict episodes, S_floor, T_floor (B0) |
| C10, P0-GI | F_inc, binding, artefacts, t_blk, waiting time behind the obstacle; S, T, h_all, Dl and C in cohort_I and the full cohort (report) |
| P0-G2 | origin, c, τ (report); insertion GEH, departDelay, lane split; health; free-flow speeds; manifest vs SUMO speed factors; S, T (both thresholds, both speed conditions), TIT, h_all base rates (report) |
| P0-G3 | CAV severe-event rates (B1 vs the same vehicles in B0, definition per A28) with the cut-in, location and speed splits; the −3 m/s² ratio; CAV collisions; CAV free-flow speed; Dl, C, energy (flag); T, TET at 1.5 s, h_all, r_S and r_T of B1 vs B0 (report) |
| P0-G4 | single-vehicle energy per km per class at 0–120 km/h; idle; acceleration and braking cycle; truck model and its sensitivity models; regeneration sign and bound |
| P0-G5 | per-run mean door-to-door time (J1); triangle discharge (J2), paired by seed; S (J3); T (J4, report); onset and origin shares, censored share (report) |
| T0a | per-step speed and acceleration; the `exec` call log |
| T0b | zone space-mean speed, zone density |
| T0c | Dl, section throughput, S, T |
| T0d | zone summaries with per-lane fields, per-lane spacing, V2V lists with relative lanes, radar gap vs trace, side/rear gaps, the incident message; command log |
| T0e, T0f | `exec` log, lane trace, own TTC, neighbour accelerations, `getLaneChangeMode` read-back; lane shares at zone ends; execution rates |
| T0g | t_blk per run and arm; teleports; waiting time behind the obstacle |
| T1a | onsets, triangle discharge, ρ_T1, health |
| T1b | S, T |
| T1c | S, T, h, h_all, h_HDV, Dl, C, energy, TTT, throughput, validity |
| T3 | per-step trace hash (lane calls and the obstacle state included), manifest hashes (INC schedule included), record fields |
| P2 | S, T, h, h_all, Dl, validity, censored count, origin-queue time, served throughput, C (secondary) and every §3.1 reported row; S_raw for (b_S′); cohort_I and the full cohort at INC points |

### 3.4 Gameability guards (ARCH docs/lessons_metric_gameability.md §4–§5; evidence: metrics, lanes)

| Route to game S or T | Guard |
|---|---|
| **Crawl** (slow everything: every speed-variability measure falls; in the rigor toy a 50 km/h limit cut S by 32 % at +62 % travel time and raised the stop-and-go count by 20 %; in the CIRCLES field data, lower speed variance behind the AVs came partly with lower speeds, Jang et al. 2025, Sec. 4.3.2, full text) | delay guard (e), V37. **Heuristic, not a derivation:** under (e) the cohort's time in W can grow by at most about 0.02 · Dl_B0, which is small against the speed reductions that a ≥ 10 % S gain by crawling needs in the toy. Reported FLAG: S/v̄_W moving against S (it rose by 10.2 % under the toy's crawl and by 3.8 % under the toy's 70 km/h limit). Mean speed in W and the CAV speed give-up are shown next to S |
| Smooth only the commanded CAVs (a held CAV has a small σ̃ by construction) | S counts all vehicles. S_HDV and T_HDV are reported with a FLAG when either worsens by > 2 % at a band point (ASSUMPTION). The claim text must state the HDV share of the gain |
| Move the jam outside the window | W reaches from the network entry to 15,500 m (W1); per-strip rows. Holding vehicles off the network is caught by guards (g1) censoring and (g2) origin queue, by the arm-independent imputation of S (vehicles with < 150 samples in W) and of T (unserved planned km) (§3.1), and by delay from the desired departure |
| Serve fewer km / empty the road (TTC) | fixed planned-km denominators for S, T, h and h_all; T's unserved planned km carry imputed exposure at B0's per-km rate (§3.1); (g1), (g2); TTT and delay include the origin queue |
| Convert at-speed conflicts into low-speed ones, or trade severe exposure for milder exposure | (e); all-speed TET outside the zipper zone with a FLAG; the severe (1.5-s) row with its FLAG when the fork has fired; CAV targets cannot go below 50 km/h (V47). **Limits, stated:** (e) does not constrain delay-neutral redistribution inside a saturated queue, the 50 km/h floor bounds CAV targets, not actual speeds, and the FLAGs gate nothing; zeroing at-speed T inside a slow queue by removing stop-and-go is the intended wave-damping effect under B14. Gating either row is an option in [AUTHOR A56] (v2 review MV-2) |
| Avoid conflicts by opening huge gaps (CAV followers) | cut-ins then raise T for the new HDV followers, which T counts; served throughput with CI and (e) |
| Push braking onto neighbours with lane commands (mechanics toy: urgent mode 1621 raised neighbour hard braking up to 2.4 × the no-command baseline) | mode 1878 (no speed adaptation asked of neighbours; [AUTHOR A45]); (f2) on all vehicles at speed ([AUTHOR A38]); the neighbour-braking row; T0e(b) |
| Block a lane (Vinitsky et al. 2018 report that ARS agents on the bottleneck benchmark "simply learn to block a lane" to raise outflow; full text, evidence: metrics) | (e); per-lane flows; lane-change rate; served throughput with CI |
| Lane-change concentration (lane changes concentrated in space and time create voids and waves; evidence: lanes, Schakel et al. 2012 and Zhang C. et al. 2019 full texts) | lane changes per vehicle-km (CAV and HDV): FLAG when > 2 × B1 (ASSUMPTION); T split by lane-change attribution; LM-E spreads its change points |
| TTC blindness during the manoeuvre (instant lane changes; SSM has no cut-in conflict type) | T counts the cut-in from the step after the change (instant changes, A51); T0e(g) proves capture of a staged cut-in, and T0e(g′) records what commanded changes under M_cmd look like to T (accepted gap, new follower's TTC and headway); the short-headway row with its lane-change split and FLAG (§3.1); C11 reports the share of merging and adjacent-lane conflicts for B0 and B1, and the T0f SSM rows do so for R-LANE vs R-KEEP (§5). Under priority 3 a commanded change needs SUMO's secure gap, so a cut-in at a low TTC is not expected (the review's arithmetic; [VERIFY] in T0e(g′)) |
| Structural zeros | C9 pre-check, F6 and P0-G6 (with the conflict-episode count); r_T not computable when median T_B0 = 0; per-point informativeness at every tuned and INC point ([AUTHOR A59]) |
| Move harm outside cohort_I at an INC point (slow X2-bound vehicles in Z1, push the stored queue onto later vehicles) | the guards (e)–(g) on cohort_I **and** on the full cohort, plus the full-cohort non-inferiority row ([AUTHOR A55]); the X1/X2-bound and late-arrival subgroups reported |
| Change the oscillation period, not its amplitude (a filter whose response depends on the period) | a Gaussian trend whose response falls steadily with the period; the co-confirmation (b_S′): S_raw must not get worse; the Lagrangian wave-period row next to the filter's pass band ([AUTHOR A58]) |
| Automation credited as control | B1 rows and (d_S), (d_T) on both co-primaries; the decomposition (Y_B0 − Y_B1) / Y_B0 and (Y_B1 − Y_A) / Y_B0 printed for S and T (and C); the wording rule (§6.8) |
| A chosen window, filter or threshold | W, the Gaussian trend (σ_g = 100 s, [AUTHOR A58]), TTC\* and its fork, the speed condition, K, cohort_I and the floors are fixed here, before data (principle 13); alternatives appear only as pre-registered rows |
| *Secondary C:* serve fewer km to improve a per-km ratio | fixed denominator D (planned km), the same for every arm |
| *Secondary C:* push delay into the origin queue; vehicles waiting off-network use no energy in SUMO | τ and TTT from the desired departure; origin-queue vehicle-hours reported; band guard (g2); an idle-energy imputation row (§6.12) [AUTHOR A23] |
| *Secondary C:* push vehicles past the cap (censoring) | band guard (g1); a row with censored trips completed by imputation (§6.12) |
| *Secondary C:* slow CAVs to save energy | delay guard (e); CAV speed give-up reported; R4 includes the 50 km/h floor |
| Raw counts grow with time in the network | every rate per vehicle-km or CAV-km of a fixed denominator |

---

## 4 P0 checks (plant realism and metric informativeness)

### 4.0 Order of execution
S0 ([AUTHOR] answers, Addendum B) → P0-B (with the ACC pre-screen, P0-G4 and the incident injector test, which need no project seed) → P0-S → Addendum A → P0-C (C0′, then C1–C8, C9, C10, C11; calibration report; futility stops F1–F7) → Addendum D → **P0-G1 alone** → P0-G2, G3, G5, **G6** and **GI** (one batch on 040–059, job order shuffled across arms and scenarios) → verdict. G1, G2, G6 and GI need only the plant, the metrics and the incident injector; G3 and G5 need the CAV vType, which is part of the plant, but no `exec`. The execution layer, V2X and the arms are built only after P0 PASS. Every run passes the health layer (§2.7); FAIL runs are counted, never dropped.

### 4.1 P0-B: build and code acceptance (no project seeds; all must pass before seed 7,400,000 is used)
Each item is a unit or micro-scenario test in the repo, run on non-project seeds 1–999 [AUTHOR A16a], observing no F3Z plant outcome. Each test includes a **negative control** that shows the code can produce the FAIL it is meant to catch. PASS = 100 % of tests pass. FAIL = fix the code; no seed is consumed and no deviation note is needed (logged in the commit).

| Id | Test |
|---|---|
| B1 | Built net: every edge length within ±0.5 m of the spec (V68 positions); lane counts; zipper connections 0, 1 → 0 and 2 → 1 (and, for CD1, `visibility="20"` read back from the net file); auxiliary lanes continue to X1/X2; zones (V71), bottleneck groups, the incident group and the ban segment map to x ranges; probe (i = 1…72), section, incident and lane-use positions read from the built net; through lanes and auxiliary lanes labelled per probe; spec hash recorded and mandatory |
| B2 | Config table §2.2 applied; a run with a misspelt vType attribute stops with an XML error (negative control) |
| B3 | R-0 inertness: in a 20-vehicle single-lane micro-scenario, perturbing each non-default attribute of every vType (HDV car under each CD candidate, truck, each ACC candidate, the obstacle) by ±20 % changes the per-step trace hash; 0 inert attributes (ARCH STATUS #10). Every vType states its mass, and the severe-event threshold of [AUTHOR A28] differs by ≥ 0.01 m/s² from every decel in the manifest |
| B4 | Conservation identities exact on synthetic manifests, including departures exactly on a step boundary (STATUS #3) **and a manifest with the incident obstacle as a deterministic special vehicle**: the identities hold with it (its removal at 13,200 m counts as its arrival), and it is excluded from the cohort, from D and D_K, and from r; each FAIL code of §2.7 fires on an injected fault (unbounded backlog at constant sub-capacity inflow, a stopped vehicle at a lane end, an injected collision, a SUMO error, an injected late incident start t_blk = T_on + 20 s) |
| B5 | The collision counter counts pairs, not vehicle-steps; a merge collision is detected on the junction lane (toy c_collide.py, c_junction.py) |
| B6 | Virtual detectors = E1 counts per interval (±1 at bin edges), totals equal; timestamps are interval ends; reads use `getLast*` (toy e_detect.py: `getInterval*` returns the new interval) |
| B7 | Generator on seeds 1–999: per route and 15-min bin, the exact two-sided Poisson test of the realised count against the nominal integral rejects at α = 0.01 in 0.5–1.5 % of bins; mean count within ±0.5 % of nominal; a 5,000 veh/h route is not capped; changing one route's rate leaves the other routes' arrival times identical; attribute shares within ±0.5 percentage points of §2.3; the per-vehicle speed factor read back from SUMO equals the manifest for 100 % of vehicles; **RNG isolation: the attribute and arrival hashes are identical with and without the INC schedule** (the obstacle draws no random number) |
| B8 | `stats`: paired median and percentile bootstrap (numerator and denominator resampled) reproduce a hand-computed case; **joint resampling** (S, T and every guard of one (s, A) evaluation use the same resampled seed indices, §8.2); the invalid-run encodings of §8.4 (effect and guard forms); the continuity-corrected rate ratio of §8.5; the empirical per-metric KILL-1 calibration of §6.5; bad input raises, never `assert` (port_map stats trap 1) |
| B9 | Seed guard: refuses any seed outside the role declared for the job (negative control) and appends to a burned-seed ledger |
| B10 | Metrics identities on synthetic traces with hand-computed values, exact to 10⁻⁹ relative: cohort, K and cohort_I (built from the manifest and r_13 only), τ, censoring, fixed D and D_K identical across arms, delay, R_tot, kWh conversion, € cost, C, hard-brake events (CAV h; h_all at onset speeds 9.99 / 10.00 m/s and a = −3 ± 10⁻⁹ m/s², all categories), stop-and-go at 20/50 and 30/60 km/h, TTT from the desired departure (origin queue included), served throughput; no negative censored times (runner trap 6); arrivals after the cap are censored; the power-trace writer fires only for the pre-declared subset (§10.4). **S:** the fixed weights L_i are identical across arms; a vehicle that stops counts at full weight (negative control: a time-weighted pooled variant gives a different value); the arm-independent imputation c_s fires for a never-inserted vehicle and for one with < 150 samples in W and takes the same value in two arms on one seed (negative control: the earlier run-maximum rule gives different values); T's imputed exposure for unserved planned km uses B0's per-km rate e_s in every arm; the Stern form and the 1-s acceleration noise; per-strip S. **Storage schema (§3.1):** on a non-project seed with a full 0.2-s trace written, every pre-registered S, T, braking and lane row recomputed offline from the trace equals the online value to 10⁻⁹ relative (negative control: a row computed from a truncated trace fails). **T:** (i) a synthetic leader pair with a known TTC; (ii) the overlap record fires for g ≤ 0 and is not counted; (iii) the at-speed filter uses the follower's speed; (iv) conflict episodes are counted as maximal runs; (v) a zipper-partner leader is counted as returned; (vi) the obstacle is never an ego and is counted as a leader; (vii) the libsumo "no leader" return value is handled; (viii) the obstacle/other leader split and the 300-m incident attribution; (ix) the short-headway exposure at g/v = 0.99 / 1.00 / 1.01 s and v = 9.99 / 10.00 m/s; TIT and DRAC |
| B11 | Realism estimators on synthetic speed and flow series with a known onset, recovery, drop, active/inactive minutes and wave: t_b, t_rec, origin, ρ, c and τ recovered within one aggregation interval or 1 %. Cases: (i) an upstream breakdown that starves bottleneck k: those runs and minutes are excluded from Q_ff(k) and Q_dis(k); (ii) one stopped auxiliary-lane vehicle with free through lanes: no onset; (iii) acceleration values at exactly −4.5 m/s² and at −4.5 ± 10⁻⁹ m/s²: the event counts follow the [AUTHOR A28] definition and do not depend on floating-point rounding; (iv) a metronome series (fixed headways) raises the zipper FLAG; (v) **S's high-pass:** synthetic sinusoids (periods 200, 300, 400 and 600 s, amplitude A) give σ̃ equal to the hand-computed Gaussian (σ_g = 100 s) residual, i.e. variance shares 0.986 / 0.789 / 0.502 / 0.178 away from the series ends (checked in this revision on a 6,000-s series: 0.983 / 0.792 / 0.503 / 0.175), and a single step change gives the hand-computed residual (negative control: a version without the high-pass fails it; the 300-s boxcar row gives 1.469 at 200 s); (vi) **incident binding and F_inc** on a synthetic series with a known blockage, **including a late probe onset (t_b = T_on + 590 s): F_inc uses only minutes in [T_on + 300, T_on + 900), so no post-clearance minute enters** (negative control: a window anchored at the probe onset fails) |
| B12 | `ops`: the CPU-guard tests pass (launch only below 90 % utilisation; SIGSTOP to the process group; resume one per 30 s; every pause logged); the CPU-time cap: a job SIGSTOPped for longer than the cap in wall time is **not** killed or marked invalid (negative control), a CPU-bound runaway job is killed as a collapse, a job with no CPU progress while not paused is an OPS hang and re-run once; job order is shuffled across arms within each batch by a seeded permutation; a ledger row for every job, failed ones included; health columns filled by code from run summaries (ARCH ledger: 89 of 221 run rows had 0/0/0 health counts); a resume after a kill skips finished (arm, config, level, seed) tuples without duplicate rows |
| B13 | Determinism-lite: a 600-s B0 run on non-project seed 1 at low inflow, run twice in fresh processes, gives an identical per-step trace hash (no metric is read) |
| **B14** own TTC | Micro-scenario, two vehicles on one lane, scripted speeds: own g = d + minGap equals the hand value exactly, and own TTC equals g/Δv. Against the SSM device (TTC, trajectories, ego as follower, SSM times shifted by +1 step): per-step \|ΔTTC\| ≤ 0.01 s on every same-lane following step (the evidence matched them within 0.005 s; evidence: mechanics). **Cut-in case:** a CAV changes lanes ahead of an HDV at a 1.2-s TTC (test-only mode); own TTC records 1.2 ± 0.05 s for the new follower at the step after the change (negative control: a leader search that skips the new leader fails). **SSM type filter:** a scripted two-lane merge in which the ego reaches the merge point after the foe appears in the raw SSM output with type 7 in its span and survives the offline ego-as-follower filter {2, 7, 11} (negative control: the device option `exclude-conflict-types foe` drops it). **No side effect:** the per-step trace hash is identical with and without the SSM device |
| **B15** S on SUMO | A lone vehicle at constant speed has σ̃ < 0.05 m/s. A vehicle driven behind a scripted stop-and-go leader cycle has σ̃ within ±5 % of the value computed offline from its logged trace |
| **B16** incident injector | On a free three-lane road (non-project seed): the obstacle stops at x_I on lane 0 with t_blk = T_on ± 1 s, **which fixes Δ_ins**; it moves again at T_on + 900 ± 0.2 s with its lane-change mode read back as 1621, and it is removed at the first step its front is ≥ 13,200 m; it is never in K, never an ego in S, T or h_all, and is a leader in T. Negative controls: a stop given `until` without `duration = 0` never ends (as found in the toy), and the test catches it; an obstacle on the wrong lane is caught; an injected late start raises the integrity FAIL. **Fallback case** (run now, used only after a T0g FAIL): forced insertion of the stopped obstacle at x_I at exactly T_on with relaxed insertion checks works in 1.27.1 [VERIFY], and its onset row is produced |

### 4.2 P0-S: smoke (seeds 7,400,000–001; throw-away)
- **Runs:** B0 and B1 on the triangle at λ ∈ {0.7, 1.0} and on the plateau probe at λ = 0.8, both seeds, plus **one B0 INC run per seed** (triangle λ = 0.8): **14 episodes**. The B0 triangle run at λ = 1.0 on seed 7,400,000 records the **golden B0 trace hash**.
- **Purpose:** the pipeline runs end to end; every §3 estimator returns a number (S, T, h_all and the incident diagnostics included); CPU time, wall time, memory and trace volume are measured, **for the full production pipeline** (ground-truth reads, S/T accumulation with the storage schema, probe and section crossings, the per-step trace hash of T3a) **in a batch at 32 workers**, not in isolation (v2 review SF10). The per-episode cost of commanding arms (V2X and boundary logic) is measured later in the P1-B and P2-B timing tests. **No criterion is evaluated on smoke data**, and no smoke value is tabulated except costs and pipeline errors. Addendum B (VoT, prices, metric constants) is already committed (§9.4).
- **On a defect:** an estimator or implementation fix goes into a dated **Addendum A** before any calibration run (ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md:119-166: the smoke test found the estimator defect and the H4 collapse). Addendum A may not change any bound; it may fix the injector's Δ_ins only if B16's free-road value proves wrong on F3Z.

### 4.3 P0-C: calibration (seeds 7,400,002–009) and Addendum D
Calibration follows §2.6 exactly, including the ACC pre-screen and the futility stops F1–F7. The **calibration report** shows only what C0′–C11 need: the C0′ adoption (every screened setting with its screen fields, the adopted setting, k_cal), λ_c, λ_sub, BN*, λ_a, the insertion configuration, the ACC pre-screen outcomes and the chosen candidate with the margin case it fell into (R ≤ 0.85, or the flagged 0.85–1.25 case), r and r_13 with their fallback cells and sanity FLAGs, the T3-early result, the CPU-time caps, **S_floor, T_floor and the TTC\* in force (C9)**, **the INC points that passed C10 (with the I1 outcome, the waiting-time margin of [AUTHOR A64]) and the tuned INC point**, **the C11 FLAG status**, the B0 severe-event count of every screened CD setting ([AUTHOR A65]), and which futility stops fired. ρ, Q_dis (except as screen pass/fail), discharge values, C, delay, energy, the per-candidate R values, every B1 value of S and T and every B1 − B0 contrast are stored in the ledger but are not tabulated or read until the P0 report. **Addendum D** (informational) re-issues the CPU plan from C8 if the measured cost differs from §10 by more than 25 %.

### 4.4 P0-G1 Queue discharge, artefacts and the capacity drop at the primary bottleneck (the first gate; [AUTHOR A42], recommendation (b) written as the primary design)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Does B0 discharge from the queue at BN* at a plausible per-lane rate, without artefacts (gated)? Does it show a capacity drop in the field band (report-only under A42 (b); gated under (a))? |
| Measurement | B0, plateau probe at λ_a of the **adopted** plant (C0′). Between-run estimator at BN*'s section (§3.2), with the upstream-starvation exclusions; Q_dis also at every other bottleneck with ≥ 5 valid Q_dis runs |
| Statistic | medians over runs; 95 % percentile bootstrap CI of ρ (runs resampled within the two groups, 20,000 resamples), reported, not gated |
| PASS under A42 (b) (all) | **(a′)** ≥ 5 runs valid for Q_dis at BN* (fewer: not computable → FAIL, principle 7); **(c)** **Q_dis(BN*) ∈ [floor, 2,300] veh/h/lane**, floor per [AUTHOR A25] (1,600, V65), and every other bottleneck with ≥ 5 valid Q_dis runs also in that band; **(d)** **over the original 30 runs (010–039)**: 0 teleports, 0 invalid runs, 0 health FAIL, and ≤ 2 collisions in total. Artefacts in extension runs are reported prominently, and such runs are invalid for the estimator, but they cannot turn (d) into a FAIL |
| Report-only under A42 (b) | (a) the onset split (≥ 5 runs with and ≥ 5 without an active onset at BN*); **(b) ρ with its CI and v_cong**, stated as inside or outside the field band [0.82, 0.97]; the C0′ screen outcomes. **Mandatory limitation sentence** in every report and thesis claim when ρ is outside the band: "the plant shows no capacity drop in the field band (ρ = …, CI …); delay and throughput results are secondary and are not interpretable as capacity gains; lane-command effects that act through lane-change voids at the drop are muted on this plant". **When the P0-R9 FLAG fires** (the zipper output lane discharges below the floor while the section mean is in band), the sentence adds: "the zipper output lane discharges below the 1,600 veh/h/lane floor (… veh/h/lane) while lane 2's continuation carries …; the section mean hides this" ([AUTHOR A62], recommended (b)). ρ and Q_dis per lane at BN-LD are reported in every case. (In the review's read of the mechanics toy, `toy/out/t4b.jsonl`, a 3 → 2 zipper and not F3Z, 5 seeds, short queues: zipper-lane discharge inside the queue was 1,036–1,461 veh/h under every visibility setting, with section means of 1,542–1,880; v2 review SF3, not re-read in this revision) |
| PASS under A42 (a) (all) | v1: (a) ≥ 5 runs **with** and ≥ 5 runs **without** an active onset at BN* (the latter also free of upstream activity), and ≥ 5 runs valid for Q_dis; **(b) ρ ∈ [0.82, 0.97]** (a 3–18 % drop); (c) and (d) as above |
| Sources | (b) Yuan, Knoop & Hoogendoorn 2017, *Transportation Science* 51(3):852-862 (accepted manuscript read in full) review reported drops of 3–18 %; the originals they cite [VERIFY each]. About 10 %: Cassidy & Bertini 1999 [VERIFY, abstract only]. About 15–16.5 % at the US-169 NB Plymouth Ave merge: Geroliminis, Srivastava & Michalopoulos 2011, UMN CTS 11-05 (read in full). 2–11 % at 27 Twin Cities bottlenecks, 7 % at a 3 → 2 lane drop: Zhang & Levinson 2004, TRR 1883 (full text, `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` §1). HCM default 7 % [VERIFY, via the McTrans page]. Band choice: [AUTHOR A2]. (c) ASSUMPTION built from sources, not a published band: Dutch 3-lane A4/A12 queue discharge observed at 5,220–6,840 veh/h, i.e. 1,740–2,280 veh/h/lane, over v_cong 6–60 km/h (Yuan et al. 2015, TRB poster 15-4064, read; their fit Q = 29·v + 5,000 veh/h for 3 lanes gives 1,725–2,247 veh/h/lane over that range, my arithmetic); 1,866–2,031 at a 100 km/h 4-lane site (Han et al., arXiv 2602.22019, read); long-run mean queue discharge 2,016 pcplph, 1,994 at a 3 → 2 lane drop (Zhang & Levinson 2004, full text); M50 lane means 1,580–1,960 veh/h (TII 2012, read; pre-breakdown flows, not discharge); HCM-based ≈ 1,990–2,030 (my arithmetic, with an assumed truck PCE of 2.0 [VERIFY HCM]). The archive band [1,600, 2,400] had no source (ARCH t2_realism_protocol.md:88). Upper bound: [AUTHOR A3]; floor: [AUTHOR A25]. Neither is changed after data (archive ZM3 at 1,528 stayed FAIL) |
| Computability | With n = 30, P(the onset split fails) = 0.03 at a true onset share of 0.3 or 0.7, 0.10 at 0.25 or 0.75, 0.26 at 0.2 or 0.8 (binomial; my arithmetic). These figures bound only the onset split, **not** Q_dis validity: short breakdowns (the archive's EIDM failure mode: "EIDM's breakdowns at the 50 %-share cell are too short to measure a drop", ARCH STATUS.md:73; H5 Q_dis not computable at n = 1+15, ARCH t2_realism_step0.2.md:9) can make (a′) fail with many onsets. **Pre-registered extension, once:** if (a′) (or, under A42 (a), (a)) fails only by group size or by valid Q_dis runs, B0 is run at λ_a on seeds 090–099 and pooled (n = 40: 0.016 at 0.25/0.75, 0.076 at 0.2/0.8). Bounds unchanged; (d) stays on the original 30. The archive's onset-share gate (≥ 0.3) is not used: the share is a property of the cell choice, not of realism, and its drift between calibration and check seeds made R-a "not computable" in 9 of 15 archive gate runs (ARCH notes_networks.md L5). The share is reported |
| Known risks | Under A42 (a), **the most likely P0 FAIL** (§0.4). A plant with a high queue speed v_cong can show ρ ≈ 1 for a realistic reason: Yuan et al. 2015 observed discharge above the 6,300 veh/h three-lane pre-queue capacity when the speed in congestion was high. BN* is expected to be BN-LD, a zipper node, the bottleneck type whose archive analogues discharged as a metronome below the floor; the metronome FLAG (§2.7) and P0-R9 watch it |
| Reported | v_cong next to ρ (Yuan et al. 2015 relation, my arithmetic: against their pre-queue capacity of 6,300 veh/h, 0 % drop at v_cong ≥ 44.8 km/h, 17.9 % at 6 km/h, ρ = 0.79 at v = 0; against their maximum observed discharge of 6,840 veh/h: 63.4 km/h, 24.4 %, 0.73); ρ and Q_dis at the other bottlenecks; the onset share; runs and minutes excluded for upstream activity; per-lane discharge at BN-LD with the metronome FLAG (P0-R9); the within-run "pre-breakdown flow" ratio (confounded; values only); 5-min discharge CV (Cassidy & Bertini 1999 report near-stationary discharge [VERIFY]); pre-breakdown flows vs TII 2012 (P0-R5); lane flow shares at breakdown |
| Seeds, n | 7,400,010–039, n = 30 (+ 090–099 if extended) |
| On FAIL | **STOP P0** → §9.2. [AUTHOR A2], [AUTHOR A3] and [AUTHOR A25] concern the bounds themselves and were settled before seed 7,400,000 |

### 4.5 P0-G2 Onset location, waves, artefacts, insertion and desired speeds in the experiment scenario

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | In the triangle scenario, does congestion start at the bottlenecks, do waves travel upstream at a plausible speed, does the plant receive the inflow it is given, are the runs artefact-free, and are desired speeds implemented? |
| Levels | **λ_c**, and a second level **λ_2** = 1.0 if 1.0 ≥ λ_c + 0.05, else λ_c + 0.2 |
| Measurement | B0 triangle at both levels. Origin per run; c per run; insertion (per entry and hour bin: M = vehicles inserted, S = vehicles scheduled; departDelay; lane split at insertion); health; free-flow speeds; manifest vs SUMO speed factors |
| PASS (all) | **(a) Onset location (R-d):** pooled over all B0 runs of G1 and G2 with any sustained onset, ≥ **80 %** have their origin in a bottleneck group and ≤ **5 %** in the entry group; ≥ 20 such runs, else not computable → FAIL. **(b) Wave speed (R-b):** median c over runs with a valid estimate ∈ **[−25, −10] km/h**, with ≥ 5 valid run estimates **pooled over the 40 B0 runs at λ_c and λ_2** (the v1 reading, now written out; the per-level counts and medians are reported); fewer → not computable → FAIL. Wave **presence** is reported per level and later per band point ([AUTHOR A60]). **(c) Artefacts.** *Realism gate* at λ_c, and at λ_2 if λ_2 ≤ λ_c + 0.1: 0 teleports, 0 invalid runs, 0 health FAIL and ≤ 2 collisions in total over the 20 runs. *Admissibility* at λ_2 > λ_c + 0.1: the single admissibility rule of §8.8 (B0 invalid share ≤ 5 %, i.e. ≤ 1 of 20); artefact counts reported. **(d) Insertion fidelity**, on uncongested entry intervals (the first 500 m of the entry edge at a 1-min speed ≥ 70 km/h on the mainline, ≥ 0.7 × the ramp limit on ramps), at both levels: GEH = √(2(M − S)² / (M + S)) **< 5 in 100 %** of uncongested entry-hours; median departDelay ≤ 1 s, 99th percentile ≤ 10 s, maximum ≤ 60 s; each mainline lane receives ≥ 20 % of mainline insertions; 0 vehicles discarded. **(e) Desired speeds:** the manifest speed factor equals SUMO's per-vehicle value for 100 % of vehicles; median free-flow speed of the 100 km/h class ∈ [97, 103] km/h, of the (100, 120] class ∈ [107, 113] km/h, of trucks ∈ [87, 92] km/h |
| Sources | (a) 80 %: archive R-d, which discriminated (MRG3 d3 scored 0.15 and 0.00; ARCH t2_realism_d3.md:7-10); about 400 A5 breakdowns, all at bottlenecks (Treiber, Kesting & Helbing 2010, arXiv 1004.5545, §2 read; venue [VERIFY]). 5 %: ASSUMPTION (an entry onset is an insertion or lane-choice artefact). (b) −15 ± 5 km/h (Schönhof & Helbing 2007, *Transportation Science* 41(2):135-166, relevant sections read; their cited originals [VERIFY]); −20 to −15 km/h (Treiber, Kesting & Helbing 2010); UK M4 about −14 and US 19–20 km/h upstream (Zielke, Bertini & Treiber 2008 [VERIFY, abstract level]); 22–24 km/h (Mauch & Cassidy 2002 [VERIFY]). The band keeps the archive's [−25, −10]. Not computable = FAIL: stop-and-go oscillation inside congestion is a stylised fact (Treiber et al. 2010), and wave-damping comparators have nothing to act on without it; **under V74 the claim rests on these waves, so (b) stays gated**. (d) GEH form and the < 5 criterion: TII PAG Unit 5.1 (PE-PAG-02015, Dec 2023, §3.2, Table 5.1.2, read; > 85 % of cases there), tightened to 100 % because a synthetic plant has no counting error, so any miss is an insertion artefact. 1 s, 10 s, 60 s and 20 %: ASSUMPTION (toy: random lanes gave 0.03 s mean departDelay and 32–35 % per lane; `"best"` gave 201 s and 0 % on one lane). (e) ASSUMPTION tolerances (toy: EIDM exceeded the lane maximum by up to 2.1 km/h with sigma = 0) |
| Caveat | R-b did not discriminate in the archive (IDM, EIDM and W99 all gave waves of −14 to −19 km/h; ARCH STATUS.md:69). It is necessary, never presented as evidence of realism |
| Reported | wave period τ (4–20 min for wide jams, Schönhof & Helbing 2007 [VERIFY]; IDM-family periods come out too short, Treiber & Kesting 2010); stop-and-go cycles per vehicle-km (20/50 and 30/60 km/h); spillback to the entry (share of runs, origin-queue vehicle-hours); censored share; HDV extreme-deceleration rate per 10⁴ vehicle-km (P0-R7); lane 1 vs outer-lane flows; auxiliary-lane speeds and waiting counts; **S, T (at 1.5 and 3.0 s, at speed and at all speeds), TIT, h_all and h in B0 at λ_c and λ_2 (P0-R10)**; **the Lagrangian wave-period row** T_L = τ · \|c\| / (v̄_cong + \|c\|) at λ_c, λ_c + 0.1 (G3's B0 runs) and λ_2, next to the S kernel's pass band ([AUTHOR A58]) |
| Seeds, n | 7,400,040–059, n = 20 per level |
| On FAIL | (a), (b), or (c) at λ_c: **STOP P0** → §9.2. (c) at λ_2 ≤ λ_c + 0.1: a P0 FAIL. (c) at λ_2 > λ_c + 0.1: λ_2 and every grid level ≥ λ_2 are **plant-invalid** (kept in the operating map with their B0 and B1 rows, never tuned, never in the band; stated prominently when λ_2 = 1.0); P0 does not FAIL on it. (d): the next insertion configuration of the §2.2 order (not a plant-model variant; it counts in k); C1–C11 except C0′ re-run on 002–009 ([AUTHOR A16d]), then G1 and G2 re-run on fresh seeds, because insertion changes the realised inflow. (e): vType code fix; G2 re-run on fresh seeds |

### 4.6 P0-G3 Uncommanded CAV artefacts (B1 vs the same vehicles in B0)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Do uncommanded CAVs (B1) drive without simulation artefacts, judged against the same vehicles driven by humans in B0? |
| Levels | **λ_c and λ_c + 0.1** (gated; both below the plant-invalid line by construction, and the levels of C4). λ_2 is run and reported: its B1 runs also serve G5 and P0-R2 |
| Measurement | B1 vs B0 on the same seeds and inflow files. For the CAV-flagged vehicles (same ids in both arms): severe-deceleration events, at-cap stretches and extreme events (definitions [AUTHOR A28], §2.7), and hard-brake events at −3 m/s² (V36), each per 1,000 km of those vehicles (planned km), with onset speed and location; teleports; collisions involving a CAV; CAV free-flow speed |
| Statistic | pooled rate ratio R = (Σ events in B1 / Σ km) / (Σ events of the same vehicles in B0 / Σ km), over the seeds of that level **on which the B0 run is valid with 0 health FAIL**, per level; 95 % percentile bootstrap CI over those seeds (§8.5) |
| PASS (all) | (a) on every seed whose B0 run is clean: the B1 run is valid with 0 health FAIL and 0 collisions involving a CAV (a B1 failure on a seed where B0 also failed is a plant artefact: reported, not counted against the CAV model); (b) at both gated levels: **R ≤ 1.0** (point estimate) **and** its 95 % bootstrap upper bound ≤ **1.2** (ASSUMPTION margin; a point rule alone passes a model with true R slightly above 1 on noise, especially after sequential looks); (c) CAV median free-flow speed within ±1 km/h of 100 km/h |
| Rationale | ASSUMPTION: an uncommanded automated vehicle should not brake harder than the human it replaces. **The yardstick depends on the HDV model:** under CD4 (Krauss) severe events in B0 should come mainly from emergencies, so R's denominator can be small, and under CD3 (higher sigmaerror) it grows (an inference from the model structure, [VERIFY] on the C0′ runs); the C0′ screen requires ≥ 20 such B0 events ([AUTHOR A65]), and C4 reports the pooled B0 count per level. Otherwise the V25 constraint (measured vs B1), the safety co-primary and the energy result would rest on a model artefact (ARCH STATUS #14; round3_tm21_protocol.md). Production ACC standards bound automatic deceleration (ISO 15622 [VERIFY]; not read, no value cited) |
| Expected | **A0 is expected to FAIL** the pre-screen or (b) (§0.4). The ladder A0–A3 and the ACC futility stop F4 (§2.6) exist for this reason |
| Reported | R₃ = the same ratio for −3 m/s² hard-brake events (the V36 threshold): **if R₃ > 1 + y at either gated level, band criterion (f′) is activated** (§6.8, [AUTHOR A14]); extreme events; at-cap stretches; the lane-end and speed-at-onset splits; share of severe events within 1 s of a leader change (cut-in attribution; toy b_diag.py: 67 %); median CAV time headway in steady following; the platoon and approach tests of the chosen candidate; B1 vs B0 relative ΔDl, ΔC and Δenergy with CIs; the number of ACC candidates screened and checked. **New in v2**, for the CAV-flagged vehicles and for all vehicles: T (as followers), TET at 1.5 s and h_all, B1 vs B0; **r_S and r_T of B1 vs B0** (the automation effect on the co-primaries, P0-R13) |
| Flags (not gates) | \|ΔDl(B1 vs B0)\| > 20 % at either level: shown to the author before P1 (archive: uncommanded CACC at 25 % gave +46 to +93 % delay, ARCH round4_th.md). **New:** a pooled ratio of CAV-follower T in B1 vs B0 above 1.5 (ASSUMPTION; it signals that ACC car-following, not control, will dominate T). Either can trigger nothing except a dated deviation note with a fresh-seed G3 re-run ([AUTHOR A16c]). Never credited to control (ARCH round3_tm21_protocol.md:59) |
| Seeds, n | 7,400,040–059, n = 20 per level (B0 at λ_c shared with G2; B0 and B1 at λ_c + 0.1 are 40 extra runs) |
| On FAIL | the next surviving ACC candidate of the C4 order (pre-screen passed, calibration R ≤ 1.25 at both levels), on fresh seeds (new range, [AUTHOR A16c]); only G3 and G5 (and G6, which reads the G3 B0 runs only, so it is not re-run) are re-run, because G1 and G2 are B0-only. Each such look counts in k (§9.2). Candidates exhausted → [AUTHOR A11]: P0 KILL, or a new CAV base model under a new protocol (the plant-variant ladder changes the HDV plant, not the CAV model) |

### 4.7 P0-G4 Energy plausibility (deterministic)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Are energy levels, their speed trend, and their idle, acceleration and braking behaviour plausible per powertrain? |
| Measurement | One vehicle per class, alone on a flat 10-km test edge (no inflow; non-project seed 1): (i) cruise at v ∈ {10, 20, …, 120} km/h (trucks 10–90): mean over 120 s after 120 s of settling; (ii) idle at v = 0 for 120 s; (iii) 0 → 100 km/h at +1 m/s² (trucks 0 → 90); (iv) 100 → 0 km/h at −1 m/s², and the 100 → 50 km/h braking test. Tank- or battery-to-wheel kWh (§3.1 conversion). Code checks: Σ getter × 0.2 s equals the tripinfo totals within 0.1 % (toy f_emission.py: equal) |
| PASS (all) | (1) At 80, 90 and 100 km/h: petrol, diesel and PHEV (P) in **[40, 70] kWh/100 km**; BEV and PHEV (El) in **[11, 21] kWh/100 km**. (2) Every car class: energy per km at 100 km/h > at 80 km/h. (3) Idle: a finite rate, ≥ 0, for every class. (4) Cruise at 10–40 km/h: finite and > 0 per km. (5) **Acceleration floor** (non-circular physics, ASSUMPTION road-load values stated in Addendum B): energy over the 0 → 100 km/h ramp ≥ ½·m·v² + the road-load work over the ramp, with m from the vType XML; gated for petrol, diesel, PHEV (P) and trucks; for BEV and PHEV (El) per [AUTHOR A33] (recommended: report-only, because HBEFA4 PC_BEV is known to under-charge acceleration: 184.8 Wh for 0 → 30 m/s against about 225 Wh of kinetic energy for an assumed 1.8 t car, literature_v0.md:336-342). (6) Braking: BEV electricity < 0 while braking (net of regeneration, V53), and the energy recovered over (iv) ≤ the kinetic energy at the start of braking. (7) Truck model: reproduces `truck_energy_check_v0.md` (40 t road-load + Willans: 221 / 238 / 254 g/km at 60 / 80 / 90 km/h) within ±1 % (a code-vs-specification check; its offset was calibrated to one measured point), rises from 60 to 90 km/h, and gives 0 fuel when P < 0 through cycle (iii)–(iv). The truck sensitivity models reproduce their sources within ±1 %: COPERT 215 / 216 / 248* g/km at 60 / 80 / 90 km/h (`truck_energy_check_v0.md`:13; * extrapolated above its 80 km/h validity) and the TII OGV2 fuel function L = a/v + b + c·v + d·v² (TII PAG Unit 6.11, Tables 6.11.13–14, read: a = 5.0715, b = 0.34664, c = −0.0027069, d = 0.000014479; L in litre/km, v in km/h) = 268 / 239 / 231 g/km at 60 / 80 / 90 km/h (my arithmetic, diesel 836 kg/m³; that OGV2 is the class of a 40 t articulated vehicle is [VERIFY]). (8) 0 NaN or ±∞ in any P0 run |
| Sources | ASSUMPTION bands anchored on: the Irish fuel function (TII PAG Unit 6.11 / DoT TAF Module 8 Table 10, read): petrol 45.5–49.2, diesel 52.0–55.7 kWh/100 km at 80–100 km/h (my arithmetic, RED energy contents; an average-speed function, not steady cruise); BEV 17.5–21.1 kWh/100 km at 110 km/h from manufacturer highway data (Behrisch, Flötteröd & Wagner 2024, SUMO Conf. Proc. 5, read; data origin [VERIFY]); road-load physics ≈ 12–15 kWh/100 km at 80–100 km/h (my arithmetic, assumed parameters). [AUTHOR A24] |
| Disclosure | The cruise bands (1) were proposed **after** SUMO's own cruise values had been seen (literature_v0.md F7; toy f_emission.py at 100 km/h: petrol ≈ 53, diesel ≈ 66, PHEV (P) ≈ 55, BEV 14.8, PHEV (El) 18.9 kWh/100 km, my arithmetic). They therefore catch gross errors (units, class strings, conversions), not model bias. HBEFA4 diesel near the top of the band is a known model trait (literature_v0.md F7), not a plant bug. Rows (3)–(6) were written before any run on the built vTypes; the HBEFA4 BEV acceleration figure was already known (literature_v0.md F7) and is the reason for [AUTHOR A33] |
| Disclosed model property | PHEV (El) recovers nothing while braking in HBEFA4, BEV does (toy f_emission.py). Smoothing therefore saves more for PHEV (El) and ICE than for BEVs: a model property, not a controller effect. Energy is reported per powertrain |
| Output | the table e_class(u) at 10–120 km/h (10-km/h steps, linear interpolation between them), used by the report-only ceiling (§6.4); the idle rate per class, used by the origin-queue imputation row (§6.12) |
| Seeds, n | none (deterministic) |
| On FAIL | a disclosed model or parameter fix, never a silent rescale (for BEV and PHEV (El), per [AUTHOR A33]); G4 re-run only (no other P0 gate depends on energy) |

### 4.8 P0-G5 Step-size sensitivity (paired by seed)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Is the 0.2-s plant converged enough that conclusions do not hinge on the step? |
| Measurement | B0 and B1 triangle at **λ_c and λ_2** at **0.1 s** on seeds 040–059, paired by seed with the 0.2-s runs of G2/G3. Both steps use the same inflow and attribute manifests (arrivals on the 0.2-s grid are valid at 0.1 s). SUMO's own random draws differ between steps, as they differ between arms (§2.3); pairing removes the inflow variance only. T's exposure sums use the step of the run (τ_sc = 0.1 or 0.2 s) |
| Statistic | J1 = per-run mean door-to-door time of the cohort; J2 = triangle discharge at BN*, on the seeds with an active onset at BN* in both runs (≥ 5 such seeds at a level, else J2 is not computable there); **J3 = S**; **J4 = T** (reported). ΔJ = median_s(J_0.1,s − J_0.2,s) / median_s(J_0.2,s), with a 95 % percentile bootstrap CI |
| PASS | form per [AUTHOR A20] (point estimate, V65): \|ΔJ\| ≤ **5 %** for J1, J2 **and J3**, in both arms, at λ_c, and at λ_2 if λ_2 is admissible (a plant-invalid λ_2 is reported only); J2 computable at ≥ 1 gated level. A CI reaching beyond ±5 % is a FLAG, disclosed in every report. **J4 (T) is reported, not gated**: TET counts threshold crossings of a ratio and is expected to be more step-sensitive and noisier than a mean, so a gate on it at n = 20 could force the move to 0.1 s on noise alone; \|ΔJ4\| > 10 % (ASSUMPTION) is a FLAG |
| Source | lab convention (ARCH day_pattern_metanet_protocol_v3.md:439), no published source. The SUMO ACC docs recommend steps below 1 s, e.g. 0.1 s [VERIFY, summarising fetch]. The archive found IDM door-to-door times of 356 / 392 / 403 s at 0.5 / 0.2 / 0.1 s (ARCH round3_tm21_protocol.md:121-135): a true step effect of about 3 % between 0.2 and 0.1 s, inside the tolerance; a realism verdict is step-specific |
| Reported | censored share at each step (censoring at 11,700 s compresses J1 differences at over-saturated levels); onset and origin shares at both steps (the METANET step check FAILed on event classification although J moved 0.23 %; ARCH day_pattern_decisions_v0.md:53); CAV severe-event ratio; wave speed; the paired SD of each ΔJ |
| Seeds, n | 7,400,040–059, n = 20 per arm and level (80 runs at about twice the cost) |
| On FAIL | as decided in [AUTHOR A20] (a): move to 0.1 s: C1–C11 except C0′ re-run on 002–009 at 0.1 s ([AUTHOR A16d]), a new golden hash, then all of P0 on fresh seeds ([AUTHOR A16c]); no further step check at 0.1 s (disclosed); about twice the CPU for everything after. It counts in k |

### 4.9 Report-only rows (do not block P0)
- **P0-R1 Stop-and-go presence:** share of B0 runs per level with ≥ 1 stop-and-go cycle per 10 vehicle-km on the mainline (20/50 and 30/60 km/h), or with a valid wave-speed estimate. Wave-damping rules need waves; the band rule decides whether control is worth it. The same share is reported for every band point, and it sets the claim label of [AUTHOR A60].
- **P0-R2 Automation effect (B1 vs B0):** paired median relative difference of Dl, C and energy at λ_c, λ_c + 0.1 and λ_2, with 95 % CIs; never credited to control. It is also the first measurement of the speed-compliance effect of [AUTHOR A13] (CAVs at 100 km/h vs their B0 speed factors), disclosed before seed 7,400,100. The G3 flag applies.
- **P0-R3 ACC behaviour:** median time headway of CAVs in steady car-following; the platoon and approach tests of every screened candidate; CAV free-flow speed.
- **P0-R4 Wave period** τ per level.
- **P0-R5 Irish context:** pre-breakdown flows per lane vs the TII 2012 values (lane means 1,580 / 1,660 / 1,960 veh/h for lanes 1 / 2 / 3; per-site range 1,400–2,300 veh/h; practical capacity of an unmanaged lane "measured to be between 1,700 PCU’s per hour to 1,800 PCU’s per hour" in §7.1 and 1,715 / 1,862 / 1,824 pcu/h for lanes 1 / 2 / 3 in Table 5.2; read in full). Lane 1 is expected lowest. Context only: the TII values are breakdown flows from 2012, before the eMOS VSL, not queue discharge.
- **P0-R6 Drain and spillback:** censored share and origin-queue vehicle-hours per level (G2, later P2 stage 1). They feed the band guards (§6.8(g)), not P0.
- **P0-R7 HDV extreme deceleration** (a plant diagnostic, not the V11 metric): rate per 10⁴ vehicle-km in B0; a flag to the author if > 1 (ASSUMPTION: braking at about 0.9 g at speed is a near-crash).
- **P0-R8 Plant energy:** B0 kWh per vehicle-km per category and powertrain at λ_c and λ_2; share of vehicle-steps with \|a\| > 0.6 m/s² per arm (the HBEFA slope-derived range, unverified for HBEFA4; literature_v0.md critic C).
- **P0-R9 Zipper and lane ends:** per-lane discharge at the BN-LD section with the metronome FLAG (§2.7); lane 0's discharge against the floor; auxiliary-lane speeds and waiting counts at merge1 and merge2.
- **P0-R10 Co-primary base rates:** S, T at both thresholds and both speed conditions, TIT, DRAC exposure, conflict episodes, h_all and h, per arm (B0, B1) and level (λ_sub, λ_c, λ_c + 0.1, λ_2); the share of runs with T = 0; the C9 outcome.
- **P0-R11 SSM cross-check** (C11) and the share of conflicts of type 7 (merging, ego follows) and {4, 8} (adjacent lanes). **Lane use:** per-lane flow shares at 12,750, 14,790 and 15,100 m in B0 and B1, in active minutes. A FLAG if lane 2's share at 14,790 m is < 0.25 or > 0.45 (ASSUMPTION). LM-B assumes that lane 2, the only lane that continues alone past the zipper, is under-used; the zipper asymmetry in the mechanics toy (lane 2's continuation about 2,200–2,300 veh/h against 1,100–1,400 veh/h on the zipper output lane) is a mechanical lever for lane balancing whose realism is not established (evidence: mechanics).
- **P0-R12 Room rows** per level: 1 − S_floor / median S_B0 and 1 − T_floor / median T_B0. Labelled approximations, not bounds; they play for S and T the role v1's ceiling plays for C (A5).
- **P0-R13 Co-primary automation effect** (B1 vs B0) at λ_c, λ_c + 0.1 and λ_2, for S and T, with CIs; never credited to control.

### 4.10 P0 verdict
**P0 PASS** iff P0-B, G1 (per [AUTHOR A42]), G2, G3, G4, G5 and **G6** all PASS (intersection-union), with G1 evaluated first and alone. **P0-GI never fails P0**: it decides only which INC points enter P1 and P2. The P0 report lists every calibration choice with its seeds, every run in the ledger, the values against each criterion, the burned seeds, every deviation, both outcomes of any re-analysis, and the expected outcome of §0.4 next to the result. **Its verdict sentence states k, k_cal (§9.2), the TTC\* in force, the INC points kept, and the A42 limitation sentence if it applies**, and the thesis text repeats them.

### 4.11 P0-G6 Co-primary informativeness (new; gated; [AUTHOR A53])

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Do S and T carry congestion-related information in B0 at the levels where claims will be made? |
| Measurement | B0 on 040–059 at λ_c (the G2 runs) and λ_c + 0.1 (the G3 runs); S_floor and T_floor from C9 (B0 at λ_sub on 002–009); T at the TTC\* in force after C9 |
| PASS (all) | (a) median S_B0(λ_c + 0.1) ≥ **1.5 × S_floor**; (b) T_B0 > 0 in ≥ **18 of 20** runs at λ_c + 0.1, **and** median T_B0(λ_c + 0.1) ≥ **3 × T_floor**, **and** a median of ≥ **30 conflict episodes** per run; (c) the same conditions at λ_c, report-only |
| Rationale | ASSUMPTION factors, chosen from the rigor toy (not data) as about half of what a light queue showed: S was 2.9 × the free-flow value in a light queue and 5.3 × in a heavy one (300-s boxcar; 2.9 × and 5.2 × with the Gaussian trend, review toy); T at 3.0 s 14 × and 23 ×; T at 1.5 s fails (b), because it was higher in free flow than in a heavy queue. The ≥ 18/20 rule keeps the median convention defined. The episode count (ASSUMPTION) stops a T dominated by one to three conflict pairs per run from passing, so that a 10 % change is not a single episode. **It is a floor, not a noise bound** (v2 review ST3): T sums episode durations, so its per-run CV is about √((1 + c_d²)/N) for N episodes with duration CV c_d, i.e. 0.26 at N = 30 and c_d = 1 (ASSUMPTION; the Poisson count alone gives 0.18); a paired relative SD of T near 0.2–0.37 would follow if episode noise were independent between arms (my arithmetic). F3Z's cohort of about 12,440 vehicles should give far more than 30 episodes per run at the claim levels, and the rigor toy's paired relative SD of TET at 3.0 s (harm70 vs none, 3 seeds) was about 0.077. c_d is measured in C9, and T's noise enters the planning only through the measured σ_plan,1(T) and σ_plan,2(T) (§5, §6.6, [AUTHOR A68]) |
| Reported | c_d and the episode count per run; the B0 wave-presence share (P0-R1) at both levels; the Lagrangian wave-period row ([AUTHOR A58]). G6 certifies one level only; every tuned and INC point gets its own informativeness check at the held-out stage ([AUTHOR A59], §6.8 (a′)) |
| Seeds, n | 040–059, n = 20 per level (no extra runs) |
| On FAIL | **STOP** ([AUTHOR A53]): no V74 claim is measurable on this plant. No plant change may repair a metric (principle 14). The A37 fork, if chosen, has already been spent in C9. A new metric needs a new protocol |

### 4.12 P0-GI Incident plant check (new; gated for the INC regime only; V75, [AUTHOR A43])

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Does the obstacle reproduce a one-of-three-lanes blockage, delivered identically to every arm, without artefacts, at each INC point that passed C10? |
| Measurement | B0 and B1 with INC at the passing points among I1, I2 and I3, on 040–059 (the same seeds as G2/G3, so every INC run pairs with a no-incident run of the same seed and level) |
| PASS per INC point (all) | (a) median F_inc(B0) ∈ **[0.30, 0.55]** with ≥ 5 valid runs (else not computable → FAIL); (b) the incident binds in ≥ **16 of 20** B0 runs; (c) admissibility: B0 invalid share ≤ 5 % (≤ 1 of 20; incident non-delivery included); (d) **integrity**: in each arm, ≤ **1 of 20** runs with t_blk outside ±15 s, lane and position exact in every run, **and** the median over seeds of \|t_blk(B1) − t_blk(B0)\| ≤ **5 s** (ASSUMPTION: the arm-dependence of the block start stays small against 900 s) |
| Sources | (a) one of three lanes blocked, against normal queue discharge: Knoop, Hoogendoorn & Adams 2009, EJTIR 9(4):363-379, mean 0.36 (SD 0.14, n = 21) (full text, evidence: incident; spot-checked); earlier values in their Table 1: 0.35–0.53; HCM 2010 Exhibit 10-17: 0.49 [VERIFY, via the FHWA ATDM guide]. The band [0.30, 0.55] is an ASSUMPTION built from them; US field values of 0.70–0.83 exist (Kansas City, Maryland/Virginia; snippets only [VERIFY]) and are not adopted. Toy (not F3Z): an outer-lane stopped vehicle gave F = 0.37–0.48 with no rubbernecking layer |
| Reported | S, T, h_all, Dl and C in cohort_I and in the full cohort for B0 and B1, with the X1/X2-bound and late-arrival subgroups; queue reach (does it reach Z2 at 11–12 km?); the incident − no-incident contrast per arm (cohort_I in both runs); teleport locations; the maximum waiting time behind the obstacle per run and the count above 200 s ([AUTHOR A64]); t_blk distributions per arm; T's obstacle/other split |
| Power | binding ≥ 16/20: P(FAIL) = 0.003 at a true binding share of 0.95, 0.04 at 0.90; admissibility ≤ 1/20: P(PASS) = 0.94 / 0.74 / 0.52 at a true invalid rate of 2 / 5 / 8 % (the C10 waiting-time screen of [AUTHOR A64] is meant to keep knife-edge points away from the 8 % case); integrity ≤ 1/20 out of tolerance: P(PASS) = 0.94 at 2 %, 0.74 at 5 % (binomial, my arithmetic) |
| Seeds, n | 040–059, n = 20 per INC point and arm (≤ 120 episodes) |
| On FAIL | that INC point is **not run** in P1 or P2 (kept in the map with its B0 and B1 rows, disclosed). If I2 fails, the tuned INC point is the passing point nearest λ_c (ties to the higher); if every point fails, the INC regime is dropped (disclosed; V75 not achievable on this plant). An integrity failure (d) caused by code is fixed and GI re-run on re-round seeds; one caused by traffic triggers the pre-registered fallback insertion (§2.4b), tested in B16 and then GI on re-round seeds. **Not a P0 FAIL.** C10 is never re-opened on check seeds |

---

## 5 P1 tool checks T0–T3 (seeds 7,400,060–089, n = 30, fresh)

### 5.0 What is built, levels and arms
- **Built only after P0 PASS:** `exec` with lane calls, the mask, the ban, the mode set at a first lane request and the zone-exit cancel (§2.5); a minimal `v2x` (zone summaries with per-lane fields, per-lane CAV radar spacing [AUTHOR A47], V2V lists with relative lanes, radar gap and closing speed, side/rear gaps and `couldChangeLane(±1)` bits [A47], the incident message); the arms R4, **R-KEEP** (u = 0, i.e. hold the entry target; ℓ = keep) and **R-LANE** (u = 0; ℓ = left at every boundary, masked); the command log.
- **P1-B acceptance tests** (non-project seeds, no plant outcome; all must pass before seed 060): `exec` ramp, hold, one speed call per boundary, clip, entry rounding reached by a `slowDown` ramp (never a `setSpeed` step), release; **negative control:** a once-only `slowDown` variant must FAIL the hold test; the lane-call unit tests (mask, including the predicted-position rule at the ban start and at each zone end, ban, at most one lane call per boundary, mode set at a first request and restored at exit, cancel at exit); **lane negative control:** an `exec` without the mask must send an invalid target to SUMO, and the test must catch it; `v2x` outputs equal values recomputed from the trace; an empty lane gives n = 0 plus a fallback flag; the side-feasibility bits equal `couldChangeLane(id, ±1)` read in the same snapshot [VERIFY that `couldChangeLane` applies the same gap test as execution under 1878; if not, the bits are labelled "SUMO feasibility estimate"]; **contract test:** an injected HDV-only field and a clock field never reach an arm (V27, V48; `interfaces.md` §7), and the `lane` spec (incident message included) reaches only the arms that declare it; R4, R-KEEP and R-LANE logic; the command log enters the trace hash; **timing test:** the process CPU time per episode of R4 and R-LANE relative to B1 on a fixed micro-batch, which sets their CPU-time caps (§2.6 C8).
- **Levels** (from P0-C and P0-G2, calibration and check seeds only): **λ_sub** for T0b and T0f(a). **ℓ₁** = λ_c; **ℓ₂** and **ℓ₃** = the P2 grid points nearest λ_c + 0.1 and λ_c + 0.2. A P1 level that is plant-invalid under P0-G2(c) is replaced by the highest admissible grid point below it that is not already a P1 level (in practice ℓ₃ → λ_c + 0.05). All three are P2 grid points (§6.2). Every level ≤ λ_c + 0.1 is admissible by construction (a G2(c) failure at or below λ_c + 0.1 is a P0 FAIL); a P1 level above λ_c + 0.1 has its admissibility re-checked in T1a. **Plus the INC point I2** (= ℓ₁ with the incident) if it passed P0-GI; otherwise the tuned INC point of §6.2.
- **Arms:** B0, B1, R4(70) and R4(80) at ℓ₁, ℓ₂, ℓ₃ and I2; B1 and R4(70) at λ_sub (T0b); R-KEEP and R-LANE at λ_sub and at ℓ₂ (T0f), plus R-KEEP and R-LANE at ℓ₂ re-run with the SSM device on seeds 060–064 (10 runs, report-only: the T0f SSM rows; the device does not change the trace, B14). R4(c) is a constant CAV target c, with u = sign(c − target) at each boundary and ℓ = keep. Commands act from the first second of the episode, before any onset: the archive's first BN4 T0 applied control after breakdown and tested the wrong thing (ARCH t1_bn4_plant_checks_protocol.md:76-98).
- Addendum B (VoT) is frozen before seed 7,400,000. **Addendum C** (R1–R3 parameter spaces, the lane modules, the observation specs incl. the `lane` spec and the incident-message format, §6.3) is committed before seed 060, before any commanding arm runs on a project seed.
- Every P1 batch shuffles its job order across arms and scenarios (P0-B12), so CPU-guard pauses cannot follow arm order.

### T0a Command execution, speed part (gated; deterministic micro-scenarios on the built F3Z, no inflow, non-project seed 1)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Are speed commands carried out exactly as V26/V36 specify, with F3Z's chosen ACC and HDV vTypes? Does SUMO release the vehicle between boundaries? |
| Scenarios | (1) a lone CAV in Z1 given −1, +1, then 0 for ≥ 10 boundaries; (2) a CAV behind a scripted HDV leader at 60 km/h, commanded to 90 km/h; (3) a CAV crossing Z1 → the 2,000-m gap that contains merge 2 → Z2 (release, re-entry rounding), and a CAV leaving Z2 at 50 km/h and entering Z3 through the 2,000-m gap 12,000–14,000 m (V71); (4) commands beyond [50, 100] km/h; (5) a new command during a hold; (6) zone entries at speeds 0.1, 1.2, 2.4, 2.6, 3.9 and 4.9 km/h above a multiple of 5 km/h, each with u = −1, 0 and +1 at the first boundary; (7) a CAV changing lanes (a commanded change in v2, and an autonomous one) during a ramp and during a hold; (8) negative control: once-only `slowDown` |
| PASS (all) | (1) ramp acceleration **±0.694 ± 0.005 m/s²** at every ramp step of a ±5 km/h command when unconstrained (5 km/h over 2 s = 0.6944 m/s²; toy A1, A5), except the first post-entry ramp, which is \|Δv\| / 2 s ± 0.005 m/s² with \|Δv\| ≤ 7.5 km/h (≤ 1.04 m/s²); (2) \|v − target\| ≤ 0.01 km/h at boundary + 2.0 s (± one step); (3) **hold:** unconstrained, ≥ 20 s, max \|v − target\| ≤ **0.01 km/h** and no step with a > +0.01 m/s² (no release between boundaries; toy A3: 0.000000 km/h); (4) a new command during a hold replaces it with a ramp (toy A7); (5) **exactly one speed call and at most one lane call per CAV per boundary, and none between boundaries** (toy A2b), including at a zone entry while a release is in progress; (6) entry target = current speed rounded to 5 km/h and clipped, reached by a `slowDown` ramp; no target outside [50, 100]; every clip logged; (7) behind the slower leader: 0 collisions, the gap never below the car-following minimum, the command acts only as an upper bound (toy L1/L2); (8) unconstrained exec-induced deceleration never below −0.70 m/s² (−1.05 m/s² on the first post-entry ramp); (9) release issued ≤ 2 s after zone exit, after the lane cancel and mode restore; the release acceleration is logged ([AUTHOR A10] (a)); (10) **0 exec-induced steps below −3 m/s²** in every scenario (a V36 hard brake must never come from `exec`); (11) during a lane change the target is kept and (2)/(3) hold on the new lane; (12) the negative control FAILs criterion (3) (toy a_A1_once_cav.csv: after the once-only call, the first acceleration is logged 2.4 s after the call, 0.4 s after the ramp ends, back towards 100 km/h at +2.6 m/s²) |
| On FAIL | code fix; no project seed consumed. Not a KILL |

### T0b Authority on speed and density (gated, directional)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Do CAV commands change the speed and density of **all** traffic in each zone, in the pre-registered direction? |
