| Arms, level | B1 vs R4(70) at λ_sub (sub-critical: flow is conserved, so a lower cap must lower speed and raise density) |
| Measurement | per zone over the scored window: space-mean speed of all vehicles = Σ distance / Σ time in the zone; density = Σ time in the zone / (T · L_Z · lanes) (veh/km/lane) |
| Statistic | paired median relative difference vs B1, 95 % percentile bootstrap CI |
| PASS | in **each** of Z1, Z2, Z3 (intersection-union): speed Δ ≤ **−5 %** with CI upper bound < 0, **and** density Δ ≥ **+5 %** with CI lower bound > 0 |
| Rationale | 5 %: archive convention (ARCH t1_bn4_plant_checks_protocol.md:54-55), no published source. Directional, because a sign-agnostic T0 once passed a harmful actuator (ARCH t1_bn4_plant_checks.md:59-68). Sub-critical, because a cap applied inside a queue does not bind. Size check (my arithmetic, not a prediction): if HDVs were unaffected, 26 % of vehicles at 70 instead of about 100 km/h would cut the space-mean speed by about 10 % |
| Seeds, n | 060–089, n = 30 (half-width ≈ 4.5 % at σ = 0.10; §8.7) |

### T0c Authority at the bottleneck, on the claim metrics (gated, two-sided)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Can commands change outcomes where the breakdown happens (either sign)? |
| Arms, level | B1 vs R4(70) at the highest P1 level that is admissible after T1a (normally ℓ₃; all P1 levels are run for T1b) |
| Statistic | paired median relative differences of (i) Dl, (ii) served throughput in the peak hour at BN*'s section, (iii) **S** and (iv) **T**; Bonferroni over the four (α = 0.0125 each, z = 2.498) |
| PASS | any of: \|ΔDl\| ≥ 5 %, \|Δ throughput\| ≥ 3 %, \|ΔS\| ≥ 5 %, \|ΔT\| ≥ 5 %, with its 98.75 % CI excluding 0 (3 % for throughput: ASSUMPTION; throughput moves less than delay) |
| Seeds, n | 060–089, n = 30 |

### T0d Sensing and command integrity (gated, exact, on every P1 run)
Zone summaries (with per-lane fields and per-lane CAV radar spacing) and V2V lists (the 5 nearest CAVs ahead within 300 m with their relative lanes, plus the count and mean speed of all heard ahead; V50) equal the values recomputed from the trace; radar gap and closing speed, side/rear gaps and the side-feasibility bits equal the trace values; the incident message (content, and availability from t_blk + 60 s) equals the trace; own lane, lane masks, time since the last lane change and the pending-request flag equal the trace; the contract-test log shows no field outside each arm's declared spec (the `lane` spec included); **0** hold violations (v > target + 0.1 km/h at a hold step, ramps and leader-constrained steps excluded); no target below 50 km/h; masked actions logged; 0 lane requests reaching SUMO with a predicted position at or beyond the ban start or the zone end (checked at the issue position and speed); 0 commanded changes in the ban segment or after a zone end; mode 1621 restored at every zone exit where it was changed. Reported: share of CAV-boundaries at 50 and at 100 km/h per arm (V47); share of commanded CAV-steps more than 5 km/h below target (leader-constrained).

**On T0b or T0c FAIL:** the speed actuator does not bind on F3Z. The consequence is decided in [AUTHOR A34] (decided (b), V65): one disclosed zone move under V56, re-run on fresh seeds from [AUTHOR A16c], then a KILL of the CAV-only actuator on F3Z if T0b or T0c FAILs again. A fix found after the look cannot turn this FAIL into a PASS without a fresh-seed re-run (§9.3). **On T0d FAIL:** code fix, re-verified on replicates of 5 P1 seeds; the P1 effect results stand, because R4, R-KEEP and R-LANE read no V2X field.

### T0e Lane-command execution (new; gated; deterministic micro-scenarios on the built F3Z with scripted vehicles, chosen ACC and HDV vTypes, non-project seeds)

| # | Scenario | PASS |
|---|---|---|
| (a) | Free road: left and right requests from every valid lane (0→1, 1→2, 2→1, 1→0) in each zone at 60, 80 and 100 km/h (20 trials per zone and direction) | executed within 0.4 s in 100 % (toy: at the next step, +0.2 s), exactly one lane per request |
| (b) | Blocked target: a vehicle abreast on the target lane for the whole 2-s window, under M_cmd | no execution while `couldChangeLane` is False; **the abreast vehicle's acceleration trace is identical (to 10⁻⁹) to the same scenario without the request** (under priority 3 neighbours are not asked to adapt); and 0 neighbour steps below −3 m/s² within 3 s of any executed commanded change |
| (b′) | **Negative control:** scenario (b) under mode 1621 (TraCI priority 2) | (b)'s identity check **FAILs** (toy: the abreast vehicle braked to −4.5 m/s²), which shows that (b) detects mode leakage |
| (c) | CAVs bound for X2 in Z1 receive "left" at every boundary | 100 % exit at X2; 0 stops > 60 s within 50 m of any lane end; 0 teleports (strategic override; the mechanics toy stranded a CAV under strategic bits 1) |
| (d) | Requests with the front in [14,800, 15,000) m | 100 % masked; 0 commanded changes there |
| (d′) | **Requests issued just before a boundary** (v2 review SF1): a CAV at 100 km/h on lane 0 or 1 whose front is 1, 3 and 5 m before 14,800 m, and before 9,000 m (Z1 end) and 12,000 m (Z2 end), with "left" at that boundary; also at 55 m and 57 m before 14,800 m | masked whenever x + 2.0 · max(v, v_tgt) ≥ the boundary (here: at 1, 3, 5 and 55 m; not at 57 m); 0 lane changes after the boundary or inside [14,800, 15,000); the unmasked 57-m request, if executed, executes before 14,800 m |
| (e) | Zone exit with a pending request | at the first boundary after exit: the request is cancelled, mode 1621 is read back with `getLaneChangeMode`, then the speed is released; no TraCI-originated change after exit |
| (f) | Invalid targets (right from lane 0, left from lane 2, any request from an auxiliary lane) | masked to keep and logged; 0 SUMO lane calls issued |
| (g) | A **staged** cut-in ahead of an HDV at a TTC of 1.2 s: the scripted vehicle alone runs with lane-change mode 0 and is moved by `moveTo` to the adjacent lane (a measurement test, not an exec test, like B14; under M_cmd such a gap is never accepted) | own TTC of the new follower = 1.2 ± 0.05 s at the step after the change |
| (g′) | **What commanded changes look like to T** (v2 review MV-5): under M_cmd, a CAV requests "left" beside a following HDV whose gap is shrunk step by step, at Δv = 0, 3 and 6 m/s (follower faster) and 80 and 100 km/h | report the tightest gap SUMO accepts, and the new follower's TTC and time headway at the step after the change (a review estimate from SUMO's secure-gap rule puts the accepted TTC well above 3 s at motorway speeds, [VERIFY]); report-only, used by the short-headway row and the TTC-blindness guard (§3.4) |
| (h) | A speed and a lane command at the same boundary | both take effect; one speed call, one lane call (plus the mode call at a first request) |
| (i) | INC: CAVs evacuated from lane 0 in Z2, released at 12,000 m | re-entries into lane 0 before 13,000 m counted (report-only) |
| (j) | A request at a boundary followed by keep at the next | the request expires at its 2-s duration; nothing is re-issued |
| (k) | A zone with speed commands but no lane requests | per-step trace **bit-identical** to the v1 speed-only `exec` on the same scenario (the mode is set only at a first lane request) |
| (l) | Determinism | identical trace hash in two fresh processes |
| (m) | A request that expires unexecuted (v2 review SF8) | the pending-request flag is cleared at expiry and the command log marks the request "expired", distinct from an executed change, so that LM-B's no-repeat rule can count executed changes only (tested with the LM-B code in P2-B) |

**On FAIL:** code fix; no project seed is consumed; not a KILL. **Exception for (b), decided now:** if the identity check fails and a minimal reproduction with no project code shows that the cause is SUMO itself (LC2013 signalling neighbours under priority 3), (b)'s identity criterion is reported, and the gate falls back to its second criterion (0 neighbour steps below −3 m/s² within 3 s), disclosed; this is not a code FAIL.

### T0f Lane-command authority (new; gated, directional)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Do lane commands change lane use (a) in every zone where gaps exist, and (b) at the drop in congested traffic, where claims are made? |
| Arms, levels | R-LANE vs R-KEEP, paired by seed: **(a)** at λ_sub (sub-critical, gaps exist); **(b)** at **ℓ₂** (congested; a pure lane contrast, because the speed behaviour of the two arms is identical) |
| Measurement | (a) the share of CAVs on lane 2 at each zone's downstream end (8,990, 11,990 and 14,790 m; the last is the ban start), over the scored window; (b) at 14,790 m, **over congested minutes only** (1-min speed < 60 km/h at the 14,750-m probe, BN-LD's head probe; v2 review SF2, because over the whole window free-flow minutes, where R-LANE's "left" executes at the next step, could carry a PASS): (i) the share of **all** vehicles crossing in lane 2, (ii) the share of **CAVs** crossing in lane 2. A seed enters (b) only if both arms have ≥ 10 congested minutes (ASSUMPTION); (b) needs ≥ 15 such seeds; if fewer qualify at ℓ₂, R-LANE and R-KEEP are run once at ℓ₃ (if admissible; 60 runs) and (b) is evaluated there; if still fewer, (b) is not computable → FAIL (principle 7). Reported in every case: the same shares over free-flow minutes; CAV zipper merges per hour (CAVs passing BN-LD from lanes 0/1); the execution rate within 2 s in congested and free-flow minutes |
| Statistic | paired median difference in percentage points (pp), 95 % percentile bootstrap CI (§8.2) |
| PASS (all, intersection-union) | (a) in **each** of Z1, Z2, Z3: Δ ≥ **+20 pp** with CI lower bound > 0 (ASSUMPTION; about +60 % on an expected one-third baseline share); (b) on congested minutes, Δ(ii) ≥ **+10 pp** with CI lower bound > 0, **and** Δ(i) ≥ **+2 pp** with CI lower bound > 0 unless (i) is **not applicable**: (i) is not applicable, and only reported, when R-KEEP's lane-2 flow at 14,790 m in congested minutes is ≥ 95 % of its highest 5-min lane-2 flow in the run (median over the qualifying seeds; ASSUMPTION), because lane 2's continuation is then saturated and more CAVs in lane 2 can only displace HDVs (mechanics toy, review read of `toy/out/t4b.jsonl`: lane 2's continuation inside the queue 2,156–2,316 veh/h; not F3Z). ASSUMPTIONS: a lane actuator that moves less than this at the drop cannot plausibly reshape the lane balance at the zipper. The not-applicable clause loosens (i) and is listed in Appendix D's relaxations |
| Reported | execution rates per zone and level; neighbour hard braking; lane changes per vehicle-km (CAV, HDV); S, T and h_all for both arms; masked actions; the short-headway row (§3.1). **SSM rows** (v2 review MV-3; report-only): R-LANE and R-KEEP at ℓ₂ on 060–064 with the SSM device (§2.2): the share of SSM conflicts below TTC\* of types 7 or {4, 8}, which own same-lane TTC cannot see before a lane change, and the own/SSM TET ratio. **FLAG** when R-LANE's share exceeds R-KEEP's by > 5 pp or its own/SSM TET ratio is > 10 % lower (ASSUMPTIONS); the FLAG is printed beside every lane row and every band row of a lane-commanding arm |
| Seeds, n | 060–089, n = 30 per arm and level (120 runs; + 10 SSM runs; + 60 at ℓ₃ only if (b) needs them) |
| Sizes | (a) lane-share SD unknown; at a paired SD of 25 pp, the 95 % half-width ≈ 1.96 × 1.2533 × 25 / √30 ≈ 11 pp, so a true +30 pp effect clears the 20-pp bar with its CI; (b) at an assumed SD of 4 pp (unmeasured), the half-width ≈ 1.8 pp (my arithmetic) |
| On FAIL | [AUTHOR A46], decided now (recommended (a)): **lane commands are removed from every arm** for P2 and the learning phases on this plant, disclosed; V76 reverts to V3 here; the lane fields leave the observation specs (Addendum C re-issued before seed 100); P1 continues. A non-binding lane actuator is never switched to an urgent mode after the look unless the author chooses option (b) now |

### T0g Incident integrity across arms (new; gated)
On every P1 INC run (B0, B1, R4(70), R4(80) at the tuned INC point):
- **PASS (all):** in each arm, ≤ **1 of 30** runs with t_blk outside ±15 s; lane and position exact in every run; the median over seeds of \|t_blk(A) − t_blk(B0)\| ≤ **5 s** for each arm A (ASSUMPTION).
- Reported: the distribution of t_blk − T_on per arm, the paired differences, teleports and their locations.
- **On FAIL:** if the cause is code, an injector fix, then the P1 INC runs are re-run on re-round seeds (§9.3), never on the same seeds. If the cause is traffic (arm-dependent insertion or approach), the pre-registered **fallback insertion** (§2.4b) replaces the injector, after its B16 case passes, and P0-GI and the P1 INC runs are re-run on re-round seeds. If the fallback also fails, the INC regime is dropped (disclosed).

### T1a Discharge and artefacts in the experiment scenario, on fresh seeds (gated)

|---|---|
| Question | Does the triangle scenario break down at BN*, with a discharge consistent with P0 (and a drop per [AUTHOR A42]), without artefacts, on seeds that P0 never saw? (Archive: onset shares drifted between calibration and check seeds) |
| Measurement | B0 at ℓ₁, ℓ₂ and ℓ₃ (no-incident runs only). BN* is the bottleneck fixed in calibration; the other bottlenecks are reported separately |
| PASS (all) | (i) **realism artefact gate** at the P1 levels ≤ λ_c + 0.1 (normally ℓ₁ and ℓ₂): 0 teleports, 0 invalid B0 runs and ≤ 2 collisions in total over their runs; (ii) **admissibility** at the P1 levels above λ_c + 0.1 (normally ℓ₃; §8.8: B0 invalid share ≤ 5 %, i.e. ≤ 1 of 30): a level that fails it, and every grid level above it, become plant-invalid (this is not a T1a FAIL), and its artefact counts are reported; (iii) active-onset share at BN* ≥ 0.2 at ≥ 1 admissible P1 level; (iv) at the admissible P1 level with the most active onsets at BN*: the median triangle discharge at BN* ∈ **[floor, 2,300] veh/h/lane** (floor per [AUTHOR A25]), not computable → FAIL; and **ρ_T1** = that median ÷ Q_ff(BN*) from P0-G1 (same section, estimator conventions and upstream exclusions), **≤ 0.97 gated under [AUTHOR A42] (a), report-only under (b)** (with no lower bound, because the triangle confounds within-run levels). A peak-relative drop alone is not enough: BN4's T1 passed at 0.862, then the plant failed realism at 900 veh/h/lane (ARCH t1_bn4_plant_checks.md:5-8) |

| On FAIL | treated as a P0 FAIL (§9.2) |

### T1b Control matters, on the co-primaries (gated)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Question | Can a simple, pre-specified command rule improve smoothness or safety over the same fleet without commands? |
| Arms | R4(70) and R4(80) vs B1 at ℓ₁, ℓ₂, ℓ₃ and the tuned INC point: **16 pre-specified tests** (2 caps × 4 points × {S, T}), no selection. A point found plant-invalid in T1a or failed in P0-GI has its tests reported and dropped from the union, with m kept at 16 (conservative). A T test at a point with median_s(T_B1) = 0 is not computable; it is dropped from the union, and m stays 16 |
| Statistic | r_Y = median_s(Y_R4 − Y_B1) / median_s(Y_B1), Y ∈ {S, T}; percentile bootstrap CI at 1 − 0.05/16 |
| PASS | at least one test with r ≤ **−5 %** and its Bonferroni CI upper bound < 0 |
| Power | z = 2.955 (α = 0.05/16, two-sided); with n = 30, 80 % power needs a true effect ≈ 0.87 σ (σ = SD of the per-seed relative difference; my arithmetic, §8.7) |
| Rationale | archive T1c template (≥ 5 %, CI excluding 0; ARCH t2_mrg3_r1_protocol.md:49-54). Directional: authority is not usefulness (ARCH t1_bn4_plant_checks.md:59-68). Restated on S and T because C is no longer the claim (V74) |

An untuned R4 is a weak rule (the archive's untuned per-CAV PI gave +119 to +180 % delay), and the CLAUDE.md early KILL needs a tuned proven controller or the oracle; a FAIL here predicts but does not prove a P2 KILL |

### T1c Attribution (report-only)
B1 vs B0, and each R4 vs B0 and vs B1, in S, T, h, h_all, h_HDV, C (secondary), Dl, energy, TTT and served throughput at the four P1 points (the INC point with its incident − no-incident rows), signed, with 95 % CIs. The automation effect (B1 − B0) is never credited to control.

### T2 Mechanism (report-only)
Computed in P2 from the tuning and held-out runs, with no extra simulation (§6.11), per co-primary. Prior: inflow scheduling gave G of 2–4 % on cost-type metrics in the archive (ARCH notes_networks.md:392; CORR2 ALINEA over 4 inflow conditions 0.4 %). T2 never gates (CLAUDE.md; D5.1).

### T3 Determinism and common random numbers (gated)

| Part | Content |
|---|---|
| T3-early | calibration C7 (§2.6) |
| T3a | 10 runs drawn uniformly from the P1 run list, INC runs included (`numpy.random.default_rng(0)`), each re-run (i) in a fresh process on another worker inside a mixed 32-worker batch and (ii) as the second episode of one process after an in-process close/start (as RL resets will do); 2 of the 10 are also (iii) SIGSTOPped by the CPU guard for ≥ 60 s mid-run and resumed (they must not be killed by the CPU-time cap). Hash = per-step trace (all vehicles: id, x, lane, speed, acceleration rounded to 12 decimals; the obstacle's state), **built as float64 arrays per step (ids as integer manifest indices) and hashed as bytes, never through string formatting** (review microbenchmark `review_sumo/hash_cost.py`, unloaded: about 0.81 µs per vehicle-step for string formatting against 0.08 µs for bytes, i.e. about 39 s vs 4 s per λ = 0.9 episode; v2 review SF10) + command log (lane calls, masks and mode changes included) + per-vehicle energy, delay, hard brakes, σ̃_i² and TET_i. PASS: **22 / 22 bitwise identical** |
| T3b | for every seed used in P0 and P1: inflow-manifest, attribute-manifest and **INC-schedule** hashes (T_on, lane, x_I, Δ_ins) identical across all arms. PASS 100 % |
| T3c | every ledger row holds config hash, code commit + dirty flag read at start **and** end, network spec hash, inflow hash, vType XML hash, observation-spec hash (per rule, `lane` spec included), **r-table and r_13 hash, Addendum B constants hash** (VoT, prices and their date, LHV and density, road-load values), the **C0′ adopted-setting id and hash, the TTC\* in force, M_cmd and the lane-mask constants**, the scenario (level, INC point), seed and role, validity, OPS re-run flag, metric summary, trace hash, SUMO version and build. PASS 100 % |
| Scope | one machine and one toolchain lock. Cross-machine determinism is not established (the EIDM docs warn that results may differ by compiler, processor or OS [VERIFY]); hashes are never compared across machines |
| Evidence | toy d_determinism.py: identical hashes over 4 concurrent processes × 2 in-process restarts; the archive found no determinism FAIL, but its hash covered aggregates only (port_map runner trap 7); the v2 toys were bit-identical across repetitions with the same seed (evidence: mechanics) |
| On FAIL | STOP; find the cause, fix, re-run every affected comparison (both outcomes logged); no P2 without a T3 PASS |

**P1 verdict.** PASS iff T0a–T0e, T0g, T1a and T3 PASS, T0f PASSes or [AUTHOR A46] has been applied (a T0f FAIL never stops P1: the lane actuator is removed instead), and T1b PASSes or the [AUTHOR A17] rule says proceed.

**Planning variances for P2.** For each co-primary Y ∈ {S, T}: σ_plan,1(Y) = 1.136 × the largest SD, over the admissible no-incident samples, of the per-seed relative difference (Y_A,s − Y_B0,s) / median_s(Y_B0,s) on 060–089 (the 80 % upper confidence limit of an SD with df = 29; §8.7). **The samples are the six (R4 cap, level) pairs plus (R-LANE, ℓ₂) and (R-KEEP, ℓ₂)** (v2 review ST6, BDP2-9): every stage-1 candidate carries a lane module, R4 runs speed-only in P1, and the lane samples are already run; taking the largest SD and, for KILL-1, the smallest quantile over more samples keeps both conservative. A sample with median_s(T_B0) = 0 is excluded for T (count reported). The samples of paired values feed the empirical calibration of the stage-1 futility thresholds t_S and t_T (§6.5), each sample's 0.5 % quantile is reported, and the INC-point samples are reported separately; no futility rule uses them (principle 15). Neither covers λ_c − 0.2, λ_c − 0.1, λ_c − 0.05 or the levels above ℓ₃ (stated in the P2 report). **The P1 report also gives** (v2 review ST3, ST5): σ_S and σ_T separately (point SDs and the 1.136 upper limits) and the SDs vs B1; the per-seed correlation corr(r_S, r_T) per sample; and the predicted P(enter) of the band for a true 12 % and 15 % gain on both co-primaries at n = 40 and 60, from the separate σ (normal planning approximation, independent and perfectly correlated bounds, and the observed correlation). **Resolution-futility stop ([AUTHOR A68], recommended (b)):** if, for S or for T, the point SD σ̂ (no upper-limit factor) exceeds **0.29**, i.e. even a true 15 % gain on that co-primary would enter with probability < 0.5 at n = 60 (A39 (a), m = 18; my arithmetic, `review_fix/desk_review.py`), the protocol STOPs before seed 100 with the verdict "the co-primaries cannot be resolved at the approved sample sizes on this plant"; it is a STOP, never a claim.

---

## 6 P2: scenario band on the co-primaries, tuning of R1–R4 with lane modules, O and F, band rule, early KILL

### 6.1 Arms
- **B0, B1** at every scenario point (the references).
- **R1** PI with saturation (Stern et al. 2018), re-parametrised for 100 km/h; `local` observation spec. The sign of its dx_s term is [VERIFY] before use.
- **R2** an explicit MicroAccel-family controller (Hayat et al.), local + planner mode; `zone` spec. Its parameters are unpublished, so they are tuned; its Eq. 14 is implemented as the clip the text describes (disclosed; literature_v0.md). Leader acceleration is not an onboard signal under V36, so it is a finite difference of the radar-derived leader speed over 2 s (a disclosed adaptation).
- **R3** CAV speed harmonisation (FHWA-HRT-21-008 linear transition; its Eq. 12 [VERIFY]), with the loop inputs replaced by V2X zone means (a disclosed adaptation); `zone` spec. With only Z1 upstream of a merge (V71), R3 harmonises towards the speed of the active downstream bottleneck (the drop) in every zone; how it chooses its target is fixed in Addendum C [VERIFY that the FHWA method covers lane drops; VERIFY against X01 when available].
- What R2's planner and R3 know about BN-LD is fixed in Addendum C by [AUTHOR A30], decided (b) by V65: the V2X probe-summary unit Z3+ on [15,000, 16,000) m, just downstream of the drop, with no commands there, is Z3's "next downstream zone" for every zone-spec arm (R2, R3, and later L2).
- **R4** a constant CAV target, one parameter in {50, 55, …, 100} km/h (11 values), with the lane module at its mapped defaults (no lane tuning; R4 stays the trivial comparator). In P1, R4(70) and R4(80) run speed-only (ℓ = keep), so that T0b, T0c and T1b test the speed actuator alone.
- **Lane modules (V76; the fair-DRL rule of CLAUDE.md: same actuators).** R1–R4 each carry **one shared lane module**: identical code, cadence (2 s), `exec` semantics, mode, mask and ban for every rule ([AUTHOR A48]). Its two parts:
  - **LM-B, lane balancing at the drop** (Z2 and Z3; "keep" in Z1, so that no rule commands moves next to the merge-2 weave). An adaptation of Schakel, Klunder, van Arem, Harmsen & Hagenzieker (EWGT 2012, full text, evidence: lanes; venue [VERIFY]). F3Z's drop mirrors their A20 layout: two combining lanes (0, 1) and one continuing lane, here lane 2. Per CAV and boundary:
    1. **Zone switch with hysteresis:** ON when the zone's CAV mean speed (V2X zone summary) is < v_on; OFF when it is > v_on + 10 km/h; initially OFF.
    2. **Eligibility while ON:** the CAV is on lane 1; its own speed is ≥ 50 km/h ("keep" inside queues, following Schakel's validity-speed condition and dynamic late-merge practice; the latter from secondary sources [VERIFY]; 50 km/h is the V47 floor, ASSUMPTION); no **executed** commanded lane change of that CAV in the last 60 s (Schakel's 1-min no-repeat, fixed; a request that expired unexecuted does not count, so that LM-B does not become inert in dense traffic, where only 4–8 % of requests executed within 2 s in the mechanics toy; v2 review SF8).
    3. **Share rule:** lane 2's estimated share of the zone's traffic must be < 0.50 to start commands, and commands stop once it reaches ≥ 0.60 (Schakel's 50 % / 60 %, fixed). The share is estimated from the per-lane CAV radar spacing in the zone message (a probe density proxy, [AUTHOR A47] (ii)); without it, from per-lane CAV counts, disclosed as endogenous (once a rule moves CAVs, their lane shares no longer reflect total traffic).
    4. **Selection:** each eligible CAV commands "left" with probability p, drawn from a NumPy generator keyed by (seed, CAV id, boundary), so it is deterministic and identical across replays and arms; otherwise "keep".
    5. **Never "right"** (towards the combining lanes).
    - Tuned: **v_on ∈ [60, 90] km/h** and **p ∈ [0.2, 1.0]**. Mapped default: v_on = 80 km/h (Schakel's 80/90 km/h trigger pair, re-mapped for the 100 km/h limit) and p = 1.0 (their 1/0.6 compliance factor becomes full compliance). The re-mapping from detector inputs to V2X probe inputs is disclosed in Addendum C.
  - **LM-E, incident evacuation** (Z2, INC runs only, active once the hazard message is available, i.e. from t_blk + 60 s):
    - direction from Zhang & Ioannou's rules (Ioannou & Zhang, PSR report 2018, full text, evidence: lanes): a closed edge lane changes towards the open side, so blocked lane 0 → "left";
    - each CAV on lane 0 in Z2 draws a change point uniformly in [a, a + w] ∩ Z2 from a NumPy stream keyed by (seed, vehicle id, "LM-E"), never from SUMO's RNG, so it is identical in every arm that uses LM-E; "keep" before the change point; "left" at every boundary from it until the CAV is on lane 1 or leaves Z2;
    - tuned: **a ∈ [11,000, 11,800] m, w ∈ [200, 1,000] m**, clipped to Z2: a simplification of Zhang C., Sabar, Chung, Bhaskar & Guo 2019's optimised segment proportions (TR-C 106:303-316, accepted manuscript read, evidence: lanes), disclosed;
    - mapped default (used by R4, by F at INC points, and as the LHS default): a = 11,000 m, w = 1,000 m, i.e. a change point uniform over the whole of Z2.
  - **Precedence (fixed now):** while the hazard message is active, LM-E overrides LM-B in Z2, and **no lane module ever commands a move into the blocked lane**. The module's own logic enforces this; the `exec` mask is not used for it, because the mask must stay identical for every arm and arms without the `lane` spec do not know the incident. P2-B tests it.
  - **Not used as rule arms** (evidence: lanes): Roncoli/Markantonakis LQ lane-changing control and Mehr & Eskandarian's probabilistic timing rule, because they need per-lane densities or HDV headway distributions, which V27 and V36 exclude; Chen, Srivastava & Ahn 2021's gap control, which needs coordinated control of the follower. Disclosed.
- **Keep-only twins** (report-only): at the held-out stage, O(s) and F are also run with ℓ = keep throughout, on seeds 200–239, so that the lane module's own contribution is visible. By T0e(k) a keep-only arm is bit-identical to its speed-only version.
- **O(s)** the informed oracle: the best (rule, candidate) looked up per tuned scenario point, tuned on tuning seeds only (`system_context.md` §4); at the INC point it is informed of both the level and the incident. **F** the best fixed (rule, candidate) over the no-incident levels ("the best of R1–R4", V45). Both are chosen in §6.6.
- All commanding arms use the same `exec` layer at 2 s (`system_context.md` §4). Sources: literature_v0.md F1, F2, F4 (Stern 2018 and Hayat 2023 read in full; the FHWA report in the relevant chapters); evidence: lanes for the lane modules. VSL arms I1/I2 are not in the band rule [AUTHOR A21]. Native-rate (unquantised) rule versions are not run in P0–P2 [AUTHOR A22].

### 6.2 Scenario grid (rule fixed now, applied to λ_c from P0-C)
- **No-incident levels, L = 8:** {λ_c − 0.20, λ_c − 0.10, λ_c − 0.05, λ_c, λ_c + 0.05, λ_c + 0.10, λ_c + 0.20, 1.0}, rounded to 0.01. If 1.0 lies within 0.025 of another grid point, that other point is replaced by λ_c + 0.35; the P1 level that would have been the replaced point is then 1.0, its nearest grid point (§5.0).
- The grid is densest near critical, where the archive found authority (ARCH notes_networks.md:392). **λ = 1.0 is always in the operating map.** ℓ₁, ℓ₂ and ℓ₃ are the P1 levels.
- **INC points ([AUTHOR A44] (b)):** the **tuned INC point** is I2 = λ_c with the incident if it passed P0-GI, else the passing INC point nearest λ_c (ties to the higher). I1 and I3, if they passed P0-GI, are **held-out map rows** (B0, B1, O(I2), F), never tuned.
- **S_tuned** = the tuned no-incident levels ∪ {the tuned INC point}; **m = 2 · |S_tuned|** (18 at L = 8 with the INC point; 20 with an edge level). Levels marked **plant-invalid** by P0-G2(c) or T1a(ii) and INC points failed in P0-GI are not tuned; they keep their B0 and B1 rows. L in every formula below counts tuned no-incident levels, including an edge level added under §6.10.

### 6.3 Addendum C and P2-B
- **Addendum C** is committed **before seed 7,400,060**, before any commanding arm runs on a project seed, so that no P1 contrast can shape it. Per rule it fixes: the parameter list (rule parameters, LM-B's v_on and p, LM-E's a and w) and the dimension **d = the number of parameters active at no-incident points** (rule + LM-B); each parameter's **inner range** (sampled in stage 1) and **outer range** (the inner range widened by 25 % of its width on each side, clipped to physically admissible values; used only by the refinement of §6.10); its scale; the published default point mapped to 100 km/h and to F3Z (mapping disclosed); the **joint Latin-hypercube** sampler (`scipy.stats.qmc.LatinHypercube`, seed 0) over all of the rule's parameters, with **n_cand = max(20, 5 · d)** candidates per rule (the default + n_cand − 1 sampled points; [AUTHOR A48]); the refinement sampler (seed 1). LM-E's two parameters ride on the same LHS: they are inactive at no-incident points, so they do not thin the design there (an LHS stays stratified in every one-dimensional projection). It also fixes the R4 grid, the lane-module constants and RNG keys, the incident-message format, and **freezes the observation specs** `local`, `zone` and **`lane`** (`interfaces.md` §7 [PROPOSAL]): field list (§12.2 lists the lane fields), normalisation constants, Z3's "next downstream zone" field (Z3+, [AUTHOR A30]); the spec hash is recorded per rule. Every R arm's declared spec = its speed spec ∪ `lane`, and the contract test enforces it. Any later spec change that touches a field a rule reads re-runs that rule's P2 on fresh seeds. The author approves Addendum C before seed 060; the checkpoint "plant and all opponents done" (High effort, ultracode on for the adversarial code review) reviews the code against it.
- **P2-B acceptance tests** (non-project seeds): each rule reproduces a hand-computed command sequence on a scripted scenario; **LM-B and LM-E reproduce hand-computed lane-command sequences (hysteresis, share rule, deterministic draws, change points, precedence: no move into the blocked lane; LM-B's no-repeat: a CAV whose request expired is eligible again at the next boundary, one whose request executed is not for 60 s)**; each reads only its declared observation spec (contract test); the O lookup and the ledger resume work; per rule, a timing test (CPU overhead ratio over B1) sets its CPU-time cap (§2.6 C8).
- R1 and R4 may start stage 1 before R2 and R3 exist, on the same seeds. No futility, selection or band decision is taken until all four rules have finished the stage concerned.

### 6.4 Baselines, the C ceiling and the room rows (report-only, [AUTHOR A5])
- B0 and B1 run on every seed and scenario point that any tuned arm uses (stage 1, stage 2, held-out).
- **Ceiling for C (secondary, report-only).** After stage 1, for each no-incident level ℓ, an approximate upper bound on what any CAV-command arm could gain in C over B0 on the same inflow files: G_max(ℓ) = 1 − median_s C_floor,s / median_s C_B0,s, with C_floor,s = [Σ_v VoT_v · w_v + Σ_v f_v] / D, where
  - w_v = queue-delay floor: the largest, over the bottleneck sections on v's route, of v's delay in a fluid first-in-first-out point queue at section k, with arrivals = desired departure + reference travel time to k and service rate Q_cap,k = 1.10 × the largest 5-min flow observed at section k over all B0 and B1 runs at all levels on 100–107 (10 % margin: ASSUMPTION);
  - f_v = the minimum over cruise speeds u ∈ [50, min(v_des, 100)] km/h of [VoT_v · L_v · (1/u − 1/u_ref,v) + p_v · e_class(u) · L_v], with e_class(u) from P0-G4 (interpolated) and u_ref,v the speed implied by r.
  - **Labelled approximation, not a theorem:** it assumes first-in-first-out queues, no throughput above Q_cap, and no trajectory beating steady cruise on energy (pulse-and-glide could, under HBEFA4). It is reported per level with a 95 % bootstrap CI over seeds, and it **never prunes a level or KILLs**.
- **Room rows for S and T** (P0-R12 extended to P2 levels): 1 − S_floor / median S_B0 and 1 − T_floor / median T_B0 per scenario point; labelled approximations, not bounds.

### 6.5 Stage 1: screen (seeds 7,400,100–107, n₁ = 8), additions and KILL-1
- **Runs:** every candidate at every tuned scenario point, paired with B0 and B1: R1, R2, R3 with n_cand joint candidates each (equal budget across R1–R3), R4 with 11; **the same candidate set at every point**, so that the pooled objective and T2 come at no extra cost. At n_cand = 20: 73 episodes per point and seed.
- **Objective** for a candidate at point s: J_Y = median_s(Y_cfg − Y_B0) / median_s(Y_B0) for Y ∈ {S, T}, and **J = max(J_S, J_T)** (lower is better; the weaker co-primary decides, consistent with the intersection claim; [AUTHOR A40]); gain g = −J, and g_Y = −J_Y. If median_s(T_B0) = 0 at a point, J_T is not computable there and J = +∞. The **pooled objective** of a candidate is defined in §6.6.
- **Eligibility to advance** (a screen; in-sample point estimates, per candidate and point, **with a noise allowance**, so that a noisy 8-seed guard draw cannot remove the best S/T candidate before the 38-seed selection, which re-applies the guards without allowance; v2 review ST4): the delay guard **Δ_D ≤ 0.02 + 1.645 × 1.2533 × σ_D,plan / √8** (§6.8(e)), with σ_D,plan the largest SD of the per-seed relative delay difference vs B0 over the P1 samples on 060–089 (§5); the continuity-corrected CAV hard-brake ratio vs B1 ≤ **(1 + y) · exp(1.645 · √(1/k_A + 1/k_ref))**, k the pooled event counts of the arm and the reference (§8.5, [AUTHOR A14]); the all-vehicle at-speed ratios (f2) vs B0 and vs B1 ≤ the same form ([AUTHOR A38]). With the allowance a neutral candidate (true Δ_D = 0) passes the delay screen with probability 0.995 / 0.982 at σ_D = 0.05 / 0.10 (0.82 / 0.67 without it), one at a true +2 % with 0.95 (0.50); at 100 events per arm a ratio at a true 1.0 passes with 0.99 (0.75), while a true 1.3 still passes with 0.68 and is caught by the stage-2 guards (normal and log-Poisson approximations; my arithmetic, `review_fix/desk_review.py`). Shared B0 and B1 runs make screen errors correlated across candidates, which the allowance also covers; **at most 1 invalid run among the 8** (incident non-delivery included; with 0 of 8, a candidate with a 2 % per-run invalid rate would be screened out with probability ≈ 0.15 per point; with ≤ 1 of 8, ≈ 0.01; my arithmetic). If fewer than 2 candidates of a rule qualify at a point, the lowest-J others fill the slots, flagged.
- **Metric screen** (ARCH lessons_metric_gameability.md §4, on stage-1 data, before stage 2): (1) constant policy: the R4 values are constant policies, and their S, T and C are reported; (2) extremes: if the best R4 value at any point is 50 km/h, the report shows the delay guard and the CAV speed give-up there; (3) decoupling: any arm with lower S or T but higher Dl than B0 beyond the guard is reported; (4) tautology: whether collisions, extreme decelerations or T > 0 ever occur; (5) exposure: all rates per fixed km; (6) harm moved onto HDVs: h_HDV, S_HDV and T_HDV of the best candidates vs B1 [AUTHOR A29]; (7) at-speed vs all-speed T (FLAG); (8) lane changes per vehicle-km vs B1 (FLAG at > 2 ×); (9) the S/v̄_W FLAG. It informs the report and never changes the band rule.
- **Additions** (§6.10): the edge-level check and the parameter-adequacy check run on stage-1 data; any triggered addition runs on 100–107 and joins stage 1 **before** KILL-1 is evaluated.
- **KILL-1 (stage-1 futility; no-incident levels only, principle 15).** If, at **every** tuned no-incident level (an added edge level included), no candidate over all rules (guards ignored, in-sample, refinement candidates included) has **g_S ≥ t_S and g_T ≥ t_T**, the claim on this plant is **KILLed**. The thresholds are calibrated **empirically** from P1 data and recorded in the P1 report, before seed 100:
  - for each admissible P1 no-incident sample of 30 paired values (Y_A,s, Y_B0,s) on 060–089 (the six (R4 cap, level) pairs and (R-LANE, ℓ₂), (R-KEEP, ℓ₂); §5, v2 review ST6), and for each Y ∈ {S, T}, a constant is added to every Y_A,s so that median_s(Y_A − Y_B0) / median_s(Y_B0) = −0.10 exactly;
  - 10⁴ resamples of 8 seeds are drawn with replacement (`numpy.random.default_rng(20261013)`), **with the same resample indices for S and T**, and g*_Y = −J*_Y is computed in each;
  - **t_Y = min(0.05, the smallest 0.5 % quantile of g*_Y over these samples)**, per co-primary.

  So, by the union bound, a candidate whose true gain is 10 % on both co-primaries falls below either threshold with probability ≤ 1 % at a level whose paired-difference distribution resembles a P1 sample, without assuming normality or a median SE formula. The normal-approximation value t_N = min(0.05, 0.10 − 2.576 × 1.2533 × σ_plan,1(Y) / √8) = min(0.05, 0.10 − 1.141 σ_plan,1(Y)) is reported alongside (σ_plan,1 = 0.05 → 0.043; 0.07 → 0.020; 0.10 → −0.014; my arithmetic). Selecting the best of 73 or more candidates only raises the in-sample estimate, so the real false-KILL rate is lower still. The calibration covers only the P1 levels (§5). If a t_Y ≤ 0, KILL-1 fires only if no candidate improves on B0 at all on that co-primary, and the realistic early KILL is KILL-2 (§10.3). Whether KILL-1 fires more or less often than in v1 is not established: requiring both co-primaries makes it fire more readily at a given threshold, while the per-metric 0.5 % quantiles lower the thresholds.

### 6.6 Stage 2: selection (seeds 7,400,108–137, n₂ = 30, fresh), O and F, KILL-2
- **Finalists per tuned point:** per rule, the 2 eligible candidates with the best stage-1 J at that point, plus per rule the 2 eligible candidates with the best stage-1 pooled objective (over the tuned no-incident levels), deduplicated (refinement candidates count as candidates of their rule); plus B0 and B1. At most 18 arms per point and seed.
- **Selection eligibility** mirrors the band rule: on all 38 selection seeds 100–137, a finalist is eligible at point s if it meets band criteria (d_S), (d_T), (b_S′), (e), (f), (f′), (f2), (g), (h) and, at the INC point, (j) with the guards on both cohorts (§6.8), computed in sample with the band's own statistics, in point form ([AUTHOR A27], V65); (h) is an invalid share ≤ 5 % (≤ 1 of 38).
- **Selection** uses stages 1 and 2 together (n = 38) and the **paired** statistic, never the median level (ARCH STATUS #19: choosing by level picked an arm paired-worse than 4 of 5 rivals):
  - **O(s)** = the eligible finalist with the lowest J at point s (a per-point lookup tuned on tuning seeds only, never per evaluation seed); if none is eligible at s, the finalist with the lowest J among those meeting (h), flagged;
  - **F** = among pooled finalists meeting (h) at every tuned **no-incident** level, the one with the lowest **pooled objective** J̄ = mean over the tuned no-incident levels of J_ℓ, where J_ℓ is set to 0 (no gain) at every level at which the finalist is not eligible (fixed parameters at every level); ties are broken by the unpenalised mean J. The INC point does not enter J̄, so incident performance cannot shape the choice of the best fixed rule;
  - **F at INC points** = F's configuration with LM-E at its mapped default (§6.1), so that F stays one fixed rule chosen without incident data. It is first run at the held-out stage (labelled: no in-sample selection at INC points);
  - each rule's per-point and pooled bests are kept for the map (in-sample rows, labelled).
  O and F are chosen with the same effort on the same seeds. The lowest J stays the objective (the CLAUDE.md oracle is a lookup tuned on tuning seeds); the predicted pass rate of §6.7 is reported for the chosen arm and the two runners-up at each point.
- **KILL-2 (in-sample futility; no-incident levels only).** If, at **every** tuned no-incident level, no finalist reaches **r_S ≤ −0.10 and r_T ≤ −0.10** in sample (guards ignored), no configuration reaches a 10 % median reduction on both co-primaries even in sample: the claim is **KILLed** and the held-out stage is not run. The in-sample minimum over the finalists is biased towards larger reductions, so this is conservative relative to the band criteria.
- **σ_plan,2** = 1.136 × the largest SD, over the tuned **no-incident** points and over {vs B0, vs B1} × {S, T}, of the per-seed relative difference O(s) vs that reference on 108–137 (80 % upper confidence limit, df = 29). Two changes from the earlier draft (v2 review ST5, BDP2-8): the vs-B1 SDs are included, because the CI part of (d_S), (d_T) binds before its 5 % point criterion once z_c · SE > 0.05 (0.054 at n = 40, σ = 0.085, m = 18), and the INC point's SD is reported, with the half-width it achieves, but not used ([AUTHOR A61], recommended (c)), because no decision rests on the INC point's band membership while its noise could raise n_eval for every point. The SDs of the guard quantities (Δ_D, the hard-brake ratios, origin-queue time) on 108–137 are reported with it.

### 6.7 Held-out sample size (fixed after stage 2, before any held-out run)
- **(i) σ rule:** the smaller of {40, 60} for which z_c × 1.2533 × σ_plan,2 / √n ≤ 0.05, with z_c = Φ⁻¹(1 − α_c/2) and α_c the per-component level of [AUTHOR A39]. Under A39 (a) at m = 18 (z_c = 3.197): n = 40 if σ_plan,2 ≤ 0.079, n = 60 if ≤ 0.097 (m = 20, with an edge level: 0.078 / 0.096); under A39 (b) at m = 18 (z = 2.991): 0.084 / 0.103 (my arithmetic). Rationale: a true 10 % reduction then has its Bonferroni CI upper bound near −5 %.
- **(ii) Whole-rule check:** for every tuned point s and A ∈ {O(s), F} with in-sample r_S ≤ −0.10 and r_T ≤ −0.10 on 100–137, 2,000 bootstrap resamples of n = 40 and of n = 60 seeds are drawn from the 38 selection seeds (rows of B0, B1 and A paired by seed; joint resampling, §8.2); the full band rule ((a′), (b_S)–(h), (b_S′) and, at the INC point, (j)) is applied to each, giving the pass shares P40 and P60.
- **n_eval = 60** if the σ rule requires it, or if P60 − P40 ≥ 0.10 for any such (s, A) (ASSUMPTION); otherwise 40. If neither n satisfies the σ rule, n_eval = 60 and the achieved half-width is reported as a limitation. **n = 80 is not available** ([AUTHOR A49] (a)): at a planning σ of 0.15, a true 12 % gain on one co-primary passes with probability 0.75 / 0.80 / 0.83 at n = 40 / 60 / 80 (normal approximation, m = 18; my arithmetic), so 20 more seeds buy about 0.03, while seeds 260–299 are kept for the D5.8 re-tuning ([AUTHOR A32]).
- P40, P60, the expected bounds of the guards (e)–(g) and the binding criterion per (s, A) go into the P2 report **before** the held-out stage. They are in-sample predictions, optimistic by construction (winner's curse).
- Seeds 200–239 or 200–259. Arms at every tuned point: B0, B1, O(s), F (if O(s) = F, one set of runs serves both); keep-only twins O(s)|keep and F|keep on 200–239 only; at I1 and I3 (if they passed P0-GI): B0, B1, O(I2), F; B0 and B1 also at untuned levels.

### 6.8 The band rule (fixed now; non-learning arms only)
For a tuned scenario point s and an arm A ∈ {O(s), F}, on the held-out seeds, over K (the cohort, or cohort_I at the INC point). **At the INC point the guards (e), (f), (f′), (f2) and (g) are evaluated twice, on cohort_I and on the full cohort, and both must pass**, and criterion (j) applies ([AUTHOR A55]; §2.4b). The effect criteria use intervals at level 1 − α_c, with α_c per **[AUTHOR A39]** (recommended (a): α_c = 0.05 / (2m), the V74 correction for two applied on top of the Bonferroni correction over the m = 2·|S_tuned| pairs); a one-sided bound is the corresponding end of that two-sided interval, which is conservative. For (d_S) and (d_T) this is a deliberately stricter reading of V61's "95 % CI" (as v1's Bonferroni level already was). Every bound at α_c uses B ≥ 200,000 resamples (§8.2). The guards use the point form of **[AUTHOR A27]** (decided (c), V65), with the Bonferroni interval reported.

| # | Criterion | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| (a) | Admissible point | s is not plant-invalid (P0-G2(c), T1a(ii)), and the single admissibility rule of §8.8 holds on the held-out B0 runs (B0 invalid share ≤ 5 %, n ≥ 40). At the INC point also: P0-GI passed, and incident non-delivery counts as invalid |
| (a′) | Informative point ([AUTHOR A59], recommended (a); v2 review BDP2-2) | on the held-out B0 runs at s: T_B0 > 0 in ≥ 90 % of runs, a median of ≥ 30 conflict episodes per run, and median S_B0 ≥ 1.5 × S_floor (the G6 conditions, applied per point). A point that fails is **"co-primary-uninformative"**: it stays in the map with all its rows, but it is out of the band and out of the D5.8 regime set; m is unchanged (conservative) |
| (b_S) | Smoothness effect | r_S,A = median_s(S_A − S_B0) / median_s(S_B0) **≤ −0.10** (V74, V45 re-read) |
| (b_S′) | Smoothness co-confirmation ([AUTHOR A58]; v2 review BDP2-1) | r on S_raw (no high-pass), A vs B0, **≤ 0** (point form). A change of oscillation period at equal amplitude moves the high-passed S but not S_raw, so it cannot carry the claim alone |
| (c_S) | Smoothness significance | the CI upper bound of r_S,A at level 1 − α_c is **< 0** |
| (d_S) | Smoothness vs B1 ([AUTHOR A7], V61 reading (a)) | r_S,A,B1 = median_s(S_A − S_B1) / median_s(S_B1) **≤ −0.05** **and** its CI upper bound at 1 − α_c **< 0** |
| (b_T) | Safety effect | r_T,A **≤ −0.10** (T at the TTC\* in force; not computable if median_s T_B0 = 0 → FAIL) |
| (c_T) | Safety significance | the CI upper bound of r_T,A at 1 − α_c is **< 0** |
| (d_T) | Safety vs B1 | r_T,A,B1 ≤ **−0.05** and its CI upper bound at 1 − α_c < 0 |
| (e) | Delay guard (V37) | Δ_D = median_s(Dl_A − Dl_B0) / max(median_s(Dl_B0), 0.05 · median_s(R_tot)) **≤ +0.02**, over K. The floor in the denominator (ASSUMPTION) keeps the guard continuous where B0's delay is near or below 0; there the margin is 0.1 % of K's total reference time |
| (f) | CAV hard braking (V25) | the pooled CAV hard-brake rate ratio A / B1 (h, all speeds), continuity-corrected (§8.5), ≤ **1 + y** ([AUTHOR A14]: y = 10 %); its bootstrap and exact conditional intervals are reported |
| (f′) | CAV hard braking vs B0 (only if activated) | activated, by a rule fixed now, if P0-G3's R₃ (B1 vs the same vehicles in B0 at −3 m/s²) exceeds 1 + y at either gated level: the same as (f) with the same vehicles in B0 as the reference |
| (f2) | All-vehicle hard braking at speed ([AUTHOR A38] (a)) | the continuity-corrected pooled ratio of h_all, A / B0 ≤ **1 + y** **and** A / B1 ≤ **1 + y** |
| (g) | Gameability guards ([AUTHOR A23]) | pooled totals over seeds: (g1) Σ_s censored_A,s ≤ Σ_s censored_B0,s; (g2) Σ_s origin-queue veh·h_A,s ≤ 1.02 × Σ_s origin-queue veh·h_B0,s (1.02 mirrors V37; ASSUMPTION); and for each of the two quantities, the number of seeds with A > B0 does not exceed the number with B0 > A (sign count) |
| (h) | Validity | A's invalid share ≤ 5 % (incident non-delivery included); invalid runs enter (b_S)–(g) as in §8.4 |
| (j) | Full cohort at the INC point only ([AUTHOR A55]) | full-cohort S and T of A vs B0: r ≤ **+0.05** each (point form, as A27); the X1/X2-bound and late-arrival subgroups are reported |

- **Point s is in the band** iff (a) and (a′) hold and, for A = O(s) or A = F, every criterion (b_S)–(h) holds, (b_S′) included, plus (f′) if activated and (j) at the INC point: **both co-primaries, for the same arm** ([AUTHOR A40] (a)). The **band** is the set of such points. It need not be contiguous; there is no interpolation or smoothing; its hull is reported but not used. **A GO needs at least one no-incident level in the band** (§6.9).
- **Band table** at every tuned point, in or out of the band: r_S and r_T vs B0 and vs B1 with CIs; the **automation/control decomposition** for S and T, (Y_B0 − Y_B1) / Y_B0 (automation share) and (Y_B1 − Y_A) / Y_B0 (control share), with CIs; Δ_D; **served throughput with its CI**; TTT; h, h_all, h_HDV (with the [AUTHOR A29] FLAG when A/B1 > 1 + y); censored counts; origin-queue time; validity; which criterion binds; **C (secondary)** vs B0 and vs B1 with CIs and its decomposition, with v1's band criteria evaluated on C as a labelled "C-band (secondary)" row; the keep-only twin rows; the HDV-share (S_HDV, T_HDV) and lane-change FLAGs; the severe (1.5-s) FLAG, the all-speed-outside-the-zipper FLAG, the short-headway FLAG and the T0f SSM FLAG (lane-commanding arms); the imputed shares and the "imputation-dependent", "obstacle-dependent" and "filter-dependent" labels; S_raw for (b_S′); B0's wave-presence share; the informativeness fields of (a′); at the INC point, the full-cohort and subgroup rows; the A42 limitation sentence if it applies.
- **Wording rule** ([AUTHOR A56], recommended (a); v2 review MV-4, HC-7, BDP2-4, HC-1). A claim names its measures; "smoother" and "safer" alone are never the claim. The pre-approved form is: "≥ 10 % less time exposed to TTC below {TTC\*} s at follower speeds of at least 36 km/h (a surrogate safety measure from microsimulation, not a crash rate), and ≥ 10 % lower within-vehicle speed fluctuation (high-passed speed SD), than no control". It is never written without: the B1 decomposition; the delay row; the C row and the energy row (kWh per vehicle-km); the h_all ratios vs B0 and vs B1; the TTC\* in force and, if the C9 fork fired, the sentence "V74's 1.5-s measure is reported as the severe row" with the severe FLAG's status; the short form of the §0.5 disclosure ("the claim metric was chosen after a pilot on non-project seeds had predicted that the delay headline would fail; every definition was fixed before any project data"); the A42 limitation sentence if it applies; the label "smoothing without stop-and-go waves" where B0's wave-presence share at that point is below 0.5 ([AUTHOR A60]); and, at an INC point, "incident regime: lane 0 blocked for 15 min at 13 km, onset 3,000 s, information after 60 s" ([AUTHOR A7], [AUTHOR A43]). **The word "safer"** may be used only if, in addition, the h_all point ratios vs B0 and vs B1 are both ≤ 1.00 and the severe FLAG has not fired.
- **Error control, stated exactly.** A (s, A) claim is false if, for at least one co-primary, the true θ ≥ 0; that co-primary's CI condition then passes with probability ≤ α_c. With Bonferroni over the m pairs, the family-wise error of "some point is in the band" is **≤ m · α/(2m) = 0.025 under A39 (a)** and **≤ 0.05 under A39 (b)**, under the null of no gain on at least one co-primary at every pair. The 10 % and 5 % thresholds are point criteria with no error control, consistent with D5.8's bar (a median of 10 % with the CI excluding 0), so a point whose true gain is below 10 % can enter. Seeds 200–259 decide **band membership only**; any effect size quoted for a band point or regime is re-estimated on fresh seeds (300+) in the learning-phase protocol (§12). **Monte Carlo and a distribution-free check** (v2 review ST8): at α_c = 0.05/36 each tail of the interval holds about 14 of 20,000 resamples (binomial SD about 3.7), so a borderline (c) or (d) verdict could depend on the analysis RNG seed; every bound at α_c therefore uses B ≥ 200,000 (about 139 per tail, SD about 12; my arithmetic) and is printed with its Monte Carlo SE. Because median_s(Y_ref) > 0, "CI upper bound of r < 0" is a bound on the median paired difference; its sign-test counterpart at α_c needs at least 31 negative paired differences of 40 (43 of 60) (P(Bin(40, ½) ≤ 9) = 0.00034, P(Bin(60, ½) ≤ 17) = 0.00053, against 0.000694 per tail). The sign-test count is a check row; a disagreement with the bootstrap verdict is a FLAG.
- **Operating characteristic** (normal planning approximation, n = 40, σ = 0.085 per co-primary, m = 18 under A39 (a), so SE ≈ 0.0168 and z_c · SE ≈ 0.054; guards and (d) ignored; my arithmetic). Because z_c · SE < 0.10, the CI condition is implied by the point criterion on the same estimate, so the single-co-primary probability is P(point ≤ −0.10) (the rigor draft multiplied the two events as if independent, which understated it; Appendix D):

  | True gain on each co-primary | 7 % | 8 % | 9 % | 10 % | 12 % | 15 % |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | P(enter), one co-primary | 0.04 | 0.12 | 0.28 | 0.50 | 0.88 | ≈ 1.00 |
  | P(enter), both, if the two estimates are independent | 0.001 | 0.014 | 0.08 | 0.25 | 0.78 | ≈ 1.00 |
  | P(enter), both, upper bound (perfectly correlated estimates) | 0.04 | 0.12 | 0.28 | 0.50 | 0.88 | ≈ 1.00 |

  Levels that rise together with congestion do not make the **paired-difference estimates** positively correlated: in the rigor toy (3 seeds, not F3Z) the harm70 stand-in ranked its S and T changes in opposite order across seeds (S −8.2 / −11.1 / −11.0 %, T −0.6 / +4.0 / +14.5 %). Under negative dependence the joint probability is below the "independent" row, so that row is **not** a lower bound (v2 review ST5). This table is orientation only, with a common σ; the P1 report re-issues it with separate σ_S and σ_T and the observed corr(r_S, r_T) (§5), and the operating characteristic that decides n_eval is the whole-rule joint bootstrap of §6.7 (ii), which includes (d), the guards and the actual dependence.
- **Why both O and F.** V45 says "the best non-learning arm or the informed oracle". O(s) is the oracle; F is the best fixed tuned rule. Testing both keeps the family at m = 2·|S_tuned| pre-chosen tests.

### 6.9 GO, or KILL-3
- **KILL-3:** no admissible **no-incident** level is in the band → **KILL** of the claim on F3Z with this fleet (CLAUDE.md, Method): no rule of the comparator set (the published speed rules R1–R3 adapted to the 2-s CAV interface, the constant target R4, and the protocol-adapted lane modules LM-B and LM-E, all tuned within the Addendum C ranges and the pre-registered refinement of §6.10) and no informed oracle cuts **both S and T by ≥ 10 % vs no control (and by ≥ 5 % vs B1)**, within the guards, at any admissible no-incident level. The KILL statement names which rule and lane module carried F and O at each level, and calls them "the best tuned rule", not "a proven controller" (v2 review HC-10). If the INC point is in the band while no no-incident level is, this is reported prominently ("the incident regime alone meets the bar") and **never yields a GO** (principle 15). No bound is revisited; a re-designed plant needs a new protocol. All three KILLs are logged with every point's numbers, and the full operating map is still reported.
- **GO:** at least one no-incident level is in the band. The band and the map are frozen in the P2 report. The **pre-registered regime set** for the learning phases (§12.2) is the no-incident band levels **plus the INC points that passed P0-GI** (V75: the incident is a pre-registered regime), with each INC point's band membership reported, minus any point that fails (a′) ([AUTHOR A59]). Its **regimes** follow [AUTHOR A57] (recommended): each maximal run of consecutive no-incident band levels on the §6.2 grid is one regime, and each INC point is one regime; their number R is fixed here, at GO. The **designated level ℓ*** is fixed at the same time by the rule of [AUTHOR A6] (c) **among no-incident band levels only**: λ = 1.0 if it is in the band, else the band level closest to 1.0, ties to the higher; the later D5.8 test "≥ 10 % vs no control at the realistic level" is made at ℓ* only, on both co-primaries, each component at **α/2 under A39 (a)** (z = 2.241) or α under (b) (z = 1.960), so it needs no multiplicity correction across band levels (v2 review ST2).
- **Wording** ([AUTHOR A6]): no band level is called "realistic" unless it is λ = 1.0; ℓ* is called "the designated level". λ = 1.0's row is always shown, in or out of the band, with the §2.1 statement that the nominal inflow exceeds F3Z's capacity on paper.

### 6.10 Additions after stage 1 (pre-registered, once each)
- **Order, fixed now:** stage 1 → (i) edge-level check and (ii) parameter-adequacy check, both on stage-1 data → triggered additions run on 100–107 → KILL-1 on the extended set → stage 2 on the extended set → KILL-2 → n_eval → held-out. Nothing is added after stage 2, so no addition can be prompted by stage-2 data or rescue a KILL-2.
- **(i) Edge level (no-incident levels only; the INC point never triggers an addition).** If the no-incident level with the lowest stage-1 J (the best candidate over all rules) is the lowest or the highest tuned level, one level beyond it (λ_c − 0.30, or the highest tuned level + 0.15) is added: B0, B1 and all candidates on 100–107, then stage 2. L and m grow by one and two, and F's pooled objective covers it. Cost ≈ 584 episodes in stage 1, ≤ 540 in stage 2 and ≈ 240 at the held-out stage (with twins), ≈ 6.8 h (my arithmetic). The archive found authority only in narrow bands just above critical (ARCH notes_networks.md:392), so an optimum at the grid edge warns that the grid missed it.
- **(ii) Parameter adequacy.** If, at any tuned point, the best stage-1 candidate of R1, R2 or R3 lies within 10 % of the normalised inner-range width of a range bound (lane-module parameters included), or is the published default, one refinement batch runs for **all three** rules (equal budget): per rule, 10 LHS points (refinement sampler) in a box of half-width 25 % of each parameter's inner range around that rule's best pooled stage-1 candidate, clipped to the outer range; at every tuned point, on 100–107. R4 is not refined: its grid already spans the V47 bounds. Cost ≈ 10 × 3 × 9 × 8 = 2,160 episodes at L = 8 with the INC point, about 10.8 h (my arithmetic). Per rule, the spread of J across candidates and the gap between the best and the default are reported. Reason: a false KILL from under-tuning is the most expensive error for the thesis, and the archived PI with ring constants gave +119 to +180 % delay untuned (literature_v0.md:757).

### 6.11 T2, G and headroom H (report-only)
- **G_S(s), G_T(s)** = median_s(Y_F − Y_O(s)) / median_s(Y_F) for Y ∈ {S, T}, on the held-out seeds (paired), with 95 % CIs: the adaptation room between the fixed and the per-point tuned rule. Because O(s) may switch rules across points, they are labelled "G (oracle with rule switching)"; at the INC point, "adaptation room under the incident".
- **Per rule, in sample** on 100–137: the pooled best vs the per-point best, labelled "biased upwards" (selection on the same seeds); whether the per-point optimal parameters differ across points; the cost of using a neighbouring level's optimum; and the same-rule lookup I_F (F's rule with its per-point best parameters), the CLAUDE.md "I" in its narrow sense.
- **Headroom H** per [AUTHOR A31], decided (b) (V63): no non-learning adaptive scheduler and no MPC arm run in P2 (MPC dropped and disclosed, `system_context.md` §4, allowed under D5.8; the scheduler drop disclosed as a deviation from CLAUDE.md, Method). The quantity reported is labelled "G, i.e. H without the adaptive scheduler", never "H". Prior: archive inflow scheduling gave G of 2–4 % on cost-type metrics.
- Never gated.

### 6.12 Operating map (every scenario point, every arm)
One table per point, tuned, untuned, plant-invalid or INC, with B0 always next to B1 and the tuned rules (each rule's pooled and per-point best, in sample and labelled; O(s) and F, held out): S and T with every §3.1 reported row of their families; h, h_all, h_HDV; Dl; TTT; served throughput with its CI; per-zone travel time; CAV speed give-up; target floor and ceiling occupancy (V47); origin-queue vehicle-hours; censored count; the lane rows (§3.1); validity counts; r_S and r_T vs B0 and vs B1 with CIs; the automation/control decomposition; the guards; band membership; G_S, G_T; the room rows; **C (secondary)** with energy in kWh per vehicle-km by category and powertrain (HDV energy labelled "estimated", V23), scenario totals in kWh and € (V24) and the C ceiling; the metronome and lane-end FLAGs. **Per INC point:** the incident − no-incident contrast per arm (same seeds, common random numbers, **cohort_I in both runs**), the full-cohort rows with the X1/X2-bound and late-arrival subgroups, T's obstacle/other split and the obstacle-dependent label, the queue reach, the blockage and recovery sub-windows, the t_blk distribution per arm, the incident-onset row if the fallback insertion was used, and the keep-only twin at the tuned INC point (LM-E's contribution).

**Sensitivity rows** come from the same runs by re-scoring (no new simulation). They never change the band, but **band membership under each S/T row is reported**, and a point whose membership changes under a row is labelled (e.g. "filter-dependent", "threshold-dependent", "window-dependent"):
- S: S_raw, S_g40, S_g200, S_box300, the Stern form, W2 and W3, S/v̄_W, the imputation forms (§3.1);
- T: T at the other threshold, all-speed TET (also outside the zipper zone), T without the zipper zone, T without imputation, T without the obstacle as a leader (INC), the pooled-ratio form of r_T (§8.1);
- braking: h_all at −2.67 and −4.5 m/s²;
- the guards with a reference-only failure encoded as 0 instead of the arm's worst case (§8.4).
- **C rows** (reported as C rows, with the C-band (secondary) membership under each): the V21 lower-price / resource-cost row and truck fuel net of VAT [AUTHOR A18], with values and sources fixed in Addendum B; truck energy by COPERT and by the TII OGV2 fuel function (V34), both evaluated on each vehicle's **per-edge** average speed (edge length ÷ edge traversal time; ASSUMPTION), with COPERT's extrapolation above 80 km/h disclosed, and whether the sign of r_C vs B0 agrees across the three truck models; BEV energy by a road-load physics model in our code ([AUTHOR A33] (b), V65); C with censored trips completed by imputation and C with origin-queue idle energy [AUTHOR A23].

## 7 Seed allocation

The seed guard (P0-B9) enforces this table in code, and the burned seeds are written to a ledger after each check (as in ARCH report_S1:82-88). An exploratory test once consumed 90 reserved confirmatory seeds in the archive (ARCH t1_meter_gscan_protocol.md:240-243); the guard exists to prevent that. **v2 needs no new seed range**: every new use lies inside the approved roles.

- A seed means one inflow realisation plus one attribute manifest **plus one INC schedule** (T_on, lane 0, x_I, Δ_ins; deterministic); every arm, level, scenario and step on that seed is paired with it. Using the same seed number at several levels or scenarios inside one round (incident and no-incident runs included) is common random numbers across levels and scenarios, not reuse across rounds. The seed guard records the scenario (level, INC point) of every job.
- **No P0 seed is reused in P1**, and no selection seed in the band evaluation.
- Calibration seeds 002–009 may be reused for re-calibration after a plant variant, an insertion change, a step change or a new ACC candidate, because they never feed a PASS, only a STOP, a screen outcome or a design quantity [AUTHOR A16d].
- The archived ranges 7,100,000–7,399,999 are never used.
- If a needed round does not fit in the approved or the requested ranges, the protocol stops until the author approves seeds.
