## 8 Statistics

### 8.1 Unit and convention
The unit of replication is the seed. Every comparison is paired on seeds. The binding statistic is the **median of paired differences** θ = median_s(Y_arm,s − Y_ref,s). Relative effects are r = θ / median_s(Y_ref,s), the denominator over valid reference runs (CLAUDE.md, Statistics; ARCH vsl_lab/eval/stats.py `rel`). The sign is arm − reference throughout (negative = the arm is better on S, T, C or delay); a gain is g = −r. **S and T both use this convention**, so D5.8's "median paired difference" holds literally for both co-primaries. **T is not computable** at a point where median_s(T_ref) = 0: the criterion FAILs there (principle 7). The median of per-seed relative differences is reported alongside as a sensitivity row and never decides; for T, the pooled ratio Σ_s T_A,s / Σ_s T_ref,s with a seed-paired percentile bootstrap is also reported (it is defined whenever any reference seed has exposure). P0-G5 pairs the two steps on the same seed in the same way. Rare-event quantities use pooled ratios (§8.5).

### 8.2 Confidence intervals
Percentile bootstrap over seeds: seed indices are resampled with replacement, and the numerator and denominator medians are both recomputed in every resample. B = 20,000 resamples for 95 % intervals and **B ≥ 200,000 for every bound at 1 − α_c or 1 − 0.05/m**, each printed with its Monte Carlo SE (v2 review ST8; medians of ≤ 60 values cost seconds), generator `numpy.random.default_rng(20261013)` (a fixed analysis seed outside every project block; it seeds only the resampling, never a simulation), recorded per table. **For one (s, A) evaluation, S, T and every guard use the same resample of seed indices** (joint resampling), so that the intersection rule and the J objective are evaluated coherently and the joint pass shares of §6.7 are exact resampling quantities. Two-sided 95 % unless stated; Bonferroni intervals at 1 − α_c or 1 − 0.05/m as stated per family (§8.3); a one-sided bound is the corresponding end of the two-sided interval at the stated level. Shares: Clopper–Pearson. Event rates per exposure: exact Poisson (Garwood); rate ratios also get the exact conditional (binomial) interval as a reported row. Between-run estimators (P0-G1, C0′): bootstrap within the onset and no-onset groups.

### 8.3 Multiplicity

| Family | Structure | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| Criteria inside one check (e.g. G1 (a′), (c), (d); G2; G6; GI per point; T0b over three zones and two quantities; T0e; T0f over zones and levels; T0g; G3 over two levels; the band criteria for one (s, A)) | all must pass (intersection-union) | none needed |
| The two co-primaries inside one (s, A) band claim | intersection (both must pass; [AUTHOR A40] (a)) | none needed for level α. **[AUTHOR A39]:** (a) α_c = α/(2m), the V74 correction for two applied anyway (conservative, recommended); (b) α_c = α/m |
| T0c: Dl, throughput, S, T | union of 4 | Bonferroni, α = 0.0125 each |
| T1b: 2 caps × 4 points × {S, T} | union of 16 (tests at a point found plant-invalid, GI-failed or not computable are dropped, m stays 16) | Bonferroni, α = 0.05/16 |
| P2 band: (s, A) claims over S_tuned and A ∈ {O(s), F} | union of m = 2·\|S_tuned\|, each an intersection over S and T | Bonferroni at α_c per co-primary component (c_S), (c_T), (d_S), (d_T); the guards in the point form of [AUTHOR A27] |
| A later union over the co-primaries (e.g. "beats the best rule on S **or** T") | union of 2 | not used: [AUTHOR A54] (a) requires both; under (b) an extra α/2 on top |
| D5.8 conjuncts (learning phase; fixed now, §12.2) | an intersection-union test over the conjuncts (beats B0 at ℓ*, beats B1, beats the best rule in ≥ 1 regime, never worse in any regime) | none across conjuncts; within them: ℓ* at α/2 per co-primary (A39 (a)) or α (b); "beats the best rule" a union over R regimes, α/(2R) per component; "never worse" per [AUTHOR A57] (point form, no correction) |
| KILL-1, KILL-2, F1–F7 | futility rules; can only stop | none (conservative by construction) |
| T2, G, H, the C ceiling, the room rows, the operating map, sensitivity rows, INC map rows at I1/I3, P0 report rows, C | report-only | none; labelled descriptive (INC map rows are shown at the band's interval level, for comparability) |

The band's family-wise error statement is in §6.8: ≤ 0.025 under A39 (a), ≤ 0.05 under (b), under the null of no gain on at least one co-primary at every pair; the 10 % and 5 % thresholds are point criteria.

### 8.4 Invalid and collapsed runs (V6, [AUTHOR A19])
An invalid or collapsed run is that arm's failure on that seed; an OPS failure is re-run once and is not scored (§2.7). **Incident non-delivery** (§2.7) is an invalid run of that arm. Encoding before any statistic:
- the failed arm-run's S, T, C, Dl and delay-type values are set to **+∞** (worst);
- **effect criteria** (b)–(d) for S and T, and the selection objective: if only the reference (B0 or B1) failed on a seed, the paired difference on that seed is **0** (no credit for a baseline artefact; conservative for claims);
- **guards** (e)–(g): if only the reference failed on a seed, the arm's paired guard difference on that seed is set to the **largest (worst) value of that arm's paired guard differences over the valid seeds** at that point, so that a baseline failure cannot make an arm look harmless; the 0 encoding is reported as a sensitivity row (§6.12);
- if both failed, the arm's failure counts: **+∞**.
Medians and percentile bootstraps are well defined with +∞ as long as fewer than half of the seeds fail; if half or more fail, the statistic is +∞ and the criterion FAILs. Pooled event rates (h, h_all, h_HDV) keep the events and exposure an invalid run recorded (runs continue to the end, `interfaces.md` §9). Failed runs are reported per arm and point and never dropped.

### 8.5 Rare events
CAV hard braking h (V25), all-vehicle hard braking at speed h_all ([AUTHOR A38]), CAV severe deceleration (P0-G3), the HDV rates, TET at 1.5 s (when it is reported as "severe" after the C9 fork; its FLAG uses the exact conditional binomial CI of the pooled exposure ratio, §3.1), collisions, and severe and extreme events use the **pooled rate ratio**: (Σ events / Σ exposure km) of the arm over that of the reference, over the seeds of the comparison, with a percentile bootstrap over seeds. This deviates from the single convention, because many seeds can have zero events and the median of paired differences then degenerates. The deviation is disclosed in every table that uses it. Wherever a ratio is gated in P2 ((f), (f′), (f2)), it is **continuity-corrected**: R̃ = ((k_A + 0.5) / E_A) / ((k_ref + 0.5) / E_ref), with k the pooled counts and E the pooled exposures (ASSUMPTION: a standard 0.5 correction). R̃ is continuous in the counts and defined at zero events, so no event-count switch is needed; with zero events in both arms and equal exposure, R̃ = 1. P0-G3 uses the uncorrected R, because its counts are large (§0.4). **T, the co-primary, is not treated as a rare event:** C9 and G6 require its per-run values to be positive, with ≥ 30 conflict episodes per run, at the claim levels; where a point nevertheless has median T_B0 = 0, T is not computable there (FAIL).

### 8.6 Point-estimate gates vs CI gates
Plant-plausibility checks (P0-G1, G2, G4, G5) and the design checks (C0′ screen, G6 informativeness, GI incident plausibility) gate on point estimates lying in literature-anchored or labelled bands, with CIs reported: they are plausibility checks, not effect claims, and a CI inside a 3–18 % band would need far more runs than the seed block holds. P0-G3(b) gates on a point estimate **and** an upper confidence bound (≤ 1.2), because sequential candidate looks would otherwise let a ratio slightly above 1 pass on noise. Effect claims (T0b, T0c, T0f, T1b, band (c) and (d) for S and T) gate on CIs. T0e and T0g are exact or count gates. The band guards, (f2) included, follow [AUTHOR A27] (point form); (b_S′) and (j) are point criteria and (a′) a count criterion.

### 8.7 Sample sizes
- **Planning approximation** (labelled as such): for normal paired differences, SE(median) ≈ 1.2533 σ/√n, so the half-width of a CI at Bonferroni level z is z × 1.2533 σ/√n. It is used for planning only; KILL-1's thresholds are calibrated empirically instead (§6.5), and the held-out n also uses a resampling check of the whole band rule (§6.7).
- **Planning σ** is the 80 % upper confidence limit of the SD from planning data: factor √(df / χ²₀.₂₀(df)) = 1.351 for the 8 calibration seeds (df = 7) and 1.136 for 30 seeds (df = 29). In v2 it is taken per co-primary and the largest is used.

| Check | n | Justification (my arithmetic) |
|---|---|---|
| P0-G1 | 30 (+10) | P(onset split not computable) ≤ 0.03 for a true onset share in [0.3, 0.7]; the extension gives ≤ 0.016 at 0.25/0.75. Q_dis validity is gated separately (≥ 5 valid runs) |
| P0-G2, G3, G5 | 20 per level and arm | R-d needs ≥ 20 onset runs pooled with G1; wave speed needs ≥ 5 valid estimates; artefact and insertion gates are exact counts; G5 compares paired medians at a 5 % tolerance (archive across-seed CV of the mean time in system 0.030–0.062 for no control, computed in the v1 review from `/home/catalin/work/phd/vsl_lab_runs/t4/gscan_1791047971`, 10 seeds per cell; the paired step SD is unmeasured and reported) |
| P0-G6 | 20 per level | the ≥ 18/20 rule keeps the median convention defined; the S and T factors and the episode count are ASSUMPTIONS (§4.11); ≥ 30 episodes is a floor: T's per-run CV is about √((1 + c_d²)/N), 0.26 at N = 30 and c_d = 1 (ASSUMPTION), so T's noise is taken from the measured σ_plan,1(T), never from the floor |
| P0-GI | 20 per INC point and arm | binding ≥ 16/20 gives P(FAIL) ≈ 0.003 at a true binding share of 0.95 and 0.04 at 0.90; admissibility ≤ 1/20 gives P(PASS) = 0.94 / 0.74 / 0.52 at a true invalid rate of 2 / 5 / 8 % (binomial) |
| C10 screen | 8 per INC point | binding ≥ 7/8 gives P(fail) = 0.06 at a true share of 0.95 and 0.19 at 0.90 (binomial); a screen, not a gate |
| T0b, T0c | 30 | a 5 % effect is resolved when σ ≤ 0.10 (95 % half-width 4.5 %); T0c at z = 2.498 (m = 4) |
| T0f | 30 per arm and level | (a) at a paired lane-share SD of 25 pp, the 95 % half-width ≈ 11 pp, so a true +30 pp clears the 20-pp bar with its CI; (b) at an assumed SD of 4 pp, ≈ 1.8 pp (SDs unmeasured; ASSUMPTIONS) |
| T1b | 30 | z = 2.955 (m = 16); MDE at 80 % power ≈ 0.87 σ (about 4 % at σ = 0.05, 9 % at σ = 0.10) |
| P2 stage 1 | 8 | t_S and t_T calibrated so that a true 10 %/10 % candidate is falsely screened out with probability ≤ 1 % per level at the P1 levels (union bound over two 0.5 % quantiles, over the R4 and lane samples; §6.5); not calibrated at other levels or at the INC point. The guard screen carries a noise allowance: a neutral candidate passes the delay screen with 0.995 / 0.982 at σ_D = 0.05 / 0.10 (§6.5) |
| P2 stage 2 | 30 (selection on 38) | 95 % half-width ≈ 0.40 σ on the 38 selection seeds (≈ 0.45 σ on the 30 stage-2 seeds alone) |
| P2 held-out | 40 or 60 | §6.7: under A39 (a) at m = 18 (z_c = 3.197), the Bonferroni half-width is ≤ 0.05 when σ_plan,2 ≤ 0.079 (n = 40) or ≤ 0.097 (n = 60), σ_plan,2 now taken over vs B0 and vs B1 and over the no-incident points ([AUTHOR A61]); plus the predicted pass rate of the whole joint rule. The joint operating characteristic of §6.8 is orientation only (true 12 % on both: 0.78 if independent, ≤ 0.88, lower under negative dependence) |
| Band guard (e), for orientation (v1, m = 16, z = 2.955) | 40 | under a **non-inferiority** form at α/m, an arm with a true Δ_D = 0 passes with probability 0.17 / 0.026 / 0.007 at σ_D = 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20; under the decided point form, 0.98 / 0.84 / 0.69; an arm with a true +5 % passes the point form with probability 0.001 / 0.065 at σ_D = 0.05 / 0.10 (normal approximation; [AUTHOR A27]) |
| Band guards (f), (f2), for orientation | — | a non-inferiority bound exp(z·√(2/N)) at z = 2.955 and equal true rates is about 1.52 / 1.21 / 1.14 at N = 100 / 500 / 1,000 events per arm; about 1,920 events per arm are needed to reach 1.10 (Poisson, no overdispersion; my arithmetic). The decided point form avoids this |

Incident teleport risk (binomial; the toy rate is not plant data): P(B0 invalid share ≤ 5 %) = 0.95 at n = 40 if the true rate is 2 %, 0.68 at 5 %, 0.37 at 8 % (my arithmetic). The archive's failures of habit are the reason for this table: a 10-seed screen had CI widths of about ±40 s against a 10 s target, and in R5-M2 a 3–5 % effect was below what 100 seeds resolved per learner (ARCH t1_meter_gscan_protocol.md:126,238).

### 8.8 Share and count gates, and the single admissibility rule
Every share-based gate states its minimum n and tolerance (the archive stopped learners spuriously at 1 of 96 > 1 % and 4 of 64 > 5 %; ARCH t1_meter_gscan_protocol.md:112-118). Two roles are kept apart:
- **Realism artefact gates** (P0-G1(d) on its original 30 runs; P0-G2(c) at λ_c and at a λ_2 ≤ λ_c + 0.1; P0-G3(a); T1a(i) at the P1 levels ≤ λ_c + 0.1): exact counts with **zero tolerance** for teleports, invalid runs and health FAILs, and ≤ 2 collisions in total, over the stated runs. Near critical the plant must be clean; these are realism properties of the plant, as the brief requires a drop or discharge "without artefacts". Whether to split them into code-integrity FAILs (zero tolerance) and rate-based traffic-artefact gates was an option in [AUTHOR A24] (decided: no split).
- **Admissibility of a level or INC point** (one rule everywhere it is used: P0-G2(c) at λ_2 > λ_c + 0.1, T1a(ii) at the P1 levels above λ_c + 0.1, P0-GI(c), band (a)): the B0 invalid share at that point ≤ **5 %** over the runs of that check (≤ 1 of 20, ≤ 1 of 30, ≤ 2 of 40, ≤ 3 of 60), incident non-delivery included at INC points. A level that fails it, and every grid level above it, is plant-invalid: kept in the map, never tuned, never in the band. An INC point that fails it is plant-invalid for INC (kept in the map) and does not affect the no-incident level at the same λ.
- **Arm validity:** stage-1 advancement ≤ 1 invalid run of 8 (a screen, with a flagged fill-in rule, §6.5); selection eligibility and band (h) ≤ 5 % (≤ 1 of 38; ≤ 2 of 40; ≤ 3 of 60).

---

## 9 FAIL handling and deviations

### 9.1 Kinds of FAIL

| Kind | Checks | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| Code | P0-B, P1-B, P2-B, T0a, T0d, T0e, T3 | fix; no project seed consumed for unit tests; plant runs affected by the bug are re-run (§9.3); logged in the commit and the ledger |
| Operations | a hung or killed worker (§2.7) | re-run once on the same seed, logged; a second hang is a FAIL of that arm, reported |
| Calibration futility | F1–F3 | P0 FAIL path (§9.2) without spending a check seed |
| Calibration futility | F4 | [AUTHOR A11]: P0 KILL, or a new CAV base model under a new protocol |
| Calibration futility | F5 | STOP; a re-designed plant or inflow needs a new protocol ([AUTHOR A26] superseded by V58/V64) |
| Metric futility | **F6** (C9), **P0-G6** | **STOP** ([AUTHOR A53]): no V74 claim is measurable on this plant; principle 14 (no plant change to make a metric informative); a new metric needs a new protocol |
| Capacity-drop futility | **F7** (only under [AUTHOR A42] (a)) | P0 KILL without a check seed |
| Plant | P0-G1 (gated parts), G2 (a)–(c), T1a | STOP; the plant-variant ladder (§9.2), with author approval |
| Capacity drop | G1(b), T1a ρ_T1 | per [AUTHOR A42]: report-only with the limitation sentence under (b); §9.2 under (a) |
| Insertion | P0-G2 (d) | the next insertion configuration of the §2.2 order; C1–C11 except C0′ re-run on 002–009, then G1 and G2 on fresh seeds; not a plant-model variant; counts in k |
| ACC base model | P0-G3 | the next surviving ACC candidate (§2.6 C4) on fresh seeds; G3 and G5 re-run; counts in k; exhausted → [AUTHOR A11] |
| Step | P0-G5 | as decided in [AUTHOR A20]; counts in k |
| Energy | P0-G4 | disclosed model or parameter fix; G4 re-run (BEV per [AUTHOR A33]) |
| Incident plausibility | C10 screen (with the waiting-time margin of [AUTHOR A64]), **P0-GI** | the INC point (or the regime) is dropped from P1/P2 and disclosed; **not** a P0 FAIL; never counted in k |
| Incident integrity | **T0g**, P0-GI(d), the §2.7 row | code cause: injector fix, affected INC runs re-run on re-round seeds (§9.3); traffic cause: the pre-registered fallback insertion (§2.4b), then the regime is dropped if it also fails |
| Speed actuator | T0b, T0c | as decided in [AUTHOR A34] |
| Lane actuator | **T0f** | [AUTHOR A46]: lane commands removed from every arm (recommended), disclosed |
| Resolution futility | the P1 planning SDs (§5) | [AUTHOR A68] (recommended (b)): if the point SD of S or of T exceeds 0.29, STOP before seed 100; never a claim |
| Usefulness | T1b | as decided in [AUTHOR A17] (proceed to P2) |
| Claim | P2 | KILL-1, KILL-2, KILL-3 (decided on no-incident levels only), or GO; no plant variant inside P2 |

### 9.2 Plant-variant ladder and the settings counters (fixed now; each use needs the author's approval and a dated deviation note)
- **The C0′ capacity-drop ladder is a calibration-only selection** made before any check seed (§2.6). It is not the plant-variant ladder. Under [AUTHOR A41] counting (i): the adopted setting is the original plant (k = 1), and every other setting screened on calibration seeds is listed in **k_cal**, reported next to k in every verdict sentence (v1's rule for calibration-only candidates). Under counting (ii), every screened setting also counts in k.
- **At most two plant-model variants after the adopted plant** (V59). Each variant re-runs calibration C1–C11 except C0′ on 002–009 ([AUTHOR A16d]) and **all** of P0 on fresh seeds from the requested range ([AUTHOR A16c]), then P1.
- **Settings counter k.** k counts every plant setting whose check ran on check seeds: the adopted plant counts 1, and every later insertion configuration, ACC candidate checked on fresh seeds, step change and plant-model variant adds 1. **k ≤ 7**: the original plus at most six changes (V59); an eighth would be a **P0 KILL**. Candidates rejected in calibration only (the ACC pre-screen, C3, C4, the C0′ screens) do not count in k but are listed in the P0 report (k_cal for C0′). k and k_cal appear in every P0 verdict sentence and in the thesis text (principle 11).
- **Order rule after a check-seed FAIL of G1 (gated parts), G2 (a)–(c) or T1a (fixed now, not chosen after seeing the failure):**
  1. **the C0′ ladder continues** from the setting after the adopted one, in the C0′ order: a setting not yet screened is screened on 002–009 with the C0′ screen; a setting already screened uses its recorded screen values (no new runs). Under [AUTHOR A42] (b) the continuation screen drops the ρ_cal condition (G1 no longer gates ρ) and keeps every other condition; under (a) it keeps all of them. The first setting that passes is variant 1;
  2. **then V-2: HDV cars on SUMO IDM defaults**, screened in the same way first. In the archive P3 pre-check, IDM at its defaults discharged a standing single-lane queue at 2,323–2,335 veh/h/lane with 0 teleports (ARCH docs/lab/day_pattern/p3_precheck.md:9-18). This shows that IDM's car-following does not by itself hold discharge below the floor. It is not a merge discharge: every archive merge plant discharged less, and 2,323–2,335 lies above this protocol's 2,300 bound, so whether F3Z passes with IDM is exactly what V-2 tests. IDM stranded ramp vehicles at a dead-end acceleration lane in MRG3 (ARCH round2_protocol.md:119-123); F3Z has no dead-end acceleration lane, but stranding remains possible (§2.1) and the detector stays. If CD3 or CD4 was adopted, V-2 replaces those HDV car models (disclosed);
  3. variant 2 is the next passing setting in the same order. No passing setting left → **P0 KILL**.
- **V-1 (zipper-type junctions at the OR1 and OR2 merges) is removed** ([AUTHOR A41] (iv)): the pilot found that the merges never become active bottlenecks below λ = 1.0 (V73, not data), V71 placed no zone before merge 1, and BN* is expected to be BN-LD. (v1's rationale: archive ZM3 produced the only measurable merge drop, ρ = 0.967, but its discharge of 1,528 veh/h/lane failed the floor; ARCH t2_zipmerge_protocol.md:31-38.)
- **A lane drop without the zipper node** is CD2, the last C0′ setting (the brief's C2). It is not expected to help: the archive's non-zipper lane drop LD3 (priority nodes, ARCH vsl_lab/netgen/mrg3.py:29, :54) failed realism (H5 Q_dis not computable at n = 0+8, H3 ρ = 1.037; ARCH docs/lab/t2_realism_ld3.md), the pilot's plain lane end carried an unrealistic 2,310 veh/h/lane with almost no queue (V69, not data), and in the mechanics toy a dead-end lane formed a queue in only 3 of 5 runs. The zipper's own behaviour is watched through the metronome FLAG and the lane-end attribution (§2.7).
- **Never used as a fix:** `lcAssertive` (it moved onsets to the entry); driver error terms above the SUMO defaults (the H4 collapse), **except inside the pre-registered CD3 and CD4 settings adopted through the C0′ screen** ([AUTHOR A41] (v), §1.1 item 34); a higher time-to-teleport; any bound change; any zipper visibility other than CD1's 20 m; any variation inside a CD setting; changing W, the S trend kernel, TTC\* (beyond the C9 fork), the speed condition, K or cohort_I after data; any plant change to make S or T informative (principle 14); any rubbernecking or driver-parameter step to bring F_inc into its band (principle 10).
- **Not plant-model variants** (they do not count towards the two, but they count in k): the insertion configuration (G2(d)), ACC candidates (G3), the step (G5). The TTC\* fork (C9) and the INC outcomes (C10, GI) are not plant settings: never in k, always reported.
- **Hard stop:** after the second variant fails, or when k would exceed 7, **P0 KILL**: "no realistic, artefact-free F3Z plant with this fleet in SUMO 1.27.1 at the chosen step, after k plant settings on check seeds and k_cal screened on calibration seeds". A headline on a plant with a disclosed realism FAIL is ruled out ([AUTHOR A1], V59). Under [AUTHOR A42] (b), a plant without a capacity drop is not a realism FAIL: it carries the mandatory limitation sentence (§4.4). A re-designed plant needs a new protocol.

### 9.3 Bugs found after results
Fix, re-run everything affected, log both outcomes (CLAUDE.md). **Asymmetry** (archive decision W13, ARCH day_pattern_decisions_v0.md:67): a fix that turns a PASS into a FAIL counts at once; a fix that turns a FAIL into a PASS on the *same* seeds does not count. The check is re-run on fresh seeds (the 138–199 reserve in P2, the requested range in P0–P1), and only that run can PASS. The asymmetry applies equally to the S, T, lane and incident code.

### 9.4 Addenda (dated, committed before the data they govern; never relax a bound)
- **B** before seed 7,400,000 (no data needed), because C remains a reported secondary that needs frozen VoT and prices: VoT_car and VoT_truck in 2026 € with every step written out (`interfaces.md` §5.4, V54; only the uplift is open); the V21 prices with their date; the sensitivity-row prices and their sources; the energy constants (LHV, density); the road-load values used by the G4 acceleration floor and the BEV physics row ([AUTHOR A33] (b)); the hash of these constants (T3c).
- **A** after P0-S: estimator or implementation fixes found by the smoke test; it may also fix the incident injector's Δ_ins if B16's free-road value proves wrong on F3Z.
- **D** (informational, not a deviation) after P0-C and whenever needed: CPU plan updates.
- **C** before seed 7,400,060, before any commanding arm runs on a project seed: R1–R3 parameter spaces (inner and outer ranges, defaults, dimensions, the joint samplers with n_cand and the sampled points), the lane modules (ranges, constants, mapping text, RNG keys), the R4 grid, the incident-message format, and the frozen observation specs `local`, `zone` and `lane` (§6.3, [AUTHOR A30, A47, A48]). If [AUTHOR A46] removes lane commands after T0f, Addendum C is re-issued before seed 100 without the lane parts.
- **No addendum may move** the incident's lane, onset, position or duration, the TTC\* fork rule, the windows W and cohort_I (relative to T_on), the S trend kernel or the speed condition after seed 7,400,000.
- A value resolved by external source research (V57) enters only through one of these addenda, before the data it governs, after Claude Code has spot-checked its citation.

### 9.5 Deviations
Any departure from this protocol is a dated deviation note in the phase report: what changed, why, which result prompted it, the author's approval, and the outcome under the original rule next to the new one. **Zone moves** (V56) follow [AUTHOR A34]: a move applies to every arm, uses fresh seeds from [AUTHOR A16c], re-runs P1 and, if P2 has started, P2 (P0 is not re-run, because B0 and B1 carry no commands), and has its own cost row (§10.2). After a move, the band and the regime set are re-fixed by the unchanged band rule in a dated addendum before any validation-seed evaluation of a learning arm, and they are frozen with the zones before the confirmatory run (V56). A zone move does not move the incident (x_I stays 13,000 m) unless the author decides otherwise before seed 000 of the re-round.

### 9.6 Records
- **Every run goes into the ledger**, including FAIL, invalid, OPS, crashed and abandoned ones, with the T3c fields. Health columns are filled by job code from the run summaries, never by hand. Each row also holds: the scenario (level, INC point); M_cmd and the TTC\* in force; the C0′ setting id; the INC schedule hash and the realised t_blk; the masked-action count; the `lane` spec hash.
- **SUMO crash:** the same seed is re-run once; a second crash keeps the run failed and reported (`interfaces.md` §9). **Hung worker:** killed, marked OPS and re-run once (§2.7). **Collapse:** killed at the CPU-time cap and marked failed for its arm.
- Raw runs go under `/home/catalin/work/phd/` (ext4), in a v5.B.1-only run root (not the archive's); the repo holds summaries only (< 5 MB per file). Raw SSM output is never kept beyond the C11 and B14 aggregation.
- One report per phase, with the verdict, the values against each criterion, the burned seeds, k, k_cal, the TTC\* in force, the INC points kept, and the deviations.

## 10 CPU budget and schedule

### 10.1 Per-episode cost (ASSUMPTION, to be measured in P0-S and P0-C)
- **Measured anchors:**
  - the pre-P0 pilot (V69; not data, but a cost measurement on the V68 layout): the run `base_B1_l0.90_s1` logged 146.9 s of wall time with UPS 329,648, i.e. about 4.8 × 10⁷ vehicle-steps (24 workers in parallel, the emissions device on; read from its log in this synthesis);
  - v1's archive anchor (CORR2 G-scan, 3 lanes, 9.5 km, EIDM, 0.2 s): median 112 s wall per run (range 87–161 s) for about 6,674 vehicles and 5,475 s simulated, under concurrent load; 360 runs took 1,937 s of batch wall time, i.e. about 21 effective parallel runs on 32 workers (`/home/catalin/work/phd/vsl_lab_runs/t4/gscan_1791047971`).
- **Added per vehicle-step** (toys, unloaded): the plain-getter ground-truth read about 1.34 µs (v1 toy); own TTC through `getLeader` about +0.74–1.0 µs (evidence: mechanics, metrics); the lane logic about 1.3 µs per `couldChangeLane` call, a few calls per CAV per 2 s (negligible); Python metric accumulation and boundary logic about 25–50 s per run (ASSUMPTION).
- **Estimate** (my arithmetic): at λ = 0.9, 147 s + 65 s (read) + 36–48 s (TTC) + 25–50 s + 4 s (the per-step trace hash as bytes, T3a; about 39 s if it were built from formatted strings) ≈ 276–315 s. **That adds unloaded toy per-call costs to a loaded pilot time** (v2 review SF10): the pilot ran SUMO at about 3.0 µs per vehicle-step under load (146.9 s / 4.84 × 10⁷), the mechanics toy at about 2.0 µs unloaded (a different net; indicative only), and scaling the added per-call costs by that factor of about 1.5 gives up to ≈ 375 s at λ = 0.9 (`review_fix/desk_review.py`). λ = 1.0 has more vehicle-steps (the pilot's B0 time lost per vehicle doubled from 241 to 489 s). **Planning value kept at v1's 350 s per episode at λ = 1.0 (range 250–500 s); throughput about 200 episodes per hour** at 21 effective parallel runs. The planning value may be 15–25 % low at the upper levels; the 250–500 s range covers that, P0-S measures the full pipeline at 32 workers (§4.2), and [AUTHOR A50]'s acceptance is explicitly conditional on Addendum D. Lower levels are cheaper, plateau runs cost about 0.7 times, 0.1-s runs about twice as much, and SSM runs about 2.5 times, so the totals below are conservative for the sweep.
- **The SSM device never enters production:** about +160–215 s per project-size run (estimate from toy per-vehicle-step costs, evidence: mechanics), which over ≈ 13,000 episodes would add ≈ 600–800 core-hours (my arithmetic). It is used only in C11 (18 runs) and the B14 micro-scenarios.

### 10.2 Budget per phase (episodes; 0.1-s runs counted twice; my arithmetic, `synth/desk_synth.py`)

| Phase | Episodes (≈) | Wall at 200 per hour | Code needed first |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0-B | micro-scenarios only (incl. ACC pre-screen, P0-G4, B14–B16) | < 0.5 h | plant, inflow, health, metrics (S, T, h_all), stats, seeds, ops, incident injector, SSM harness |
| P0-S | 14 | < 0.1 h | — |
| P0-C (C0′, C1–C8, C9, C10, C11) | 280–610 eq (CD0 adopted → full ladder with C1–C8 re-run) | ≈ 1.4–3.1 h | CD variants, CAV vType (B1) |
| P0-G1 | 30 (+ 10) | ≈ 0.2 h | — |
| P0-G2, G3 (G6 reads them) | 120 | ≈ 0.6 h | — |
| P0-G5 | 80 at 0.1 s ≈ 160 eq | ≈ 0.8 h | — |
| P0-GI | ≤ 120 | ≈ 0.6 h | — |
| P1 | 660 + 22 replicates + 10 SSM re-runs (≈ 25 eq) | ≈ 3.5 h | exec (speed + lane), v2x (lane fields, incident message), R4, R-KEEP, R-LANE |
| P2 stage 1 (8 levels + the INC point, n_cand = 20) | 5,256 | ≈ 26.3 h | R1–R3, LM-B, LM-E, O, tuning code (after Addendum C) |
| P2 stage 2 | 3,780–4,860 | ≈ 18.9–24.3 h | — |
| P2 held-out (n = 40 / 60; twins on 40; INC map rows) | 2,480 / 3,360 | ≈ 12.4 / 16.8 h | — |
| **Total, unconditional** | **≈ 12,900–15,200** | **≈ 65–76 h** | ≈ 1,260–1,480 core-hours at 350 s |
| Range at 250–500 s per episode | | ≈ 43–101 h | |
| P2 alone (stage 1 + stage 2 + held-out) | ≈ 11,500–13,500 | ≈ 58–67 h | |
| Conditional | G1 extension 10; C1/C2 extensions 16–40; edge level ≈ 1,364 (≈ 6.8 h); parameter refinement ≈ 2,160 (≈ 10.8 h); each +5 candidates per rule in stage 1 under n_cand = max(20, 5 · d) ≈ 1,080 (≈ 5.4 h); per plant variant ≈ 700 eq for C1–C11 and P0 + a P1 repeat ≈ 680 (≈ 7 h); per ACC re-check ≈ 280 eq (G3 and G5 on fresh seeds, ≈ 1.4 h); T0g fallback ≈ 240 (B16 + GI + P1 INC re-runs, ≈ 1.2 h); a move to 0.1 s ≈ doubles P1 and P2 (≈ +60–70 h); per zone move ≈ 3.4 h for P1 plus ≈ 58–67 h for P2 if P2 has started | | |
| **[AUTHOR A44] alternatives** | (a) I1–I3 all tuned: ≈ 15,100–17,600 (≈ 75–88 h); (c) INC report-only (held-out map rows only): ≈ 11,800–14,000 (≈ 59–70 h); dropping the keep-only twins saves ≈ 720 (≈ 3.6 h) | | |

**The 72-h checkpoint.** v1's rule stays: if Addendum D's re-stated projection **for P2** exceeds 72 h, the author is asked before P2 (accept, or an addendum reducing the candidate counts, written before any P2 data). At the planning value P2 is ≈ 58–67 h, but the **total** of ≈ 65–76 h already reaches the 72-h mark at its upper end, so [AUTHOR A50] asks for the budget before seed 7,400,000 instead of before P2.

### 10.3 Where each KILL path stops (cumulative batch time, planning value; my arithmetic)

| Stop | Cumulative batch time |
|---|---|
| Calibration futility (F1–F5), F6 or F7 | ≈ 1.5–3.1 h |
| P0-G1 FAIL | ≈ 1.6–3.3 h |
| P0-G2/G3/G5/G6 FAIL | ≈ 3.6–5.3 h |
| P1 actuator FAIL (speed; the lane actuator is removed, not KILLed) | ≈ 7–9 h |
| Resolution-futility STOP after P1 ([AUTHOR A68] (b)) | ≈ 7–9 h |
| KILL-1 | ≈ 33–35 h (≈ 36–49 h when an edge level and/or the refinement is triggered) |
| KILL-2 | ≈ 52–59 h (saves the held-out stage, ≈ 12–17 h) |
| Held-out stage (GO or KILL-3) | ≈ 65–76 h |

KILL-1's saving of about 30–40 h is real only when the calibrated thresholds are clearly positive (under the normal approximation, σ_plan,1 ≲ 0.07 on both co-primaries); when a threshold is near or below 0, KILL-1 rarely fires and KILL-2 is the realistic early KILL. Raising n₁ from 8 to 12 would lift the thresholds at the cost of about +2,628 stage-1 episodes (≈ +13 h at 9 points; my arithmetic); it is not adopted, and the trade-off stays recorded in [AUTHOR A15]. **An incident failure never stops the protocol**; a lane-actuator failure removes the lane commands and lets P1 continue.

### 10.4 Operating rules (CLAUDE.md, binding)
32 workers on all hardware threads, ≤ 100 processes; the CPU guard ported and unit-tested before the first batch (launch only below 90 %; after 2 min above 90 % pause the newest job, then one more every 30 s; resume one per 30 s after 30 s at ≤ 87 %), with every pause logged; job order shuffled across arms and scenarios within each batch; a resource check before every batch; no overlap with v6 runs (v6 sessions confirmed idle, else CPU load < 15 %; v5.B is the primary line and has CPU priority); every batch in its own `systemd-run --user` unit, resumable from the ledger; background jobs only, logs tailed at most every 10 min; package temperature logged per batch; fans never driven by batch code; per-run CPU-time cap (C8) and hang detection (§2.7). **Power traces (V10)** are written only for a pre-declared subset: the golden run, the T3a replicates, the P0-G4 tests, the P0-G3 runs on seed 7,400,040, and in the P2 held-out stage the lowest seed per (scenario point, arm), the INC points included [AUTHOR A35]. **Disk (ASSUMPTION):** per-vehicle summaries (including per-edge traversal times for the truck sensitivity rows, σ̃_i², TET_i, TIT_i and conflict episodes) of about 0.7–2.2 MB per run; 10-s probe and section series only for P0, P1 and held-out runs (about 5 MB each); power traces about 0.2 GB per traced run (float32, compressed), about 14 GB for about 70 traced runs; about 30–40 GB in all, free space checked before each batch. Raw SSM output is aggregated on the fly and never kept (about 4 MB per toy run with trajectories; evidence: mechanics).

### 10.5 Schedule (order and gates; effort and ultracode per the CLAUDE.md phase table)

| Step | Content | Gate | Effort, ultracode |
|---|---|---|---|
| S0 | Author review of v2; every new [AUTHOR] item A36–A68 answered; Addendum B; v2 approved in a commit | approval | Extra High, on |
| S1 | Port `ops`, plant (incl. CD variants and the incident injector), inflow, runner, metrics (C, S, T, h_all), stats (joint resampling), seed guard; P0-B green (incl. ACC pre-screen, P0-G4, B14–B16) | all P0-B green | High, off |
| S2 | P0-S → Addendum A → P0-C (C0′, C1–C11) → calibration report → Addendum D | futility stops not triggered | High, off (Extra High, on to interpret the C0′ and C9 outcomes) |
| S3 | P0-G1, then P0-G2, G3, G5, G6, GI → P0 report | §4.10 | Extra High, on (interpreting a gate) |
| S4 | Addendum C (content, before seed 060); `exec` (speed + lane), `v2x` (lane fields, incident message), R4, R-KEEP, R-LANE; P1-B green → P1 → P1 report (incl. the calibrated t_S, t_T) | §5 verdict | High, off to build; Extra High, on to interpret |
| S5 | R1–R3, LM-B, LM-E, O, tuning machinery, C ceiling; P2-B green | checkpoint "plant and all opponents done" | High, on |
| S6 | Stage 1 → §6.10 additions → KILL-1 → stage 2 → KILL-2 → n_eval → held-out → band → P2 report | §6.8–6.9 | High, off to run; Extra High, on to interpret |

The critical path is engineering (S1, S4, S5), not CPU. R1 and R4 stage-1 runs can overlap the building of R2 and R3 (§6.3).

## 11 [AUTHOR] decisions needed (each before any data it governs)

**v1's items A1–A35 were all answered on 2026-10-13 (V58–V65)**; their table is kept below as the record (§11.1). Items written for the v0 or V64 layout (A6, A10, A13, A26, A30) are read through §2.1, which folds in V68 and V71. **The new items A36–A54 (§11.2) and A55–A68 (§11.4, from the v2 design review) are open.** Every one is due **before seed 7,400,000**, because each governs data from the calibration seeds on; the content that A47 and A48 decide is frozen in Addendum C before seed 7,400,060. None needs data. The primary design above uses each recommendation; nothing is decided silently. §11.3 lists the v1 items that the new ones re-open; the consequential ones are put to the author in A67.

### 11.1 v1 items A1–A35 (answered 2026-10-13; record)

| Id | Decision | Options | Cost / consequence | Recommendation | Needed before | Author answer (2026-10-13) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | What a P0 FAIL means | (i) KILL F3Z at the first FAIL; (ii) ≤ 2 pre-registered plant-model variants and ≤ 6 plant settings in all (§9.2), each a full calibration + P0 on fresh seeds, then KILL; (iii) continue as a "SUMO plant, realism FAIL disclosed" claim | (i) cheapest, but the thesis then needs another plant; (ii) ≈ 5 h CPU per variant and a new seed range; (iii) weak for a thesis and a hidden-variant risk | **(ii), then KILL; never (iii)** for the headline | seed 000 | **(ii), then KILL; never (iii)** (V59: at most 2 variants and 6 setting changes in all, each on fresh seeds, then P0 KILL) |
| A2 | Drop band | (a) [0.82, 0.97]; (b) lower bound 0.79 when the median v_cong < 10 km/h (the Yuan et al. 2015 relation against their pre-queue capacity of 6,300 veh/h; 0.73 against their maximum observed 6,840 veh/h); (c) upper bound moved towards the real US-169 SB ρ ≈ 0.98 (archive D3.1 waived ≥ 3 % there; I-394 ≈ 0.968) | (a) strictest, highest FAIL risk; a plant with a high queue speed can show ρ ≈ 1 for a realistic reason (§4.4); (b) one more estimator, admits deep-queue drops, and its value depends on the capacity reference; (c) admits a plant with almost no drop, which removes the mechanism the controllers target | **(a)**, with v_cong and (b) reported as rows only | seed 000 | (a) (V65) |
| A3 | Queue-discharge upper bound | 2,300 or 2,400 veh/h/lane | 2,400 admits flows above the highest Dutch observation (2,280) and the HCM-derived ≈ 2,000 | **2,300** | seed 000 | 2,300 veh/h/lane (V65) |
