### 11.2 New items A36–A54 (v2; open)

| Id | Decision | Options | Cost / consequence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A36** | Smoothness co-primary S: form, window, filter (V74) | (a) S = pooled within-vehicle SD of the **300-s high-passed** speed, fixed planned-km weights, worst-case imputation below 150 samples, window **W1** = the route upstream of 15,500 m (§3.1);<br>(b) the same without the high-pass (S_raw; the metrics evidence package's recommendation);<br>(c) the Stern pooled SD over W.<br>Windows: W1; W2 = [8,000, 15,500) m (the "jam area"); W3 = the whole trip. Filters: 120 / 300 / 600 s | (a) one constant (§3.2's 5-min high-pass filters Eulerian probe series and does not by itself justify this per-vehicle filter; v2 review BDP2-1). The trend kernel is decided in [AUTHOR A58] (recommended: a Gaussian with σ_g = 100 s, the same half-power period as the 300-s boxcar, plus the S_raw co-confirmation). In the rigor toy's heavy queue the boxcar keeps 48 % of the within-vehicle variance (26 % at 120 s), the part a wave damper can act on. Still crawl-sensitive (−32 % under the toy's 50 km/h crawl), which the delay guard handles.<br>(b) no constant, but about half of its variance in a heavy queue is the contrast between free-flow and queue speeds, which damping cannot remove, so it tracks queue depth and delay more than speed fluctuation. (No claim is made that a given manipulation moves S_raw more than (a): under the toy's 70 km/h limit S_raw fell 6.6 % and (a) fell 10.1 %.)<br>(c) adds between-vehicle and peak-trend variance; −50 % under the toy crawl.<br>W2 can be escaped by pushing fluctuation upstream of 8 km; W3 adds the 2.75-km two-lane exit (a free-flow dilution floor); W1 reads V74's "jam area" broadly (§1.1 item 35) | **(a), W1, with the trend of A58**; (b), (c), the other filters, W2, W3 and the strips as pre-registered rows |
| **A37** | Safety co-primary T: threshold and speed condition (V74) | (a) **TTC\* = 1.5 s at follower speed ≥ 10 m/s** (V74 literal), with the C9 informativeness pre-check (T > 0 in ≥ 7/8 calibration runs, median ≥ 3 × T_floor, ≥ 30 conflict episodes per run) and a one-way fallback to 3.0 s recorded before seed 010, then P0-G6 on check seeds;<br>(b) TTC\* = 3.0 s at speed now (amends V74's number), with 1.5 s reported as "severe";<br>(c) 1.5 s at all speeds | (a) keeps the decided number and costs no runs (C9 re-uses the C1 runs). The fork is data-dependent, but it uses B0 only, on throw-away seeds, by a rule fixed now, before any arm exists. Every toy predicts that it fires (rigor toy: 0.003 s per 1,000 veh-km at 1.5 s in a heavy queue, below free flow's 0.041).<br>(b) amends a decided value before data on toy evidence alone (EIDM, 3 seeds); (a) reaches the same place if the toys are right. 3.0 s is the SUMO SSM default and the freeway value of the Abdel-Aty & Wang review (Table 3).<br>(c) counts low-speed closing in queues and zipper creep, which B14 excludes | **(a)** (changed from the rigor draft's (b): it honours V74 at no extra cost) |
| **A38** | Hard braking under V74 (V74: safety = "time spent at TTC < 1.5 s, **plus hard-braking events**", each co-primary ≥ 10 %) | (a) **the CAV constraint (f) as decided + an all-vehicle at-speed constraint (f2) vs B0 and B1**, y = 10 %: hard braking must not get worse by more than 10 %, and is **not** part of the ≥ 10 % improvement (**a narrowing of V74's text**);<br>(b) the CAV constraint only (v1; HDV rows report-only, A29);<br>(c) **V74 literal, reading 1:** h_all as a third co-primary that must improve by ≥ 10 % (α/3);<br>(d) **V74 literal, reading 2:** a composite safety index (TET and hard braking) that must improve by ≥ 10 % | (a) **removes hard braking from the ≥ 10 % improvement that V74 attaches to safety**: it only has to stay within +10 % (and, for the word "safer", within +0 %, [AUTHOR A56]). One more intersection component, so a lower pass rate. The vs-B0 part may bind if the ACC brakes more than humans (G3 exists to prevent that). It closes the neighbour-braking route of lane commands (urgent mode up to 2.4 × the baseline in the toy) and makes the safety claim coherent vs B0. It supersedes A29 (a) for at-speed events and keeps the decided CAV gate (V25/V36, A28 (α′)) unchanged.<br>(b) leaves "push braking onto HDVs" open while T counts HDVs: an inconsistent safety claim.<br>(c) closest to V74's wording; costs power and raises the bar (a third co-primary on a rate that is rare at speed in the toys).<br>(d) also V74-literal, but needs arbitrary weights | **(a), as an explicit narrowing of V74 that the author approves** (the protocol no longer settles the reading itself, §1.1 item 21; v2 review HC-5); **(c)** if the author reads V74 as requiring hard braking to improve |
| **A39** | Multiplicity reading of V74 ("a correction for testing two") | (a) apply the decided correction anyway: α_c = α/(2m) per co-primary component;<br>(b) exact intersection-union: α_c = α/m, with an extra /2 only for unions over the co-primaries | (a) FWER ≤ 0.025 (conservative); z_c = 3.197 instead of 2.991 at m = 18; the n = 40 σ threshold falls from 0.084 to 0.079 (my arithmetic). The cost is small because the 10 % point criterion usually binds first.<br>(b) exact and more powerful, but reinterprets the V74 update's wording ("each needing ≥ 10 % … with a multiplicity correction for testing two") | **(a)** |
| **A40** | Band metric (re-reads V45 under V74) | (a) **both co-primaries, the same arm** (intersection), each ≥ 10 % vs B0 and ≥ 5 % vs B1;<br>(b) either co-primary (union, extra /2);<br>(c) S only;<br>(d) both, but possibly by different arms | (a) mirrors the D5.8 claim exactly. Joint P(enter) at a true 12 % / 12 % ≈ 0.78 if the estimates are independent, up to 0.88 if perfectly correlated, against 0.88 for one co-primary (σ 0.085, n 40, m 18); at a true 10 % / 10 %, 0.25–0.50. The highest KILL risk.<br>(b) can GO at points where no rule shows the joint effect the claim needs; m doubles.<br>(c) ignores safety when screening.<br>(d) never shows joint feasibility in one controller | **(a)**; J = max(J_S, J_T), and KILL-1 and KILL-2 follow it |
| **A41** | Capacity-drop ladder (V70) | (i) order: **CD0 → CD1 → CD3 → CD4 → CD2** (the brief's order, with the v1 plant first) or another;<br>(ii) procedure: **sequential stop at the first screen PASS** / run all;<br>(iii) screen: **full** (BN* = BN-LD; computability on the runs pooled over three plateau levels; ρ_cal ∈ [0.82, 0.97]; Q_dis,cal ∈ [1,600, 2,300]; artefacts) / ρ + 0 collisions only (brief);<br>(iv) **remove V-1** from §9.2 and adopt the continuation rule of §9.2 after a check-seed FAIL;<br>(v) the CD3 and CD4 error terms vs v1 §9.2: **allow them only as pre-registered settings adopted through the C0′ screen** / drop CD3 and CD4;<br>(vi) counting: **(α) screens in k_cal, the adopted setting counts 1 in k** / (β) every screen counts in k (brief);<br>(vii) **procedure vs V70's text** (v2 review HC-3): **screen on calibration seeds 002–009 inside P0-C (as above)** / **V70 literal:** one setting chosen now from the literature, a priori, recorded in a dated addendum before seed 7,400,000, with no screen (if it then fails G1 on check seeds, §9.2 applies) | (ii) sequential saves up to 4 × 64 plateau runs and avoids extra looks.<br>(iii) the discharge band and BN* in the screen avoid adopting a setting that would fail G1(c) or move the jam away from the zones; the three-level pooling is needed because 8 runs at one level cannot hold ≥ 5 runs in each group (a defect of the rigor draft's screen, Appendix D).<br>(i) reasoned: CD1 is a single network attribute with a stated zipper mechanism [DEV]; CD3 is published but urban-calibrated; CD4 replaces EIDM and risks spontaneous jams away from the bottleneck; CD2 showed no drop in the pilot (2,310 veh/h/lane, almost no queue) and formed a queue in only 3 of 5 mechanics-toy runs; adopting CD2 also puts the lane-0 incident on the ending lane and re-opens A43 (ii).<br>(iv) the pilot found merges never active below λ = 1.0 (not data), and V71 placed no zone before merge 1.<br>(v) CD3's sigmaerror 0.1 is 2.5 × the source default of 0.04, and CD4's sigma 0.8 is above the default 0.5: both contradict v1 §9.2's "never used as a fix: driver error terms above the SUMO defaults" (§1.1 item 34). The C0′ artefact conditions and F3 are the H4 guard. Dropping them leaves only CD1 and CD2.<br>(vi) (β) can spend 5 of the 7 k slots before any check seed; with up to 3 likely ACC re-checks, k could exceed 7 and force a P0 KILL from bookkeeping alone (my arithmetic). (α) keeps the protection that matters (k limits looks at check seeds) and discloses every screen.<br>(vii) the screen chooses on our data (throw-away calibration seeds, pre-registered discrete candidates, principle 10, as v1 allowed and `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` §4b proposed) and inside P0, which V70's text does not foresee; V70 literal honours the text but must pick blind among candidates none of which is a published SUMO lane-drop demonstration, so it is more likely to adopt a setting with no drop or with artefacts. The screen estimator itself is [AUTHOR A63] | **(i), (ii), (iii) and (iv) as bold; (v) allow; (vi) (α); (vii) the screen**, recorded as a disclosed departure from V70's procedure (§1.1 item 39) |
| **A42** | Role of P0-G1's drop ratio (and T1a's ρ_T1) | (a) gated as in v1: ladder exhaustion → §9.2 (F7 if no setting passes the C0′ screen) → P0 KILL;<br>(b) **ρ report-only** in G1 and T1a, with the mandatory limitation sentence; G1(a′), (c), (d) stay gated; the ladder still adopts the first in-band setting;<br>(c) gated, but exhaustion falls back to (b) | **For (a):**<br>• CLAUDE.md Method lists "T1: a capacity drop exists";<br>• the drop and stop-and-go share a mechanism (lane-change voids, slow merge passage) that V76's lane commands act on, so a no-drop plant mutes lane-command benefits and harms;<br>• dropping the gate after the pilot predicted its FAIL looks like moving the goalposts;<br>• delay results on a no-drop plant are not throughput gains;<br>• V59 says never continue with a disclosed realism FAIL, if the drop is read as part of realism.<br>**For (b):**<br>• V74 moved the claim to S and T because the drop is absent, and a ρ gate re-ties the claim to the old mechanism;<br>• no published SUMO lane-drop demonstration exists, so the gate may test SUMO rather than the question;<br>• the properties S and T rest on stay gated (wave speed G2(b), onset location G2(a), discharge G1(c), artefacts, G6);<br>• a drop is still adopted whenever SUMO yields one;<br>• a KILL under (a) would say nothing about CAV smoothing. Cost of (b): a plant that (a) would stop at P0 (≈ 2–3 h) continues to P2 (≈ 65–76 h in total).<br>**(c):** a gate that cannot KILL is not a gate | **(b)**, disclosed as a deviation from CLAUDE.md T1 approved before data, with the V76 caveat in the limitation sentence, a V59 scope note in `decisions.md`, and **an explicit override of V69's clause "No PASS rule or bound may change because of it"**, because (b)'s case partly rests on the pilot's missing drop ([AUTHOR A66]; v2 review HC-2). (b) also makes the G1 onset split (a) report-only and is listed with every other relaxation in Appendix D. **(a)** if the author reads V59 as covering the drop, or declines the V69 override |
| **A43** | Incident specification (V75) | (i) model: **a dedicated stopped obstacle** / a permission closure / a converted stream vehicle / a `setSpeed(0)` breakdown;<br>(ii) lane: **lane 0 fixed** / lanes 0 and 2 by seed parity (analysed as two INC points) / lane 2 fixed; the middle lane excluded in every option;<br>(iii) **T_on 3,000 s, 900 s, x_I 13,000 m**; onset ±10 min and 30-min rows only in a later protocol;<br>(iv) levels: **I1 = λ_c − 0.10 (with the I1 rule), I2 = λ_c, I3 = λ_c + 0.10** / two levels (the first binding of λ_c − 0.10 / − 0.05 / λ_c, and λ_c + 0.10);<br>(v) **cohort_I** (route through x_I, desired departure + r_13 ∈ [T_on, T_on + 3,600) s) for S and T, with the guards also on the full cohort ([AUTHOR A55]) / a desired-departure window / the full cohort;<br>(vi) teleports: **V6 strict**, with the waiting-time margin of [AUTHOR A64] / an exemption behind the obstacle;<br>(vii) information: **a message from t_blk + 60 s to `lane`-spec arms** / at t_blk / none;<br>(viii) rubbernecking: **none and no contingency** / a contingency of HDV tau 1.3 s then 1.6 s within ±200 m of x_I, on calibration seeds;<br>(ix) **F band [0.30, 0.55]**;<br>(x) insertion: **Δ_ins from B16, integrity ±15 s, the rate gates of GI(d) and T0g, and the pre-registered fallback forced insertion [VERIFY]** | (i) §2.4b toy evidence.<br>(ii) parity makes every paired difference a 50/50 mixture of two sub-regimes, so the median sits between the clusters and its CI is unstable; done properly it needs two INC points (twice the cost). Lane 0 fixed is one regime, simpler to check and to state, but the claim covers a nearside-lane blockage only (DRL training still randomises the lane). Lane 2 had the latest block starts at high inflow in the toy.<br>(iv) two levels meet V75's "2–3" and save ≈ 40 GI runs; three give the incident-only, triggering and overlapping regimes.<br>(v) a desired-departure window includes X1/X2-bound vehicles and ignores the OR2 origin.<br>(vi) strict: P(admissible at n = 40) = 0.37 at a true teleport rate of 8 %; an exemption shortens trapped trips (a gaming route).<br>(vii) without a message LM-E cannot act, and the most natural lane use in an incident goes untested (a weak-baseline risk); at t_blk is unrealistically instant; 60 s is an ASSUMPTION, and any message is an extra V2X assumption.<br>(viii) the contingency fits a driver parameter towards a realism band, which principle 10 forbids; without it an out-of-band F drops the point.<br>(ix) built from Knoop 2009 (0.36) and HCM (0.49).<br>(x) the toy shows traffic-dependent block starts (−10.4 to +2.6 s on lane 0; up to +34.4 s on lane 2 at 5,100 veh/h); the fallback makes the start arm-independent at the price of an instant appearance, reported in an onset row | **the bold options** |
| **A44** | Incident role in P2 (principle 15 holds in options (a)–(c); (d) relaxes it) | (a) I1–I3 all tuned points in the band family (m ≤ 22);<br>(b) **the tuned INC point only (I2, or the nearest passing point)**, with I1 and I3 as held-out map rows (B0, B1, O(I2), F); m = 18;<br>(c) report-only (held-out map rows only);<br>(d) (v2 review HC-8) as (b), but with principle 15 relaxed: **(d-α) principle 15 as written (recommended)** / (d-β) an INC band point may yield a GO for an incident-only claim, with ℓ* undefined and the claim scope stated ("under a 15-min lane-0 blockage only") | (a) ≈ 75–88 h in total; (b) ≈ 65–76 h; (c) ≈ 59–70 h, and P2 then shows the incident with untuned rules only, so whether a tuned rule has authority there is unknown before the learning phase. In every option the INC points that passed P0-GI enter the D5.8 regime set at GO (V75). Under (d-α) none can produce the GO: conservative, and consistent with D5.8's and the CLAUDE.md early KILL's "at the realistic demand level", which v2 reads as a no-incident level, but a plant where control helps only under incidents is KILLed by a rule the drafters wrote. (d-β) admits that case; its cost is a claim without a designated realistic level and a weaker link to D5.8's "≥ 10 % vs no control at the realistic level" | **(b) with (d-α)** |
| **A45** | Lane execution (V76) | Mode: **1878, set at a CAV's first lane request in a zone** / 1878 at zone entry / 1622 / 1621.<br>keep: **no request** / an explicit hold.<br>Ban: **200 m by the `exec` mask** / 100 m / a network lane attribute.<br>Exit: **cancel + restore 1621, then the speed release** / a hold beyond the zone | 1878: no induced braking and no stranding in the toy, but 4–8 % execution at 6,000 veh/h and 33–43 % at 3,000 (rows run as 1877, and 1878 with a hold; 1878 with keep = no request not stress-tested). Setting it at the first request keeps speed-only zones bit-identical to the v1 `exec`, so keep-only twins equal the speed-only arms.<br>1622/1621: 70–83 % execution, neighbour hard braking up to 2.4 ×, 1 junction collision in 3 dense runs.<br>A hold suppressed LC2013's speed gain (15.1 vs 27.8 m/s) and stranded a CAV under strategic bits 1.<br>A network ban also binds HDVs: a plant change that P0 would have to include | **the bold options** |
| **A46** | Lane actuator FAIL (T0f) | (a) **lane commands removed from every arm** for P2 and the learning phases on this plant (V76 suspended there, disclosed); P1 continues;<br>(b) one switch to 1622 on re-round seeds (T0e and T0f re-run; (f2) protects neighbours), then (a);<br>(c) proceed with a non-binding lane actuator | (a) loses V76 on this plant but keeps the speed claim clean.<br>(b) ≈ 0.6 h plus a re-round; it adopts, after a look, a mode that moves braking onto neighbours.<br>(c) every arm carries an actuator that does nothing, and no lane statement is possible | **(a)** (changed from the rigor draft's (b), following the lean draft) |
| **A47** | Observation additions | (i) onboard side and rear sensing for CAVs: adjacent-lane front and rear gaps and relative speeds within 100 m, plus `couldChangeLane(±1)` bits (amends V36's sensor list);<br>(ii) per-lane mean CAV radar spacing and count in the Z1–Z3 and Z3+ messages (a probe density proxy);<br>(iii) the incident message (A43 (vii)).<br>Own-lane features need no decision: lane one-hot, a combining-lane flag, lanes available left/right, distance to the drop and to x_I, time since the own last lane change, a pending-request flag, the relative lane of the 5 V2V CAVs | (i) a commanded lane change is not credible without it (disclosed ASSUMPTION; no sensor standard read); `couldChangeLane` costs about 1.3 µs per call.<br>(ii) a few lines in `v2x`, the same for every `lane`-spec arm; without it LM-B balances on CAV counts, which become biased once a rule moves CAVs (endogeneity) | **(i) yes, (ii) yes** |
| **A48** | Comparator lane modules and tuning budget (fair DRL) | Modules: **LM-B (v_on, p) + LM-E (a, w), shared by R1–R4, tuned jointly with each rule** / published defaults, untuned / LM-B only (no message, no LM-E) / none.<br>Candidates: **n_cand = max(20, 5 · d)** per rule, d = parameters active at no-incident points / 20 (v1 A15).<br>**Keep-only twins at the held-out stage (200–239)** / none | Untuned or missing modules are a weak-baseline risk (CLAUDE.md). In P2 that risk is a false KILL; in D5.8 it is covered by A32 parity. No module breaks the same-actuator rule. LM-E's parameters ride on the same LHS without thinning the no-incident design. n_cand = 20 whenever d ≤ 4; each +5 candidates per rule costs ≈ 1,080 stage-1 episodes (≈ 5.4 h). The twins cost ≈ 720 episodes (≈ 3.6 h) and show the lane module's own contribution | **the bold options**; content in Addendum C before seed 060 |
| **A49** | Held-out n up to 80 | (a) **keep n ∈ {40, 60}**; (b) approve now the conditional use of 260–279 for n = 80 under the §6.7 rule | (b) adds ≈ 880 episodes (≈ 4.4 h) when triggered and shrinks the D5.8 re-tuning reserve to 280–299. At a noisy σ of 0.15, a true 12 % gain on one co-primary passes with probability 0.75 / 0.80 / 0.83 at n = 40 / 60 / 80 (normal approximation, m = 18; my arithmetic): the step from 60 to 80 buys about 0.03. The rigor draft's case (0.59 / 0.76 / 0.82) multiplied two events on the same estimate as if independent (Appendix D) | **(a)** |
| **A50** | CPU budget | accept ≈ 65–76 h in total (P2 ≈ 58–67 h; ≈ 43–101 h at 250–500 s per episode), re-checked in Addendum D with v1's 72-h P2 trigger kept / A44 (c) ≈ 59–70 h / drop the keep-only twins (−3.6 h) / cap n_cand at 20 | v1 was ≈ 51–59 h. v5.B.1 is the primary line and has CPU priority (CLAUDE.md). The upper end reaches the 72-h mark, so the question is asked now, before seed 000, rather than before P2. The 350-s planning value adds unloaded toy per-call costs to a loaded pilot time and may be 15–25 % low at the upper levels (§10.1; v2 review SF10) | **accept, conditional on Addendum D** (P0-S measures the full pipeline at 32 workers); the 72-h P2 trigger is kept |
| **A51** | Lane-change duration | **0 (instantaneous, the default)** / a non-zero plant-wide `--lanechange.duration` | Instant changes understate manoeuvre conflicts (T counts the cut-in from the next step). A non-zero value changes HDVs too and is untested here. highD durations average about 7.6–7.8 s ([PART] in `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md`) | **0**, disclosed; non-zero values only in a later protocol |
| **A52** | TTC details | Leader range **150 m** / 100 m; overlap records **excluded and reported**; zipper partners **counted** / excluded; at-speed filter **on the follower's speed** | 150 m: a TTC below 3.0 s needs g < 3.0 × Δv ≤ 3.0 × 41.7 = 125 m at the highest desired speed (1.5 × 27.78 m/s; my arithmetic); 100 m misses rare cases. Excluding zipper partners removes real merge conflicts at the main effect location; a zipper-zone-excluded row is reported. Overlapping "leaders" (g ≤ 0) are geometry artefacts at the zipper (SSM logged them as TTC = 0 "collisions" from long vehicles in the toy, with no SUMO collision) | **the bold options** |
| **A53** | Informativeness gate | (a) **G6 gated (S ≥ 1.5 × floor; T > 0 in ≥ 18/20 runs, ≥ 3 × floor and ≥ 30 conflict episodes per run), with the C9 pre-check and F6 on calibration, STOP on FAIL**;<br>(b) report-only;<br>(c) a non-zero test only (T > 0 in ≥ 7/8, the lean draft) | (a) stops before effort is spent on an unmeasurable claim; the factors and the episode count are ASSUMPTIONS from a toy.<br>(b) risks a whole P2 sweep on a structurally null metric, ending in a KILL that says nothing about control.<br>(c) lets a T dominated by one to three conflict pairs per run pass (rigor toy at 1.5 s: 0–1 pairs per run in a heavy queue), turning the band into a metric-noise KILL | **(a)** |
| **A54** | D5.8 "beats the best tuned rule in ≥ 1 regime" (binding for the learning phase, written now so that the P2 map cannot shape it) | (a) **on both co-primaries in the same regime**, Bonferroni over the R regimes, each component at α/(2R) (§12.2; the earlier draft's "at α_c per A39" was undefined outside the P2 family, v2 review ST2);<br>(b) on at least one co-primary, with the other not worse by > 5 %, with an extra α/2 over the co-primaries (the weaker reading);<br>(c) a co-primary pre-registered per regime before learner data | (a) strictest; consistent with principle 12 and with the band.<br>(b) easier to pass, but a learner could win on one co-primary only, which principle 12 excludes, so (b) would require amending principle 12; it is where V74's correction for two genuinely applies (a union).<br>(c) adds a pre-registration step that the P2 map could shape | **(a)**, with "never > 5 % worse" applied to each co-primary in every regime, its statistic and the regime granularity per [AUTHOR A57] (changed from the rigor draft's (b), which contradicted its own principle 12) |

### 11.3 v1 items re-opened by the new ones
*The earlier draft said "no new decision needed unless the author objects". The v2 design review (HC-4) found that several of these extensions are consequential, so they are now put to the author explicitly in **[AUTHOR A67]** (marked ▶ below); the rest are bookkeeping that follows from an item the author answers anyway.*
- **A5:** the ceiling is kept for C only (secondary, report-only); room rows for S and T are added (P0-R12, §6.4).
- ▶ **A6:** ℓ* is chosen among no-incident band levels (principle 15) ([AUTHOR A67] (iv)).
- ▶ **A7 / V61:** reading (a) is applied to each co-primary ((d_S), (d_T)), at 1 − α_c ([AUTHOR A67] (i)).
- **A14:** y = 10 % also applies to (f2).
- **A15:** superseded by A48 (n_cand = max(20, 5 · d), i.e. 20 whenever d ≤ 4); n₁ = 8 kept; the n₁ = 12 trade-off is restated in §10.3.
- **A17:** T1b is restated on S and T (16 tests); the consequence (proceed to P2) is unchanged.
- **A20:** P0-G5 adds S (J3, gated) and T (J4, reported with a FLAG).
- ▶ **A24:** gains these labelled ASSUMPTIONS (T0f's thresholds, whose FAIL removes V76's lane commands, are put to the author in [AUTHOR A67] (iii)): the S trend kernel (σ_g = 100 s, [AUTHOR A58]); the W1 end at 15,500 m; the 150-sample (30-s) imputation rule; the peak sub-cohort t_pk ± 30 min; the C9 and G6 factors 1.5 and 3, the ≥ 7/8 and ≥ 18/20 rules and the 30-episode count; the 10 % G5 FLAG for T; the 200-m ban; T0f's 20, 2 and 10 percentage points; the lane-change FLAG at 2 × B1; the HDV-share FLAG at 2 %; the B1 CAV-follower T FLAG at 1.5; the lane-2 share FLAG band 0.25–0.45; the F band [0.30, 0.55]; binding ≥ 16/20 and ≥ 7/8; the integrity tolerance ±15 s, the ≤ 1/20 and ≤ 1/30 out-of-tolerance rates and the 5-s paired limit; the 60-s message delay; the 50 km/h LM-B eligibility speed; trucks unchanged under CD3 and CD4; the 150-m leader range; the 10-m/s speed condition (B14); the C11 ±10 % FLAG; the 300-m incident attribution of braking events; the 25–50 s Python cost per run and the disk sizes. **Added by the v2 design review:** the 95th-percentile S imputation constant and the 2 % imputed-share FLAG; T's imputation at B0's per-km rate; the 1.0-s short-headway threshold and its 1.1 × FLAG; the T0f SSM FLAG (5 pp, 10 %); T0f(b)'s ≥ 10 congested minutes, ≥ 15 seeds and the 95 % saturation rule for (i); the C0′ Q_ff rule (≥ 3 no-onset runs, [AUTHOR A63]); the ≥ 20 B0 severe events of [AUTHOR A65]; the 240-s waiting-time screen of [AUTHOR A64]; the stage-1 noise allowance (1.645); the full-cohort +5 % row of [AUTHOR A55]; the per-point informativeness rule (≥ 90 %, ≥ 30 episodes, 1.5 × S_floor) of [AUTHOR A59]; the 0.5 wave-presence share of [AUTHOR A60]; the 0.29 point-SD stop of [AUTHOR A68]; B ≥ 200,000.
- ▶ **A27:** the point form also applies to (f2), a new safety guard ([AUTHOR A67] (ii)).
- **A28:** h_all uses (α), onset speed ≥ 10 m/s; the G3 definitions and h's (α′) are unchanged.
- **A29:** superseded for at-speed events under A38 (a); h_HDV stays reported.
- **A32:** tuning parity extends to the lane-module parameters and to the incident regime: P2's tuning at one INC point never serves as the D5.8 comparator.

### 11.4 New items A55–A68 (v2 design review, 2026-10-14; open)
*Each comes from verified findings of the v2 design review (Appendix E). All are due before seed 7,400,000; none needs data. The primary design above uses each recommendation.*

| Id | Decision | Options | Cost / consequence | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A55** | Guards at INC points: cohort_I and the full cohort (MV-1, BDP2-5) | (a) **S and T on cohort_I; the guards (e), (f), (f′), (f2), (g) on cohort_I and on the full cohort, both must pass; criterion (j): full-cohort S and T of A vs B0 not worse by > 5 % (point form); the same in D5.8 INC regimes, vs the comparator; the X1/X2-bound and late-arrival subgroups reported**;<br>(b) everything on cohort_I, the full cohort reported only (the earlier draft);<br>(c) the full cohort for S and T too | (a) re-scoring only, no new runs; one more intersection component at the INC point, so a slightly lower INC pass rate. It closes the route of moving harm onto vehicles that never pass x_I or that reach 13 km after T_on + 3,600 s, in the regime most likely to carry a learner's conditional advantage.<br>(b) leaves that route open (the review's failure scenario is a construction, not a run; ±5 km/h steps, the 50 km/h floor and principle 15 limit it).<br>(c) dilutes the incident effect with vehicles the incident never touched | **(a)** |
| **A56** | Claim wording and the meaning of "safer" (MV-4, HC-7, BDP2-4; MV-2's gates) | (a) **the claim names its measures** (§6.8 wording rule): "≥ 10 % less time exposed to TTC < TTC\* s at ≥ 36 km/h (a surrogate safety measure from microsimulation, not a crash rate) and ≥ 10 % lower within-vehicle speed fluctuation"; beside it the TTC\* in force, the C row, the energy row, the h_all ratios and the §0.5 disclosure; "safer" only if the h_all point ratios vs B0 and vs B1 are ≤ 1.00 and the severe FLAG has not fired; the severe row and the all-speed-outside-the-zipper row as FLAGs; the CLAUDE.md Metrics bullet amended (companion edit);<br>(b) as (a), plus the two FLAG rows as gated non-inferiority guards: (g3) TET at 1.5 s at speed (when the fork has fired) and (g4) all-speed TET outside the zipper zone, each continuity-corrected ≤ 1 + y vs B0 and vs B1;<br>(c) the earlier rule ("≥ 10 % smoother and safer than no control" with the B1 decomposition and the delay row);<br>(d) hard braking inside the claim (A38 (c), (d)) | (a) no power cost; the claim says exactly what was measured, as CLAUDE.md's "no proxy headline metrics" requires; a band point may end up with "less TTC exposure" but without the word "safer".<br>(b) closes the trade of severe for milder exposure by a gate, but (g3) rests on a near-zero base rate (rigor toy at 1.5 s: 0.003 s per 1,000 veh-km in a heavy queue), so it would be noise-dominated, and (g4) re-opens B14's at-speed decision (queue and zipper creep); the pass-rate cost is unquantified.<br>(c) lets a surrogate become "safer" while at-speed hard braking may be 10 % worse and V74's own 1.5-s measure is unguarded.<br>(d) see A38 | **(a)**; (b) only if the author wants V74's 1.5-s measure gated after the fork |
| **A57** | D5.8 harm leg, regimes and test levels (ST1, BDP2-7, ST2) | **Harm leg** "not significantly worse by > 5 % in any pre-registered regime", per regime and co-primary, the learner vs that regime's best re-tuned rule:<br>(a) **point form: FAIL if the median relative difference > +0.05** (as A27), the CIs reported;<br>(b) literal: FAIL only if the CI lower bound > +0.05 (harm must be shown);<br>(c) non-inferiority: PASS only if the CI upper bound < +0.05;<br>(d) the archive's worse_gt5: FAIL if the point > +5 % and the CI excludes 0 (`port_map_v0.md`:778, "one reading … pre-register it explicitly").<br>**Regimes:** (α) **each maximal run of consecutive no-incident band levels is one regime, each INC point is one regime** / (β) every band level and INC point is a regime.<br>**Levels, fixed now:** the ℓ* test at α/2 per co-primary under A39 (a) (α under (b)); "beats the best rule" a union over the R regimes at α/(2R) per component; the D5.8 conjuncts an intersection-union test, with no correction across them | At σ = 0.085, n = 40 (normal planning approximation; my arithmetic, matching the review's): (a) an equal learner passes one component with 0.9985 and 22 components with 0.968; a learner truly 8 % worse passes one with 0.037.<br>(b) a learner truly 8 % worse still passes with 0.57 (σ 0.085) or 0.83 (σ 0.15): absence of evidence counts as a pass.<br>(c) an equal learner passes 22 components with about 0.02, so the confirmatory n would have to be several hundred (the review's estimate: n ≈ 300 at σ = 0.15).<br>(d) lies between (a) and (b).<br>(α) keeps R at about 1–4 (z per component 2.24–2.73) and 2R components; (β) gives up to 12 regimes and 24 components (z = 3.08), and splits one contiguous operating region into many tests. The confirmatory n is sized for 2R components in the learning-phase protocol | **(a) with (α)**; the literal D5.8 test (b) is reported beside it |
| **A58** | S trend kernel and a period-neutral co-confirmation (BDP2-1) | (a) the 300-s boxcar (earlier draft) + the co-confirmation (b_S′) + the Lagrangian period row;<br>(b) **a centred Gaussian trend, σ_g = 100 s (half-power period ≈ 401 s, the boxcar's ≈ 397 s) + (b_S′): S_raw r ≤ 0 vs B0 (point form) + the Lagrangian period row**;<br>(c) the boxcar alone, band membership labelled "filter-dependent" under the other filters (earlier draft);<br>(d) as (b) with a stronger co-confirmation, S_raw r ≤ −0.05 | (a) keeps the toy-checked numbers but a response that is not monotone in the period (variance gain 1.47 at 200 s, 1.00 at 300 s, 0.66 at 360 s; at equal amplitude a period change from 150 to 200 s raises S by 21 %).<br>(b) a monotone response; on a copy of the rigor toy it moved S like the boxcar (70 km/h stand-in −7.8 to −10.6 % vs −8.2 to −11.1 %; crawl −30 to −32 % vs −31 to −33 %; heavy queue 5.2 × free flow vs 5.3 ×), so G6's factors stand. It still depends on the period (0.79 at 300 s, 0.18 at 600 s), which (b_S′) covers, because S_raw does not depend on the period at equal amplitude. (b_S′) costs little: the toy's 70 km/h stand-in cut S_raw by 5.6–7.5 %.<br>(c) discloses but gates nothing.<br>(d) may fail genuine dampers whose effect leaves the queue contrast in S_raw unchanged | **(b)** |
| **A59** | Per-point informativeness (BDP2-2) | (a) **criterion (a′) at every tuned and INC point on the held-out B0 runs (T > 0 in ≥ 90 % of runs, a median of ≥ 30 conflict episodes, median S ≥ 1.5 × S_floor); a failing point is "co-primary-uninformative": in the map, out of the band and out of the D5.8 regime set**;<br>(b) **the C9 fork also evaluated at the highest C1 ladder level ≤ 1.0 with no invalid B0 run** (still one-way);<br>(c) neither: G6 at λ_c + 0.1 only (earlier draft) | (a) no runs; it may remove low band levels (λ_c − 0.2 … λ_c), where S and T sit near their floors, and INC points scored on cohort_I (about 1 h of vehicles); it stops a claim from resting on a T built from a few episodes.<br>(b) makes the fork fire more readily (every toy predicts that it fires in heavy queues) and aligns TTC\* with the levels where ℓ* is likely.<br>(c) certifies λ_c + 0.1 while claims are made at other levels | **(a) and (b)** |
| **A60** | Wave presence and the "wave damping" label (BDP2-3) | (a) a gated G6(d): at λ_c + 0.1, ≥ 50 % of B0 runs with ≥ 1 stop-and-go cycle per 10 veh-km or a valid wave estimate; FAIL → STOP;<br>(b) **wave presence reported per level and per band point; the claim text carries "smoothing without stop-and-go waves" wherever B0's wave-presence share is < 0.5; G2(b)'s "≥ 5 valid estimates" written out as pooled over the 40 runs at λ_c and λ_2, with per-level counts reported**;<br>(c) nothing | (a) matches V74's "(wave damping)" strictly, but adds a STOP that would end a still-valid smoothness claim; the pilot (not data) saw waves from λ ≈ 0.85, near λ_c + 0.1, so it would probably pass.<br>(b) prevents mislabelling at no cost and without a new KILL path.<br>(c) G6(a) can pass on queue entry and exit transitions alone (B11(v): a single step leaves an S residual) | **(b)** |
| **A61** | The INC point and the held-out n (BDP2-8) | (a) the INC point in m and in σ_plan,2 (earlier draft);<br>(b) out of both: m = 16; the INC band rows at α_c for comparability, with their achieved half-width;<br>(c) **in m (conservative: z_c = 3.197 instead of 3.163), out of σ_plan,2; its SD and achieved half-width reported** | No decision rests on the INC point's band membership (it never yields a GO, and the INC points enter the regime set regardless), but its extra noise (block-start spread, +∞ encodings) could push n_eval to 60 for every point: about 880 more episodes (≈ 4.4 h; the same count as A49's 20-seed step; my arithmetic).<br>(b) also drops the INC rows from the family-wise error statement, although "the incident regime alone meets the bar" is reported prominently.<br>(c) keeps the error control over the INC row and removes the cost | **(c)** |
| **A62** | The zipper output lane below the floor (SF3) | (a) gate per lane at BN-LD in the C0′ screen and in G1(c): the zipper output lane ≥ the floor, and a drop must not come only from a lane already below it;<br>(b) **keep the section mean (the field anchors are section-level); when the P0-R9 FLAG fires, the limitation sentence discloses the sub-floor zipper lane; ρ and Q_dis per lane always reported**;<br>(c) report-only, no disclosure in claims (earlier draft) | (a) non-standard (every discharge anchor of §4.4 is a section value) and expected to FAIL on the toy evidence (zipper-lane discharge 1,036–1,461 veh/h under every visibility setting; a toy, not F3Z): a likely P0 KILL from a SUMO zipper property.<br>(b) keeps the realism check on the anchored quantity and stops the section mean from hiding a sub-floor lane in claims.<br>(c) the FLAG would be seen only in the P0 report | **(b)** |
| **A63** | The C0′ screen's Q_ff (SF4) | (a) Q_ff pooled over the three levels (earlier draft);<br>(b) **Q_ff from the highest of the three levels with ≥ 3 no-onset runs; Q_dis pooled; the pooled ρ and the within-run plateau ratio reported**;<br>(c) the within-run plateau estimator (pre-onset plateau flow vs Q_dis in the same run) as the screen statistic | (a) biases ρ_cal towards 1: no-onset runs come mostly from the lowest level, whose inflow is 6.7 % below the next at λ_a = 0.75 (my arithmetic), so a setting with a real drop of a few percent can fail the 0.97 edge and push the ladder towards CD3/CD4 (driver error above the defaults) or to "none passes".<br>(b) keeps G1's between-run estimator class and removes the pull from the lowest level; a median over 3 runs is noisy (a screen only; G1 re-checks on 30 fresh seeds).<br>(c) same-level by construction, but a different estimator from G1, and runs that break down during the rise have no pre-onset period | **(b)** |
| **A64** | Incident teleport margin (SF5) | (a) a diagnostic only: the maximum waiting time behind the obstacle per run and the count above 200 s; per arm, the share of INC runs invalidated by teleports and its paired difference;<br>(b) **(a) plus a C10 screen condition: ≤ 1 of 8 runs with a maximum wait > 240 s**;<br>(c) nothing (earlier draft) | (b) drops knife-edge points before any check seed, at the price of possibly losing I3 (V75 asks for 2–3 levels; I1 and I2 lie in the toy's range with waits of 83–156 s). Without it a point near the 300-s timer can pass C10 (≈ 0.87 at an 8 % teleport rate) and then fail GI (≈ 0.48; my arithmetic), and an arm's effect on lane-1 gaps can turn the timer into that arm's failure (V6 strict, A43 (vi)) | **(b)**; A43 (vi) states that I3 may be lost to the timer |
| **A65** | The CD setting and G3's yardstick (SF7) | (a) report the B0 severe-event rate of every screened CD setting, and state in A41 that CD3/CD4 change G3's reference;<br>(b) **(a) plus a C0′ screen condition: ≥ 20 at-speed severe events of the CAV-flagged vehicles (driven as HDVs), pooled over the setting's plateau runs at λ_a and λ_a + 0.05**;<br>(c) (a) plus a computability floor at G3 time, below which G3(b) is not computable and the author decides | Under CD4 (Krauss) severe events in B0 should come mainly from emergencies (an inference from the model structure, [VERIFY]), so R's denominator can be tiny, R ill-conditioned, and F4, a P0 KILL path, could fire for a reason unrelated to ACC realism; CD3 pushes the other way and makes G3 easier.<br>(b) settles it on calibration seeds before any check seed, at the price of possibly skipping CD4.<br>(c) asks the author after calibration data, and "not computable" is otherwise a FAIL (principle 7) | **(b)** |
| **A66** | Pilot-informed changes and V69 (HC-2) | (a) **every pilot-informed change listed (§1, V67/V69/V73 row); choosing A42 (b) requires an explicit override of V69's "No PASS rule or bound may change because of it", recorded in `decisions.md`; V-1's removal and the CD order stay as design choices, their pilot reasons disclosed**;<br>(b) no override: A42 (b) is argued without the pilot (V74's reasoning and the absence of a published SUMO lane-drop demonstration) and the pilot reasons are struck;<br>(c) A42 (a): no PASS-rule change | (a) complete; the override is a visible author act.<br>(b) a weaker case for A42 (b), but it stands on V74 and `capacity_drop_lit_v0.md` §4a.<br>(c) keeps V69 intact, at A42 (a)'s cost (a P0 KILL is likely if no CD setting yields a drop) | **(a)** |
| **A67** | Extensions of answered items, made explicit (HC-4; replaces §11.3's "unless the author objects") | (i) V61's "≥ 5 % vs B1" applies to **both S and T** / S only / T only / C only (V61's text names the generalised cost), at **1 − α_c** / at 95 %;<br>(ii) the burden of proof for (f2): **point form (as A27 (c))** / non-inferiority at α/m;<br>(iii) T0f's thresholds (+20 pp per zone at λ_sub; +10 pp for CAVs and +2 pp for all vehicles on congested minutes at ℓ₂, the latter not applicable under saturation): **as proposed** / other values;<br>(iv) ℓ*'s scope: **no-incident band levels only** / any band point | (i) both is the strictest reading; V61 was written for C, so extending it is a reading the author should own; 95 % would loosen (d) and decouple it from the band family.<br>(ii) the point form keeps (f2) consistent with (e) and (f); non-inferiority at α/m needs about 1,920 events per arm (§8.7).<br>(iii) a T0f FAIL removes V76's lane commands from every arm (A46), so V76's survival on this plant depends on these numbers.<br>(iv) follows from principle 15 (A44 (d)) | **the bold options** |
| **A68** | Resolution-futility stop after P1 (ST3) | (a) none (earlier draft: KILL-2, at ≈ 52–59 h, is the realistic early stop when t ≤ 0);<br>(b) **STOP before seed 100 if, for S or for T, the P1 point SD σ̂ exceeds 0.29, i.e. even a true 15 % gain on that co-primary would enter with probability < 0.5 at n = 60 (A39 (a), m = 18)**;<br>(c) a stricter threshold, e.g. σ̂ > 0.193, where the CI first binds beyond the point criterion at n = 60 | The P1 SDs come from untuned R4 and the lane samples vs B0, a proxy for tuned arms.<br>(b) fires only when resolution is hopeless (at σ = 0.29 a true 12 % gain enters with 0.26 per co-primary); it saves ≈ 45–50 h when it fires and is rarely a false stop.<br>(c) would stop plants on which a true 15 % gain still enters with 0.95 (σ = 0.193): too aggressive.<br>(a) spends ≈ 45–50 h to reach the same conclusion (my arithmetic, `review_fix/desk_review.py`) | **(b)**; the predicted P(enter) is printed in the P1 report in every case |

## 12 What this protocol does not cover, and what it binds for later

### 12.1 Not covered
- Learning arms L1 and L2: the PettingZoo environment, the reward (open, V52; under V74 it should target S, T and the guards, decided in the learning-phase protocol), the trainer ladder (ADR-001 §4; its steps that use no F3Z and no project seed may proceed in parallel), the training budget and validation-only checkpoint choice. All after a GO.
- The D5.8 evaluation itself: the best tuned rule chosen on validation seeds (300–499), the test seeds (500–699), the confirmatory run (700–999), and the pre-registration of the adaptation gain and robustness rows (taken from the P2 map, evaluated on fresh seeds). P2 only fixes the band, the regime set and the designated level ℓ*.
- The VSL comparators I1/I2 (V32, V39, V55) and their compliance values.
- Regime axes other than the inflow level and the incident: CAV share, ramp-to-mainline ratio, per-episode inflow noise, communication delay and loss, adaptive drivers, weather.
- Incident variants: a blocked middle lane or lane 2, two blocked lanes, durations other than 900 s, onsets other than 3,000 s, positions other than 13,000 m, and detection delays other than 60 s (possible report-only robustness rows in the learning-phase protocol); rubbernecking (no layer; an opposite carriageway is not modelled).
- Non-zero lane-change durations and SUMO's sublane model ([AUTHOR A51]); SSM-based conflict types (merging, crossing) as claim metrics; per-lane HDV advisories (V15).
- Capacity-drop settings beyond CD0–CD4 and V-2 ([AUTHOR A41], [AUTHOR A42]).
- Whether the 1.6-fold time compression of the peak ([AUTHOR A12]) changes the waves S measures.
- Native-rate comparator rows ([AUTHOR A22]). If the band is empty, whether the 2-s quantised interface rather than the plant limited the rules (literature_v0.md verifier weakness 4) can only be examined in a new protocol on fresh seeds; it never changes the P2 verdict.
- A second plant variant for band robustness (archive D4.7, ARCH day_pattern_decisions_v0.md:59).
- Ramp meters (dropped by V15) and MPC (dropped and disclosed).
- Cross-machine determinism.
- Charging losses (V53), cold start, HVAC and auxiliary loads; a MOVES or VECTO cross-check; the validity of the HBEFA4 acceleration term beyond the G4 floor ([AUTHOR A33]).
- Validation against Irish data: no measured capacity drop, wave speed or queue discharge was found for any Irish motorway (TII open per-vehicle files returned 403 for every date tested from 2021-06 to 2026-10; irish_parameters_v0.md §4). The realism bands are international, apart from the 2012 M50 breakdown flows; Irish-specific realism is [VERIFY]. No Irish lane-blocking incident duration or share was found either (evidence: incident).
- A claim on C: under V74, C may be claimed later only through a new pre-registered analysis on fresh seeds ("only if the framework proves itself").

### 12.2 Binding commitments for the learning-phase protocol (fixed now, so that they cannot be chosen after P2 results)
- **Claim metrics.** D5.8 uses S and T exactly as defined in §3.1, with the TTC\* in force after C9, the trend kernel of [AUTHOR A58] with the co-confirmation (b_S′), the windows and cohorts of §2.4b and §3.1 (at INC points the guards also on the full cohort, with criterion (j), [AUTHOR A55]), and the effect convention of §8.1. C is reported as secondary and is never promoted to a claim metric after P2 results without a new protocol. **Every claim follows the §6.8 wording rule** ([AUTHOR A56]), including the short §0.5 disclosure of why and when the headline changed.
- **"≥ 10 % vs no control at the realistic level"** is tested at ℓ* only (a no-incident level, [AUTHOR A6]), on **both** S and T, each with its CI excluding 0 at **α/2 per co-primary under A39 (a) (z = 2.241), or α under (b) (z = 1.960)**, and **≥ 5 % vs B1 on both** (V61 reading (a)) at the same level (v2 review ST2: A39's α_c = α/(2m) belongs to the P2 band family and is undefined for one pre-designated test).
- **Regimes.** The frozen regime set is the no-incident band levels plus the INC points that passed P0-GI (V75), minus any point that fails (a′) ([AUTHOR A59]); it is grouped into R regimes by [AUTHOR A57] (α): each maximal run of consecutive no-incident band levels is one regime (its statistic computed on the paired differences pooled over its (seed, level) pairs), and each INC point is one regime. **"Beats the best tuned rule in ≥ 1 pre-registered regime"** follows [AUTHOR A54] (a): both co-primaries within the same regime, a union over the R regimes with each component at **α/(2R)** (R fixed at GO; z = 2.241 / 2.498 / 2.638 / 2.734 at R = 1 / 2 / 3 / 4). **"Never > 5 % worse"** follows [AUTHOR A57] (a): in every regime and for each co-primary, the learner FAILs if median_s(Y_L − Y_rule) / median_s(Y_rule) > +0.05 against that regime's best re-tuned rule (point form; the literal D5.8 test, "CI lower bound > +0.05", is reported beside it); an intersection, no correction. **The D5.8 conjuncts** (beats B0 at ℓ*, beats B1, beats the best rule in ≥ 1 regime, never worse in any regime) form an intersection-union test, so no correction is applied across them (§8.3). The guards (e)–(h), (f2) included, apply in every regime, and at INC regimes on cohort_I and on the full cohort ([AUTHOR A55]). The confirmatory n is sized for 2R components.
- **Tuning parity ([AUTHOR A32], extended).** P2's tuning serves the band and the KILL decisions only. Before any learner comparison under D5.8, R1–R4 **with their lane modules** are re-tuned on the frozen regime set, **the INC points included**, on tuning seeds not used in P2 (260–299, or a newly approved range), with a budget at least equal to the learners' total tuning budget counted in simulated episodes (A32 (a)), and with an LHS of at least max(20, 10 × d) points per rule (d from Addendum C, lane-module parameters included). P2's F and O(s) are never used as "the best tuned proven controller" without this re-tuning.
- **Fair DRL and lane parity (V76).** Every learning arm acts on `Discrete(9)` through the same `exec` (mode rule, mask, ban, zone exit) and the same action mask. Every learning arm that issues lane commands observes at least the `lane` spec fields that the R lane modules read (incident message included); no comparator may observe a field that the candidate cannot. If [AUTHOR A46] removed lane commands after T0f, they stay removed for every arm on this plant. The T0f SSM check and the short-headway FLAG (§3.1, §5) are repeated for every lane-commanding learning arm against its keep-only twin, and printed beside its claims (v2 review MV-3).
- **Effect sizes.** Seeds 200–259 decide band membership only. Every effect size quoted for a band point or regime, the INC points included, is re-estimated on fresh seeds (300+).
- **Adaptive scheduler.** Whether a non-learning adaptive scheduler enters "best non-learning" for the adaptation gain is decided before any learner data ([AUTHOR A31]; V63 dropped it from P2).
- **Zone moves.** After a zone move, the band and the regime set are re-fixed by the unchanged band rule in a dated addendum before any validation-seed evaluation of a learning arm ([AUTHOR A34]).
- **The limitation sentence** of §4.4 travels with every claim made on a plant without a capacity drop in the field band ([AUTHOR A42] (b)), with the zipper-lane clause when the P0-R9 FLAG fired ([AUTHOR A62]).

## Appendix B: Synthesis notes

*v2 note (2026-10-13): v0 history, kept as written. Statements in it about C as the headline, BN-M2 as the expected first bottleneck, or v0/V64 positions describe v0 and v1; the v2 synthesis notes are Appendix D.*

*Kept as written for the synthesis, before the review. Where it conflicts with the body or with Appendix C (for example on the guard test level, the release recommendation, the ACC selection rule, the wall-time cap or the [AUTHOR] deadlines), the body and Appendix C govern.*

### B.1 Base draft and scores
Totals over the three judges (sum of six criteria each): **draft_kill 143**, draft_rigor 142, draft_feasible 120 (my arithmetic). Judges 1 and 2 named rigor the winner (judge 1 called the totals a tie); judge 3 named kill. Following the rule "start from the highest total", **kill is the base**: its skeleton (KILL-first staging with a B0-only first gate, calibration futility stops, the plateau probe, the P2 selection and held-out confirmation of both O and F, the plant-variant ladder) is kept. Rigor supplies most of the measurement, statistics and health machinery; feasible supplies the implementation-acceptance tests, the operational T3 cases and the Irish breakdown threshold.

### B.2 Ideas taken, by source draft
- **From draft_kill:** the design rule "most likely failure first" and the B0-only P0-G1 before `exec`, V2X or any arm is built; the calibration futility stops F1–F3 (extended here by F4 for the ACC ladder); the plateau probe as the instrument for the between-run drop estimator; the expected outcome written before data; one independent RNG stream per route keyed by (seed, route); r indexed by (route, car/truck) and shared by every arm; the level grid anchored on λ_c with λ = 1.0 always in the map; one candidate set reused at every level, making the pooled optimum and T2 free; O and F both selected on the same tuning seeds and both confirmed on held-out seeds 200+; the delay guard at the same Bonferroni level as the effect test; the pooled rate ratio for rare events; the headroom ceiling (kept as report-only); stage-1 futility; the fixed plant-variant ladder V-1 / V-2 with "realism FAIL disclosed" ruled out; "no band level is called realistic unless λ = 1.0"; the SUMO option table; the CPU anchor and the KILL-path savings; the archive wave estimator (10-s series, high-pass, ≥ 15-min segment, correlation ≥ 0.3, interior lag).
- **From draft_rigor:** the intersection-union principle and the named Bonferroni families; the conflicts-and-gaps list (§1.1); measurement sections just downstream of each bottleneck with an active-bottleneck test; the computability probabilities for G1 and the pre-registered extension to n = 40; the wave-speed resolution argument; T0b directional at a sub-critical level, T0c two-sided at the bottleneck, T0d sensing integrity; T1a on fresh seeds with a discharge band; the held-out n rule from a planning σ; the relative-CI bootstrap that resamples numerator and denominator; the invalid-run encoding (+∞ for the arm, 0 when only the reference failed); the gameability table and the five-test metric screen; the health layer with a detector for every known artefact and the golden B0 hash; the step-size check; the W13 asymmetry; Addenda A/B/C; VoT frozen before the first check seed; the grid-edge extension; selection by the paired statistic; KILL-2 (in-sample futility after stage 2); the sensitivity rows by re-scoring; the read-status appendix.
- **From draft_feasible:** acceptance tests that prove the code can FAIL (negative controls, including the once-only `slowDown` that must FAIL the hold test); the module-by-stage mapping to `port_map_v0.md`; T3 with an in-process close/start and a run SIGSTOPped and resumed by the CPU guard; a resumable ledger with health columns filled by code; σ for the held-out n taken from the stage-2 SDs before the held-out stage; the TII 2012 60 km/h / 5-min breakdown threshold and the 60/70 km/h hysteresis; the TII breakdown flows as Irish context (report-only here); the post-hoc disclosure of the energy bands; renaming the on-ramps OR1/OR2; the truck-length question; the λ = 1.0 ≈ 2,400 veh/h/lane arithmetic; the R2 leader-acceleration finite difference and the R1 dx_s [VERIFY]; R1/R4 may start stage 1 early without any decision being taken early; the "critical path is engineering" view; the 72-h projection checkpoint.
- **New in the synthesis** (in none of the drafts): the stage-1 futility threshold t that adapts to σ_plan,1, with a stated ≤ 1 % false-screen probability; the censoring and origin-queue guards in the band rule instead of excluding drain- or boundary-limited levels; the pre-registered order rule for the two plant variants (merge-type BN* → V-1 first, otherwise V-2 first); q₁ = 0.70 and t_pk = 5,160 s from mapping the fitted US-169 curve onto the window; dropping the archive onset-share gate from G1 with the binomial reasoning; G3 on the pooled rate ratio; plant-invalid levels above λ_c + 0.1 instead of a plant FAIL when artefacts appear only far over capacity; the ACC ladder A0–A3 with CACC and tau 1.5 s excluded on archive and toy evidence; P0 seeds rearranged so that P1 has its own fresh block; an exhausted ACC ladder routed to [AUTHOR A11] (KILL or a new CAV model) instead of to the HDV plant-variant ladder, which cannot fix a CAV model; the deadline column in §11.

## Appendix C: Review log

The synthesis (`protocol_v0_synth.md`) was reviewed through five lenses: statistics (S), honesty (GH), physics (PHYS), consistency with the binding inputs (C) and a bidirectional pass (BDP: down from the thesis claim, up from each threshold). Each finding was then verified against the text and, where possible, recomputed. "Severity" shows the lens's rating → the rating after verification. Findings that the verifier marked as needing the author became [AUTHOR] items with options, costs and a recommendation (§11) instead of silent choices.

| Finding | Lens | Severity | Verdict | What changed (or why not) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | stats | blocker → blocker | confirmed | P1 levels ℓ₁–ℓ₃ redefined as grid points, ℓ₃ replaced by λ_c + 0.05 when plant-invalid (§5.0); T1a gates artefacts with zero tolerance only at ℓ₁, ℓ₂ and applies the admissibility rule at ℓ₃; σ_plan,1 and the KILL-1 calibration use admissible levels only; the §6.2 replacement rule maps P1 levels to their nearest grid point; one admissibility rule (B0 invalid share ≤ 5 %) for G2(c) above λ_c + 0.1, T1a(ii) and band (a) (§8.8); T0c falls back to ℓ₂ |
| S2 | stats | major → minor | plausible | n_eval also uses a bootstrap of the whole band rule on the 38 selection seeds at n = 40 and 60, for O(ℓ) and F (§6.7); the guards' test level went to the author ([AUTHOR A27]); the 20-event fallback of (f) was replaced by a continuity-corrected ratio with no switch (§6.8, §8.5); a reference-only failure is encoded as the arm's worst case in the guards, with the 0 encoding as a sensitivity row (§8.4) |
| S3 | stats | major → minor | plausible | Selection eligibility is re-evaluated on all 38 seeds with the band's own criteria (d)–(h) and tolerances (§6.6); F's pooled objective gives no credit at levels where it is ineligible; stage-1 advancement allows ≤ 1 invalid run of 8 (false screen-out ≈ 0.01 instead of 0.15 at a 2 % invalid rate). Not changed: the selection objective stays the lowest J (the CLAUDE.md lookup definition); the predicted pass rate is reported, not used to select |
| S4 | stats | major → major | confirmed | P0-G5 is paired by seed on the shared manifests, with the paired median relative difference and its CI; J2 on seeds with onsets in both runs (≥ 5); the test form (point estimate vs TOST) went to the author in [AUTHOR A20] (recommended: point estimate, consistent with §8.6) (§4.8) |
| S5 | stats | major → minor | plausible | The error claim is restated (FWER ≤ 0.05 under the null of no gain; 10 % is a point criterion), with an operating-characteristic table; seeds 200–259 decide membership only, and effect sizes are re-estimated on 300+ (§6.8, §12.2); [AUTHOR A6] option (c), a designated level fixed by a rule written now, added and recommended |
| S6 | stats | major → minor | plausible | G1(d) is evaluated on the original 30 runs only, so the extension cannot add FAIL risk (§4.4); the code-integrity vs traffic-artefact split is offered in [AUTHOR A24] (recommended: no split). Not changed: zero tolerance near critical, which the brief requires; §8.8 now states the two roles explicitly |
| S7 | stats | minor → minor | plausible | The KILL-1 threshold t is calibrated empirically by resampling the P1 paired samples shifted to a 10 % gain; the normal formula is reported alongside; coverage limited to the P1 levels is stated (§6.5) |
| S8 | stats | minor → minor | confirmed | C5 runs on all 8 calibration seeds, with ≥ 20 vehicles per cell, a pooled-pace fallback, a sanity FLAG, and the fallback cells recorded (§2.6, §3.1) |
| S9 | stats | minor → minor | confirmed | Order fixed: the edge check runs after stage 1 and before KILL-1; nothing is added after stage 2; F's pooled objective covers an added level (§6.10, §0.2) |
| S10 | stats | minor → minor | plausible | C4 selects at R ≤ 0.85, keeps candidates up to 1.25 for G3, and runs at the G3 levels (λ_c, λ_c + 0.1); the margin trade-off (ladder exhaustion) is shown in [AUTHOR A11]; the reported R is labelled conditional on selection (§2.6, §4.6) |
| GH-1 | honesty | blocker → blocker | confirmed | Every [AUTHOR] item is due before seed 7,400,000 (A9, A30 before 060); Addendum B (VoT, prices, constants) is committed before seed 000; the calibration report shows design quantities and futility outcomes only, and ρ, Q_dis, C and R values are not tabulated until the P0 report (§4.3, §9.4, §11). A18 records that only the VoT uplift is open |
| GH-2 | honesty | blocker → blocker | confirmed | A5, A6, A7, A14, A15, A21, A22 and A23 are due before seed 000; principle 1 now says P0/P1 results can only feed a tightening addendum; Addendum C moved before seed 060 (§0.3, §6.3, §11) |
| GH-3 | honesty | major → major | confirmed | Guard (g) uses pooled totals, an origin-queue factor of 1.02 and sign counts (§6.8); imputation rows for censored trips and origin-queue idle energy (§6.12); TTT counted from the desired departure (§3.1); the CLAUDE.md co-headline vs V29 recorded in §1.1 item 15; served throughput with its CI in the band table |
| GH-4 | honesty | major → major | plausible | A binding parity clause for the D5.8 comparator (§12.2, [AUTHOR A32]); Addendum C states each rule's dimension; a parameter-adequacy refinement inside P2 (§6.10) |

**Note (v1, 2026-10-13).** The log above describes v0 and is kept as written. Under the V64 layout some of its statements no longer hold: PHYS-01 and BDP-3 ("BN-M2 binds first on paper"): BN-LD now binds first (§2.1); PHYS-05 (per-lane discharge and metronome at BN-LD): that bottleneck is now the expected primary one; C8 ([AUTHOR A30] on BN-M2 information): A30 now concerns a unit downstream of BN-LD; C6 and PHYS-07 (the Z2 → Z3 crossing): the gap is now 500 m and contains merge 2, not the zipper. Appendix B is likewise v0 history.

**Spot-check of cited numbers done in this revision** (GH-10 asked for all of them): TII 2012 §7.1 and Table 5.2; ARCH STATUS.md:57, :69, :73; ARCH design_v0.md:43 (B14); ARCH lessons_metric_gameability.md §5; ARCH vsl_lab/netgen/mrg3.py:29, :54 and ARCH t2_realism_ld3.md; `truck_energy_check_v0.md` (the 40 t and COPERT rows); literature_v0.md F7 (lines 336–342) and line 757; PAG 6.11 Tables 6.11.13–14; the Yuan et al. 2015 poster text; `decisions.md` V1–V57; `interfaces.md` §1–§10. All other inline numbers carried over from the synthesis were not re-checked against their cited lines and should be before approval.

**Choices the reviser made that the findings left open** (each is either an [AUTHOR] recommendation or an ASSUMPTION listed in A24, so the author can reverse it):
- recommendations changed from the synthesis: A10 now recommends the decided release (a), not the ramp (b); A6 recommends the designated level (c); A27 recommends point-estimate guards (c); A13 recommends keeping CAVs at 100 km/h with the handicap stated (iii);
- G3 and C4 gate at λ_c and λ_c + 0.1 (both admissible by construction) rather than at λ_2, at a cost of 40 extra runs; G5 runs at λ_c and λ_2;
- the edge-level check moved from after stage 2 to after stage 1 (S9 allowed either order); the parameter refinement placed at the same point, before KILL-1;
- stage-1 advancement relaxed from 0 to ≤ 1 invalid run of 8; selection eligibility and band validity at ≤ 5 %;
- the delay guard uses one continuous formula with a denominator floor of 5 % of R_tot instead of a separate "B0 delay ≤ 0" branch;
- the hard-brake guard uses a 0.5 continuity correction instead of an exact conditional test with a minimum count;
- the KILL-1 threshold is calibrated by resampling the P1 samples (the S7 proposal) rather than by a studentised per-level t (the BDP-9 alternative);
- a provisional CPU-time cap of 8 × the smoke median during calibration, the final 4 × cap from seed 010;
- F's pooled objective scores an ineligible level as J = 0, with ties broken by the unpenalised mean;
- the zone-move rule keeps V56's freedom (no cap on moves) and adds only a cost row and the re-fix rule.

**Note (v2, 2026-10-13).** The log above describes v0, and the note above it v1. v2 changes the claim metric (V74), adds the incident regime (V75) and lane commands (V76), and folds in Addenda G1 and Z1; statements in this log about C as the headline, about P0-G1's drop ratio as a gate, or about V64 positions describe v0/v1. The findings of the two judges on the v2 amendment drafts, and their handling, are in Appendix D.3.

---

## Appendix D: v1 → v2 change log

*Written for v2 (2026-10-13). Every change from v1 is listed with the decision behind it and the draft it came from. "rigor" and "lean" are the two amendment drafts of the v2 workflow (`scratchpad/protocol_v2/amend_rigor.md`, `amend_lean.md`); "synthesis" marks a choice or fix made while merging them; "evidence" marks one of the four evidence packages. The earlier sentence "No change relaxes a v1 bound, except the drop ratio of P0-G1 and T1a" was incomplete (v2 review HC-6); the full list follows.*

**Relaxations relative to v1, in full.** V66 says that changes come "only by dated addenda that never relax a bound". Approving v2 is therefore an explicit, author-approved exception to that clause, made before any data; each item below is also an [AUTHOR] item or is listed in one:
1. P0-G1's drop ratio (b) and T1a's ρ_T1 become report-only under [AUTHOR A42] (b) (#11), which also needs a V69 override ([AUTHOR A66]);
2. P0-G1's onset split (a) becomes report-only under A42 (b) (§4.4);
3. v1 §9.2's "never used as a fix: driver error terms above the SUMO defaults" gains the CD3/CD4 exception under [AUTHOR A41] (v) (#12);
4. the KILL-1 thresholds move from one 1 % quantile to per-co-primary 0.5 % quantiles; the net direction vs v1 is not established (§6.5, #26);
5. T0c widens from {Dl, throughput} to any of {Dl, throughput, S, T}; family-wise control is kept (Bonferroni over four, α = 0.0125 each) (#22);
6. (v2 review) T0f(b)(i) becomes not applicable when lane 2's continuation is saturated (§5; [AUTHOR A67] (iii));
7. (v2 review) the stage-1 guard screen gains a noise allowance (§6.5); it is a screen, the selection and band guards are unchanged.
Every other change keeps or tightens a v1 bound.

### D.1 Base draft, scores and grafts
- **Judges.** Two judges scored both drafts on five criteria (consistency, falsifiability, metric validity, honesty, feasibility). Judge 1: rigor 7 / 9 / 8 / 8 / 6 = 38, lean 6 / 7 / 6 / 7.5 / 8.5 = 35. Judge 2: rigor 8 / 8.5 / 8.5 / 8 / 5.5 = 38.5, lean 6 / 7.5 / 5.5 / 7 / 8.5 = 34.5 (sums are my arithmetic). Both named **rigor** the winner; rigor is the base. Lean scored higher on feasibility, so its budget-saving and code-saving ideas were weighed against rigor's cost.
- **Grafted from lean:** the CD0–CD4 naming; the C0′ screen's three-level pooled estimator and the BN* = BN-LD condition; keeping V74's 1.5 s with a calibration fork (A37 (a)); principle 15 ("a regime cannot rescue a KILL") and ℓ* among no-incident levels; setting lane-change mode 1878 only at a CAV's first lane request, with the bit-identity test (T0e(k)); the neighbour-identity lane test with its mode-1621 negative control (T0e(b), (b′)); the congested lane-authority test at ℓ₂ as a pure lane contrast (T0f(b)); removing a non-binding lane actuator instead of switching to an urgent mode (A46 (a)); the obstacle as a deterministic manifest vehicle with the conservation and RNG-isolation tests (B4, B7); no rubbernecking contingency; lane 0 as the single incident lane; LM-B's simplified rule (two tuned parameters, keep in Z1, never right); the G5 treatment of T (reported, not gated); the KILL-1 per-metric 0.5 % quantiles (≤ 1 % union bound); the pooled-ratio T row; the "no addendum may move the incident" rule; the corrected probe range i = 1…72; the INC points entering the regime set at GO (V75).
- **Kept from rigor:** the metric definitions (high-passed S with W1 and fixed planned-km weights; T with a 150-m leader range, overlap records and floors), the informativeness gate G6 and F6, the CAV hard-brake gate plus (f2), the joint resampling and the exact FWER statement, the C0′ ladder with k_cal counting and the V-1 removal, ρ handled consistently in G1 and T1a, the incident model and cohort_I, LM-E with keyed draws and the precedence rule, the hazard message, the keep-only twins, the observation additions, the attribution rows, T0c and T1b on S and T, the A-item numbering A36–A54.

### D.3 Judge defects and how they were handled

| Draft | Defect (judges) | Handling in v2 |
|---|---|---|
| rigor | KILL-3 counted INC points: a GO could come from INC points alone, ℓ* would then be undefined, and a regime could rescue an empty no-incident band (both judges) | fixed: principle 15; KILL-1, KILL-2 and KILL-3 on no-incident levels only; GO needs a no-incident band level; ℓ* among no-incident band levels (§0.3, §6.5, §6.6, §6.9) |
| rigor | F's pooled objective averaged over INC points, coupling incident performance into the choice of the best fixed rule (judge 2) | fixed: J̄ over no-incident levels only; F at INC points uses the LM-E default (§6.6) |
| rigor | Rubbernecking contingency (HDV tau 1.3 then 1.6 s) violated principle 10 (judge 1) | fixed: no contingency; an out-of-band F drops the point; principle 10 states it (§2.4b, C10, A43 (viii)) |
| rigor | CD3 sigmaerror 0.1 and CD4 sigma 0.8 contradicted v1 §9.2 without a flag (judge 1) | fixed: §1.1 item 34, A41 (v), the §9.2 exception guarded by the C0′ artefact conditions and F3 |
| rigor | Operating characteristic multiplied P(point ≤ −0.10) by P(CI upper < 0) as if independent; the case for n = 80 overstated (both judges) | fixed: recomputed (0.75 / 0.80 / 0.83 at σ = 0.15); the single-co-primary probability is P(point ≤ −0.10) when z_c · SE < 0.10; A49 recommendation reversed to (a) (§6.7, §6.8, A49) |
| rigor | "S_raw mostly rewards spreading the slowdown upstream" contradicted by the draft's own harm70 row (judge 1) | fixed: removed; only the variance-share argument is kept, and A36 states the harm70 numbers (§3.1, A36) |
| rigor | Principle 12 (no claim on one co-primary) vs A54 (b) (both judges) | fixed: A54 (a) recommended; (b) labelled the weaker reading that would need a principle-12 amendment (§0.3, A54, §12.2) |

### D.4 Defects found during the synthesis (in neither judge's list)
- **C0′ screen computability (rigor):** ≥ 5 runs with and ≥ 5 without an onset, out of the 8 runs at λ_a, is impossible; fixed with lean's three-level pooling.
- **No passing setting under A42 (a):** neither draft said what happens; fixed with the V-2 screen and futility stop F7.
- **F's LM-E parameters at INC points** were undefined once F is chosen on no-incident levels; fixed with the LM-E default.
- **T_floor = 0** (likely at 1.5 s) makes "≥ 3 × T_floor" vacuous; the conflict-episode count closes it.
- **Vehicles with very few samples in W** near the cap would get σ̃ ≈ 0; the 150-sample rule closes it.
- **INC samples must not feed KILL-1** (principle 15), and **σ_plan,2 must cover the INC point** (the held-out n serves it); both stated.
- **V75's "pre-registered regime"** vs a band-only regime set: the INC points that passed GI join the regime set at GO whether or not they are in the band (§1.1 item 37).
- **Admissibility at n = 20** (GI) was not stated; added (0.52 at a true 8 % rate).
- **Rigor's 1622 range** (70–83 %) came from the 1621 rows; corrected in the stress table.

### D.5 Arithmetic re-checked for the synthesis (`scratchpad/protocol_v2/synth/desk_synth.py`, python3; my arithmetic)
The rigor-toy summary (S_raw 7.84 and S_hp300 5.44 m/s in the heavy queue; high-pass shares 0.48 / 0.26; 70 km/h limit −6.6 % / −10.1 %; 50 km/h crawl −32.1 % at +62.1 % travel time; S/v̄ +10.2 % / +3.8 %; TET at 1.5 s at speed 0.041 / 0.052 / 0.003 and at 3.0 s 1.70 → 39.5 s per 1,000 veh-km); Bonferroni z for m = 4, 16, 18, 20; the σ thresholds of §6.7 under A39 (a) and (b); the operating characteristics of §6.8 and the σ = 0.15 rows; the KILL-1 normal references; the T1b MDE; the binomials of GI, C10, admissibility and integrity; the incident desk inflow 3,700λ and the obstacle approach time (24.7 s); the CPU totals and the A44 alternatives; the incident toy's block-start offsets (from `inc_toy/runs/*/result.json`); the lane stress rows (from `toy/out/t2_stress.jsonl`); the pilot's cost anchor (Duration 146.92 s, UPS 329,648). The v1 numbers carried over unchanged were not re-checked in this revision.

Each finding was verified against the text and, where possible, recomputed by a verifier; "Severity" shows the finder's rating → the verified rating. Findings the verifier marked as needing the author became [AUTHOR] items A55–A68 (§11.4) or new options in A38, A41 and A44, with the recommendation written into the primary design; nothing is decided silently. Appendix D describes the pre-review v2 draft: where its rows conflict with the body (the 300-s boxcar, t_b for the block start, the earlier wording rule, the pooled C0′ estimator), the body and this appendix govern.*

**Choices the reviser made that the findings left open** (each is an [AUTHOR] recommendation or a labelled ASSUMPTION in A24, so the author can reverse it): the 95th-percentile S imputation constant (ST7 suggested it; MV-8's max form is a reported row); T imputation as the primary form, not only a row; the Gaussian σ_g = 100 s chosen to match the boxcar's half-power period; S_raw ≤ 0 (not ≤ −0.05) as the co-confirmation; T0f(b)'s ≥ 10 congested minutes, ≥ 15 seeds and the ℓ₃ fallback; the 0.29 stop threshold (hopelessness at a true 15 % gain) instead of the finding's 0.193; keeping the INC point in m while removing it from σ_plan,2; the C0′ G3-computability floor checked on plateau runs (the finding put it at G3 time); the obstacle removed at 13,200 m rather than at the block end.

**Arithmetic and toy re-checks in this revision:** `scratchpad/protocol_v2/review_fix/desk_review.py` (filter responses, σ_g, the allowance probabilities, the 0.29 threshold, sign-test counts, Monte Carlo tails, the harm-leg probabilities, R-dependent z values, the loaded cost) and `review_fix/s_kernel_toy.py` with 15 toy runs on non-project seeds 1–3 (Appendix A). The numbers quoted from the review lenses and not re-run here are marked as such in the text.

### Amendment 2026-10-13 (approval, V66)
- **A7 / V61 reading (a):** the candidate passes the B1 criterion when its paired median gain vs B1 is ≥ 5 % **and** the 95 % CI of the gain excludes 0 (as written in §6.8 (d)).

### Addenda G1 and Z1 (2026-10-13, before any project seed): folded into the body in v2
- **Addendum G1 (geometry, V68)** lengthened the plain sections: merge 2 at 9,000–9,250 m, the lane drop at 15,000 m, the two-lane exit to 18,250 m; measurement sections at 3,400 / 9,400 / 15,100 m; the metronome probe at 15,100 m; bottleneck groups around 3,250 / 9,250 / 15,000 m.
